# Contracts (locked in GRO-1961)

Source of truth for types: [`shared/types.ts`](../shared/types.ts). This file is the prose companion.

## Repo layout / scripts

```
package.json          npm workspaces: client, desktop. Root scripts:
                        dev            electron-vite dev (cwd desktop/): builds main + preload, serves the renderer with HMR, launches Electron
                        build          electron-vite build → desktop/out/{main,preload,renderer}
                        desktop:build  build, then electron-builder --mac → desktop/dist-app (.app + dmg, F1 — see "Packaging"); the root version is injected via extraMetadata
                        typecheck      tsc -p client && tsc -p shared && tsc -p desktop
                        test           vitest run  (FOUR projects: client=jsdom, desktop=node, tools=node, perf=jsdom — client/vitest.perf.config.ts, the budget tripwires kept off the main run's contended pool)
                        e2e            build, then playwright test --config desktop/e2e/playwright.config.ts (G1 — Playwright-Electron smoke suite)
client/               The renderer: React 19 + TS, @milkdown/crepe 7.22.x, plus ONE lazily-loaded dependency: `@excalidraw/excalidraw` PINNED to 0.18.1 (YAZ-878 — the module is imported only by `drawings/renderScene.ts`, and only through a dynamic `import()` on the first drawing preview or modal open, so it never enters the main chunk; its stylesheet is a second dynamic import, made by the modal's surface alone, YAZ-879). No scripts of its own — electron-vite (desktop/electron.vite.config.ts) builds it with ./index.html as the entry, and its renderer config `dedupe`s `react`/`react-dom` — 🔒 load-bearing since YAZ-879: npm hoists an OLDER react beside the Excalidraw tree at the repo root, and without the dedupe `<Excalidraw>` mounts against a second React and dies on a null dispatcher. Every entry below lives under client/src/:
  App.tsx                 composes this window's root + `useWorkspace` identity, window-local `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280), Sidebar, retained main editor layers, and the full-editor RightPanel; global settings/width still follow `storage.subscribe`, while the lens (window identity since YAZ-1628) and both panel visibility states persist independently per window. Owns shared watch/index/properties/sync feeds, menu/link/file lifecycle listeners, renderer ⌘B, the ⌘K uncollapse/focus handshake, Home adoption, rename/delete carry-before-remap ordering, external-rename notice, title/hash, theme, and responsive split-vs-overlay derivation; a null root renders Welcome with no auto-dialog.
  Welcome.tsx             the Welcome screen (C2, GRO-2164): app name, recents as one-click rows (dead folder → "Folder not found" + MRU drop), Open folder… (+ test)
  api.ts                  `api` = the fs half of window.yaseenDocs with bridge rejections wrapped in BridgeRequestError (+ test)
  bridge.d.ts             `window.yaseenDocs: YaseenDocsApi` (installed by desktop/src/preload)
  editor/                 Editor dispatches by the shared file kind BEFORE mounting an owner: Markdown gets the Crepe host + conflict bar (the scroller holds the page title, properties, Crepe, optional folder-page contents, the comment stream — YAZ-1472, see "Comments" — and "Linked mentions"), while view-only kinds delegate to `viewers/` and never mount Crepe, autosave, frontmatter, folder-page or backlinks owners. createCrepe (locked factory, see "Editor rules"), featureConfig (Crepe feature allowlist + guard test), drawingMenu (the Drawing slash item on Crepe's own BlockEdit menu, YAZ-877), drawing/drawingPreview + drawingPreview.css (decoration-only `![[x.excalidraw]]` previews, see rule 27; the editing MODAL a preview click opens is React beside the editor, `src/drawings/`, rule 28), image/ (YAZ-1656, rule 30: imageSrc — the `app://vault` URL builder + Obsidian's `alt|width` parse/format; imageView + .css — THE ONE node view, on the commonmark `image` node; insertImage — paste/drop bytes → `assets/images/`; imageOptions — the ctx slice that carries the host's options to the paste handler; ImageModal + .css — the lightbox), listItemRoundTrip (empty-item round trip), SaveIndicator — this list used to name a `frontmatter` module too; there has never been one here, only `frontmatter.test.ts`, which covers `shared/frontmatter.ts` (rule 1's split/re-prepend) from the surface that consumes it — TOMBSTONE: baseEmbed/ and baseCodeBlock/ (the `![[X.base]]` decoration plugin and the ```base node view) were deleted with the format in YAZ-844, see rules 20/21
  editor/marks/           underline (mark: Mod-u ↔ `<u>…</u>` inline HTML, $remark + $markSchema + $shortcut) (+ test), highlight (mark with an optional `color`, see rule 31) (+ test), htmlPairs (shared inline-HTML pair wrapper for underline + coloured highlight), markTestKit (the shared mark test harness)
  editor/outline/         outlineFolding ($prose plugin: collapsible parent bullets) + outlineFoldKeys + outlineFolding.css, listCommands (outliner keymap), hotkeys (Obsidian hotkeys), zoom + zoom.css (zoom into a bullet), guideLines + guideLines.css (click-to-fold guide lines), listNodes (shared helpers), bullets.css (depth glyphs) (+ tests)
  drawings/               createDrawing (YAZ-877): the empty-scene constant + the clock name + the never-overwrite create loop over `api.writeAsset`, the host half of the editor's Drawing slash item; drawingScene (YAZ-878: `isDrawingTarget` + the base64 → UTF-8 → JSON read over `api.readAsset`, throwing for everything that is not a scene — plus `openDrawing`, YAZ-879's load, which keeps the resolved path and mtime a save needs), renderScene (the ONE lazy `@excalidraw/excalidraw` module import + `exportToSvg`, and where `window.EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH` / `EXCALIDRAW_EXPORT_SOURCE` are pinned before it), drawingFeed (the subscribe/poke refresh source), and the modal (YAZ-879, rule 28): DrawingModal + drawingModal.css (the chrome — overlay, Save, dirty, discard strip, error line), ExcalidrawSurface (🔒 THE ENGINE SEAM: the only `<Excalidraw>` mount and the only serializer), saveDrawing (the write + the canvas-wins CONFLICT retry) (+ tests)
  editor/wikilink/        wikilinkPlugin + wikilink.css (decoration-only [[wikilink]] rendering, see rule 23), wikilinkClick + createFromLink (Links C click navigation + create-on-click, rule 23), wikilinkPicker + wikilinkPicker.css (the `[[` completion popup + its keymap, see rule 24), WikilinkIndexBridge (the one Markdown index feed plus the separate tree-catalog navigation feed; root changes retire old view-only rows before either new feed can compose) (+ tests)
  hooks/                  useFile (Markdown + strict view-only text load; migration/frontmatter ownership remains Markdown-only), useViewOnlyCatalog (structural-watch Files-tree catalog), useAutosave (debounce/flush/conflict; frontmatter-only external changes absorb silently, GRO-2186), useWatch (one bridge watch() per root, fan-out), usePickFolder (native dialog)
  viewers/                TextViewer (selectable monospaced read-only text with two-axis overflow), PdfViewer (revoked Blob URL around Chromium's passive native PDF frame), and ImageViewer (exact binary bytes decoded once and painted as one static canvas frame) (+ tests)
  lib/                    pure logic with unit tests: autosave state machine, storage (bridge-backed in-memory cache of the main-owned app state + window identity; init() before the first render, subscribe() for live changes), treeState, paths, windowTitle, theme (resolve the Appearance setting to light/dark, K), urlHash (the #/abs/path.md mirror, rule 17), renameContinuity (Links E1 GRO-2194 / E1b GRO-2241: the per-path autosave handle map + dirty-buffer carry across a rename — per affected open path for a folder rename — see "Links": Rename; ALSO `retireDeletedPath`/`retireDeletedDir` for GRO-2272, which retire ONLY — a delete has no destination, so capturing or stashing a buffer would be a resurrection vector), dragSlot (YAZ-1471: `insertionSlot` / `dropIndex`, the insertion-slot arithmetic BOTH horizontal tab strips reorder by — the window's tab bar and a folder page's view tabs — one spelling so the two cannot drift apart)
  links/                  completion.ts — the ONE [[…]] completion matcher (Links B, GRO-2191): trailingLinkFragment / matchLinkCandidates (ends-trimmed, case-insensitive over the candidate name, ranked exact → prefix → substring, cap 8 applied after ranking — GRO-2197; matchLinkNames is the same match over plain names) / linkCandidates (LinkCandidate { name, insert, label } rows from index records: shortest unambiguous names + one piped alias row per frontmatter alias, Links E2 GRO-2214), shared by EditableCell's link editors and the editor's [[ picker (rule 24); renameLinks.ts — the automatic link updates after an in-app rename/move (Links E1 GRO-2194, folders + cross-dir moves E1b GRO-2241: maskCode / rewriteBodyLinks / rewriteNoteLinks / updateLinksAfterRename + countLinkReferences, the E1c dry-run referencing-set count — Markdown sources/targets use index records, while explicit view-only targets use the separately pinned catalog described under "Links": Rename); renameDetector.ts — the E1c external-rename hypothesis detector (GRO-2242: detectRenames with the locked guard rails, diffRecords for the while-running snapshot feed, preRenameRecords to synthesise the engine's pre-rename view); useExternalRenames.ts — the E1c queue/banner hook (both feeds → confirm-first banner state, session dismissed set, in-app-rename suppression, the Update repair+rewrite flow — see "Links": Rename → External renames); backlinks.ts — the Links D referencing set + context snippets (GRO-2193: `backlinksFor` over an index snapshot through the shared resolver, memoized per (records, path); `mentionSnippets` over one note's raw content, code-masked, for the expanded rows — one snippet per LINE, rendered as display text through `linkDisplayText` with every mention on it highlighted, GRO-2197), BacklinksSection.tsx + backlinks.css — the collapsible "Linked mentions (N)" block at the bottom of the note (see Editor rule 25); folderPages.ts — the folder-page lookup and THE guarded step (YAZ-825/826: `folderPagesLookup` over an index snapshot through the shared resolver, memoized per records identity, answering `pagesIn` / `folderPagesOf` / `uncategorized`; `isFolderPage`, the strict `folder_page: true` test; `guardedChildren`, the ancestor-path-guarded step EVERY folder-page descent goes through — see "Links": Folder pages) (+ tests)
  comments/               the comment stream (YAZ-1472, see "Comments"): CommentsSection.tsx + comments.css (the SIXTH block of the Markdown host's scroller — the properties-panel header, one card per thread, bullet-style folds, the one composer in its three seats; a VIEW over CrepeHost's `disk`, no watcher and no read of its own), markdown.ts (`commentHtml` — the ONE Markdown→HTML door in the app, `marked` gfm+breaks → DOMPurify — and `commentSummary`, the folded row's first line); the model is `shared/comments.ts`, whose contract test sits here as comments.test.ts so vitest's client project collects it (+ tests)
  sidebar/                Sidebar, Tree, TopicsTree (the Topics lens' folder-page tree, YAZ-848) and ensureHome (Home's identity/adoption decision + the once-per-root App hook, YAZ-849) — the last two are "The Topics sidebar", which also owns the lens row below. The sidebar chrome is TWO rows since YAZ-847: ROW 1 the lens tablist (Topics ⇄ Files, Topics the default, the per-window `WindowEntry.sidebarLens` since YAZ-1628) — carrying, right-aligned at its end, the ⚡ YAZ-862 expand/collapse-all button: ONE double-chevron acting on whichever lens is ACTIVE (both of them since ⚡ YAZ-873), replacing that lens' whole expanded set in a single write — on FILES a `setAll` dispatch over every dir at every depth (`allDirs`), on TOPICS a replacement of the lifted `topicsExpanded` set with `allExpandableTopics` (the tree's OWN guarded walk from every root through `guardedChildren`, ⚡ D6 — so it opens exactly the rows that have a chevron, counts a diamond page once and stops at a loop — capped at `MAX_TOPICS_EXPANDED_PAGES`, the bucket it is written into) — so each is written through the same bucket as every other expansion in its lens (a SESSION bucket since YAZ-1642: shared by every window on the vault, never restored by a launch); it collapses that lens to `[]` whenever ANY of the CURRENT tree's foldable rows is open — measured against the tree, since the persisted list can still name paths an external change took away — and expands only when none is; the two lenses keep their OWN stores (one button, never one set), and it is GONE rather than disabled while a query is typed and whenever the active reading has nothing to unfold: a vault with no folders, a Topics tree of leaves, or the empty snapshot before the first index lands — and ROW 2 the persistent search bar (YAZ-801) — ALWAYS visible, on BOTH lenses, loading/error/empty vault included (a bar that came and went with the tree would be a view, which is the rescinded design), Esc clears a typed query and only the SECOND press gives up focus, ⌘K focuses it (the App handshake above); while `query` is non-empty the body shows a FLAT ranked results list INSTEAD of the tree (🔒 flat-list ruling, YAZ-739) — and that list also carries the loaded tree's FOLDERS as rows (🔒 D1–D2, YAZ-1491 — fed from the Sidebar's own `dirs` memo, `folderCandidates` spliced ahead of `searchCandidates` so a folder sits first within a rank bucket; a note still never matches on its folder name, the folder itself is one row), choosing a folder row REVEALS it in the Files lens (🔒 D3 — `onRevealInFiles`: App flips the lens and issues the same reveal request the tab menu uses, and the Files reveal accepts a dir, which opens ITSELF too via the create menu's synthetic-child `expandTo`; one `activate` rule serves Enter, ⌘⏎ and click alike), and folder rows carry a glyph and a `, folder` aria-label suffix (🔒 D4), replacing the ACTIVE lens tab's body while the tabs row stays live and a lens switch mid-search keeps the query (🔒 D5, YAZ-847) — a conditional render, never a teardown, so every bit of tree state (data, expansion, pending create/rename, drag) is waiting untouched when it clears — fed by `client/src/search/` (ranking engine YAZ-802, `useSearchResults` + `SearchResults` YAZ-803; the hook's index feed is LAZY since YAZ-808: no `index(root)` read and no watch subscription until the first non-empty query, latched on for good after it, because the always-on per-window index feed is WikilinkIndexBridge's) and driven entirely from the input, which never loses focus: ↑/↓ clamp at both ends and never wrap (the `[[` picker's rule) and clamp against the RESULT COUNT too, so an index refresh that shrinks the list under the selection highlights the last row rather than nothing (YAZ-808); on a NOTE row ⏎ opens it in place, ⌘⏎ in a background tab, a row click opens and ⌘-click background-opens (I3) — the list stays up either way — and every one of those gestures on a FOLDER row is the reveal above; the blank-space context menu is the TREE's, so right-clicking the results offers nothing ("New note" there would have no target) — and the Topics lens' blank BODY offers nothing for the same reason (YAZ-847, "The Topics sidebar") — its page ROWS, however, open this very menu since YAZ-865's ⚡ amendment, through the same `openMenu`/`MenuTargets` pair, so there is one context menu in this sidebar and not two; ⌘-click on a file row opens it in a BACKGROUND TAB of this window (the locked I3 ruling, GRO-2235); the file- and folder-row context menus carry New note/folder page/folder (YAZ-841 — "New folder page" is the create group's SECOND item, a note born carrying `folder_page: true`; on a FLAGGED folder-page row in the TOPICS lens that group births MEMBERS of that topic instead of files beside it — ⚡ 8H, YAZ-869, see "The Topics sidebar": The row menu; TOMBSTONE: "New base" and its `base` `EntryKind` were deleted with the format in YAZ-844, and the tree's per-row `FolderPageGlyph` went with them), the folder-page TOGGLE ("Turn into folder page" / "Turn back into normal page", YAZ-840 — one state-aware row on MARKDOWN FILE rows only, between the create group and Rename; the reverse opens `ConfirmTurnBack`, the lossless twin of the delete sheet — both under "The folder-page gestures"), Rename (files Links E1 GRO-2194, folders E1b GRO-2241 — inline input replacing the row label, prefilled name minus extension for files / raw name for folders, Enter commits / Esc-blur cancels; no hotkey v1 — F2 is a polish note), Copy path, Open in new window (D2, GRO-2168 — context menu only since I3) — TOMBSTONE: the type-conditional "New ▸ <type>" submenu and "New type…" (`NewTypeDialog`) were deleted with the type system in YAZ-836; birth-from-a-declaration now belongs to the folder page ("Folder page settings": Birth from a folder page, `newPageFromFolderPage`); Delete (GRO-2272 — opens ConfirmDelete, never deletes directly) and Reveal in Finder (GRO-2274 — file rows, folder rows AND blank space, where it targets the vault ROOT); menu ORDER is VS Code's Explorer grouping (GRO-2272 `C1a-`): utilities, then the create actions, then the folder-page toggle, then Rename and Delete LAST — destructive-last is a safety property, pinned by a test in Sidebar.test.tsx; blank space also carries Copy path targeting the vault root (GRO-2273); each menu item resolves its OWN target through `MenuTargets` — no item derives visibility from another's value (GRO-2296); ConfirmDelete is the in-app delete sheet (never a native dialog; focus starts on Cancel, `deleteConfirmMessage` owns the copy, counts come from the loaded tree + vault index so the delete path makes no extra fetch); menu and submenu clamp to the viewport edges (GRO-2204); RenameInline is the CreateInline-idiom input for Rename; dragging a FILE row onto a folder row (or the root header) MOVES it there (E1b — the TabBar HTML5-drag idiom, dropDir highlight, same rename pipeline; no multi-select, no folder dragging — Future)
  workspace/              useWorkspace (the ONE renderer owner for main tabs + right-panel pages, retained mounts, both session histories, lifecycle repair, strict transfers, and one complete identity mirror) + the private pageDrag MIME codec (YAZ-966) (+ tests)
  tabs/                   TabBar + tabs.css: main tab semantics, Back/Forward, context actions, local reorder (on `lib/dragSlot` since YAZ-1471) and validated right→main insertion slots, the strip scrolling with no scrollbar through `app.css`'s shared `.scroll-strip`; durable ownership lives in workspace/, not here (+ tests)
  right-panel/            RightPanel + right-panel.css: presentational toolbar/header stack, resize/hide/overlay behavior, retained viewer layers, context action, and validated main/right insertion slots (+ tests)
  test-setup.ts           jsdom stubs (observers, Range rects)

TOMBSTONE: BaseHost, the main-pane host for `.base`, died with the format), FolderPageContents + folderPageContents.css (YAZ-819/820: the contents block — the ONLY mount of ViewsPane left, see "The contents block"), ViewsPane (4A view chrome; 4B table for `type: table`, 4D board, 4E cards, 4F list, the folder page's outline for `type: outline`, placeholder row list for unknown view types, see "View chrome"), folderPageSettings (YAZ-830: the one door to `folder_page_settings` — reader, [D5] ordering, view-scoped typing, the whole-key writer; see "Folder page settings"), outlineDoc (YAZ-900: the outline document's grammar — parse / serialise / the link-line rule / the `order` migration / the rename walk), outlineSync (YAZ-902: what two outline documents say about BELONGING, and the `folder_pages` writes that make it true), useIndex (2C: the vault index over the bridge, watch-driven refetch), useProperties + propertiesStub (YAZ-835: the vault-wide property declarations over the bridge, live via `properties:changed`, App-owned since YAZ-846, plus the in-memory test double that implements the same `PropertiesApi` — see "Properties"), testRecords + testFolderPage (the shared client-test fixtures — the 8-note index snapshot, and the `FolderPageMode` bundle every `ViewsPane` mount needs; they are two files because the main-process index test deep-equals the first one and desktop's tsconfig has no `jsx`), writeProperty (5B write path, GRO-2141), editorType (5B editor inference, GRO-2142), newNote (5D seed derivation + create, GRO-2144), scaffold (YAZ-832: `scaffoldFromFolderPage` / `folderPageTemplatePath` / `newPageFromFolderPage` / `memberFolder` / `ensureFolder` — the folder-page half is all that is left, see "Folder page settings"; TOMBSTONE: the type scaffold, per-type templates, starter bases and `createType` died with the type system in YAZ-836), view/ (toolbar, menus, keys, TableView, BoardView, CardsView, ListView, OutlineView + OutlineEditor + ConfirmRemoveMember (YAZ-820/903, the outline skin — folder-page only: the bullets-only Milkdown instance, the members it does not name ADOPTED into it (YAZ-1152), and the remove-from-folder-page sheet; TOMBSTONE: OutlineAddRow, the picker-only "+ Link a page…" row, died with rows-are-pages in YAZ-903, and the APPENDED SECTION under the editor — the read-only row per unnamed member, with its link, glyph, direct-member count and hover × — died with adoption in YAZ-1152), EditableCell, groupDrag, cardWidth, GroupHeader, icons incl. the folder page's `FolderPageGlyph`), views.css (+ tests)
desktop/              Electron 43 shell (electron-vite 5, electron-builder): main + preload; the renderer is ../client. package.json's `build` block is the electron-builder config (F1); build/ holds icon.icns; dist-app/ is the packaged output (gitignored)
  electron.vite.config.ts     the three builds (main, preload, renderer rooted at ../client); @shared alias for all of them
  src/channels.ts             IPC channel names (CH) + the Envelope every ipcMain.handle answers with
  src/preload/index.ts        installs window.yaseenDocs over contextBridge; unwraps envelopes into BridgeError rejections (+ bridge.test.ts: contract completeness)
  src/main/index.ts           bootstrap: app:// protocol serving out/renderer (host `yaseen`) and vault images (host `vault` → `vaultProtocol.ts`, YAZ-1658), single-instance lock, yaseendocs:// claim + open-url/open-file/second-instance routing (E1/E2), the application-menu apply layer (B3), the store (userData/yaseendocs.json), registerIpc(store), the window manager's restoreAll(), store.flush() on quit
  src/main/vaultProtocol.ts   `app://vault/<root>/<ref>?from=<note dir>` — vault images straight to `<img src>` (YAZ-1658): parseVaultUrl / resolveVaultUrl / serveVaultImage (the fetch is injected, so it tests without Electron) + revealVaultImage (the context menu's Reveal in Finder, YAZ-1666); resolution is `fs/assets.ts`'s `resolveAsset`, images only, 404 for a miss, an escape or a non-image, `Cache-Control: no-cache` so a file swapped in Finder refreshes (+ tests)
  src/main/store.ts           createStore(filePath): the yaseendocs.json store — sync load with field-by-field validation (corrupt → .corrupt-<epoch> + defaults), targeted mutators, onChange, debounced atomic write, flush() (+ test)
  src/main/windows.ts         the window manager (GRO-2160): the webContents.id → window id lookup, restoreAll (bounds clamped), openWindow / duplicateWindow, deep-link routing (resolveLinkTarget, E1), the close/quit flush handshake (+ test; see "Multi-window")
  src/main/menu.ts            buildMenuTemplate / createMenuHandlers (B3, GRO-2161): the menu bar as a pure template with stable item ids, Open Recent from AppState.recents (+ test; see "Menus & shortcuts")
  src/main/theme.ts           Appearance in main (K, GRO-2218): WINDOW_BG / windowBackgroundColor (no-FOUC window backing) + subscribeNativeTheme mirroring settings.theme into nativeTheme.themeSource (+ test)
  src/main/vaultConfig.ts     vault-local config in <root>/.yaseendocs/ (J, GRO-2188): readConfig / writeConfig / subscribeConfig — one dotfolder chokidar per open-vault root (+ test)
  src/main/linkQueue.ts       cold-start deep links (E1): open-url / open-file fire before `ready`, so URLs queue and replay once restoreAll() ran (+ test)
  src/main/ipc/               envelope (handle / handleWithEvent + BridgeError mapping), broadcast (send-to-every-live-window loop), fs (tree/read/readPdf/readImage/write/create + index/cold-diff + rename/repair-rename + delete + shell-reveal + shell-open-vscode + shell-open-link), dialog (pick-folder), watch (subscriptions per sender), state (AppState mutators + state:changed broadcast to every window), window (identity / set-identity / open / duplicate + the app:flushed ack), vaultConfig (read/write + vaultConfig:changed broadcast, one subscription per open-vault root), github (status / sync-now / set-enabled + github:status-changed broadcast; builds the production GitSyncHost and drives it off the same open-vault roots, YAZ-1081) (+ tests)
  src/main/git/               GitHub sync (YAZ-1081), Electron-free end to end: exec (resolveGit + the no-shell `git` runner, non-zero never throws), detect (repo facts: isRepo / origin URL / branch / dirty files), sync (ONE pass — commit, fetch, rebase, push; a conflicting rebase is ABORTED, never left half-applied, and every failure is classified offline/auth/other), manager (createGitSync(host): the per-root cadence — adoption pull, 30s quiet-period debounce, one 2-min offline retry, focus/wake pulls behind a cooldown — with one pass at a time per root and one coalesced follow-up, plus flushForQuit) (+ tests, incl. guarantees.test.ts)
  src/main/fs/                fsUtils (BridgeFailure, path/dir guards, buildTree, atomicWrite, supported/readable/Markdown-only capability guards), boundedRead (bounded regular-file snapshot reader), file (Markdown + strict view-only text reads; Markdown-only writes), pdf (dedicated binary PDF read), image (dedicated exact-path raster-image binary read), create, rename (encoding-safe file rename/move + folder rename, Links E1 GRO-2194 / E1b GRO-2241; repairRename validates an ALREADY-happened external rename, E1c GRO-2242), remove (in-app delete to the SYSTEM Trash via shell.trashItem — never fs.rm, no permanent-delete fallback, GRO-2272), openInVsCode (YAZ-963: the vscode://file deep link, reveal's read-only twin), openLink (YAZ-1309: allowlisted URL protocols + note-relative local files through the OS), reveal (Reveal in Finder via shell.showItemInFolder, stat-first so a stale row reports NOT_FOUND, GRO-2274), assets (the separate fuzzy/base64 readAsset pipe for cards and `.excalidraw` drawing sidecars, plus `resolveAsset` — the ONE resolution rule reads and the `app://vault` protocol share, YAZ-1658; writeAsset: drawings as text YAZ-876, images as bytes YAZ-1661), watchers (one shared chokidar per root) (+ tests)
  src/main/fs/testFixture.ts  makeFixture(): temp vault (markdown, plus non-vault files that must stay invisible) for tests; failure(), until()
  src/main/fs/viewsFixture.ts  makeViewsFixture(): temp vault mirroring a real Obsidian vault (Content Pillars/, 8 notes, pngs, .trash, and the two dotfolders that must stay invisible — `.obsidian`, EMPTY since ⚡ YAZ-815 deleted the `types.json` it carried, and `.yaseendocs`) — shared by every folder-page-view and index test (+ viewsFixture.test.ts). Named basesFixture/makeBasesFixture until YAZ-861; both fixtures lost their `.base` files with the format (YAZ-844)
  src/main/vaultIndex/        the Bases property index (GRO-2128/2129): scan (scanFile + pure extractAliases/Tags/Links/Embeds), live (getIndex: cache + shared watcher, 10-min idle eviction, getColdStartDiff), cache (persistent per-vault index cache under userData — GRO-2223: initIndexCache/loadIndexCache/schedulePersist/flushIndexCache), reconcile (cold-start cache-vs-disk stat sweep + ColdStartDiff) (+ tests; see "Property index")

shared/types.ts       shared TS types (alias @shared/* in every tsconfig and build)
shared/fileKind.ts    fileKind(name): 'markdown' | 'text' | 'pdf' | 'image' | null — the one extension-based, case-insensitive capability classifier used by main-process guards and the client
shared/frontmatter.ts splitFrontmatter / frontmatterInterior (fences off, bytes otherwise untouched — the ONE place the fence regexes are applied) / parseFrontmatter / setFrontmatterProperty / replaceFrontmatter (the whole interior, VERBATIM — the properties panel's RAW-mode write, ⚡ YAZ-883; its typed rows write one key through `setFrontmatterProperty` like everything else, ⚡ YAZ-884) / buildFrontmatter (whole block from scratch — 5D seeds + the YAZ-832 folder-page scaffolds; null prints `key:`) + FrontmatterWriteError — shared by the client's split/re-prepend, the index scan, writeProperty (5B) and the properties panel
shared/links.ts       fileLink / parseFileLink — the ONE yaseendocs:// encoding (E1), shared by main's parser and its Finder Open With re-encode (E2)
shared/comments.ts    `COMMENTS_KEY` (the ONE spelling of the reserved key) + the comment model (YAZ-1472, see "Comments"): readComments / commentsShape / threadsOf / addComment / editComment / deleteComment / newCommentId / nowIso + CommentsShapeError — pure, React-free, fs-free; the block writes through it, the index scan drops the key by it, the properties panel reserves the key by it
tools/migrateFolderPages.mjs  THE one-shot `page_type` → folder-page migration (YAZ-853; see "The migration" below) + migrateFolderPages.test.mjs (41 tests, its own vitest project — tools/vitest.config.ts)
docs/CONTRACTS.md     this file
```

Import from shared: `import type { TreeResponse } from '@shared/types'`.

## Supported file capabilities (YAZ-1289, locked)

`shared/fileKind.ts` is the one case-insensitive classifier. The final filename suffix decides the kind; extensionless names, leading-dot-only names such as `.json`, compound names ending in an unknown suffix such as `data.json.gz`, and every unlisted extension are unsupported. Existing dot-entry and `node_modules` exclusions apply before classification.

| Kind | Extensions | Files view / open | Read | Write / create | Semantic index | Markdown link |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `markdown` | `.md`, `.markdown` | Visible; editable Markdown editor | `readFile`, 10 MiB maximum; preserves the existing Node UTF-8 replacement-character and leading-BOM behavior | Yes: create, edit, autosave, frontmatter and Markdown-only feature writes | Yes: the only `IndexRecord` kind and therefore the only content-search, Topics, Home, folder-page, properties, views, aliases and backlinks source | Semantic wikilinks; existing missing-note create-on-click behavior applies |
| `text` | `.txt`, `.log`, `.csv`, `.tsv`, `.json`, `.jsonc`, `.jsonl`, `.ndjson`, `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.toml`, `.ini`, `.cfg`, `.conf`, `.xml`, `.env`, `.properties`, `.lock`, `.js`, `.jsx`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.py`, `.rb`, `.go`, `.rs`, `.java`, `.kt`, `.kts`, `.c`, `.h`, `.cc`, `.cpp`, `.hpp`, `.cs`, `.swift`, `.php`, `.sh`, `.bash`, `.zsh`, `.fish`, `.ps1`, `.sql`, `.html`, `.htm`, `.css`, `.scss`, `.sass`, `.less`, `.vue`, `.svelte`, `.graphql`, `.gql`, `.mdx`, `.rst`, `.tex` | Visible; selectable, copyable, read-only monospaced viewer with exact whitespace and two-axis overflow | `readFile`, 10 MiB maximum; fatal UTF-8 decode, UTF-8 BOM preserved, any malformed UTF-8 or NUL rejected passively | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; no embeds, headings/blocks, aliases, create-on-click or semantic record |
| `pdf` | `.pdf` | Visible; Chromium's native PDF viewer in a read-only frame | Dedicated `readPdf` returns `Uint8Array`, 50 MiB maximum; never text, base64 or the asset pipe | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; the same no-semantic/no-create rules as text |
| `image` | `.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.gif`, `.webp`, `.avif`, `.bmp` | Visible with its full filename; centered, read-only static canvas viewer | Dedicated exact-path `readImage` returns `Uint8Array` + allowlisted MIME, 50 MiB maximum; never base64, fuzzy lookup or the asset pipe | Never | Never | Navigation-only, explicit extension required; the same no-semantic/no-create rules as text/PDF |
| unsupported | every other suffix, including `.svg` | Hidden from Files and refused by supported-file deep links | Never through `readFile`, `readPdf` or `readImage` | Never through the note mutation boundaries | Never | None |

Every supported text/PDF/image target can use the normal tab, background-tab, restore, reveal, delete, open-in-new-window and open-in-VS-Code paths. Rename/move and external-rename repair must preserve what the existing bytes actually encode: Markdown and text retain their same-`FileKind` behavior, PDF stays `.pdf`, images keep the same extension case-insensitively except that `.jpg` and `.jpeg` are equivalent spellings. A rename never transcodes, so `.png` → `.jpg` and every other cross-encoding transition fail before disk mutation. Markdown remains the only creatable or writable kind.

Text, PDF and image transport uses one bounded regular-file snapshot: pre-read `fstat`, reject an oversized or non-regular file before allocation, allocate/read at most the initial size plus one byte (never more than the applicable cap plus one), then post-read `fstat`. A size/mtime drift or a byte count inconsistent with the snapshot fails `IO_ERROR` rather than returning mismatched bytes and metadata. Text above 10 MiB and PDF/image above 50 MiB fail `TOO_LARGE`.

PDF rendering uses an `application/pdf` Blob URL over the dedicated bytes. The viewer revokes the owned URL exactly once on replacement, path change and unmount. Electron enables Chromium PDF plugins while retaining `contextIsolation: true`, `sandbox: true`, and `nodeIntegration: false`; there is no PDF.js, annotation/editor layer, custom pagination, base64 conversion or asset-pipe reuse.

Raster rendering gives the dedicated bytes and allowlisted MIME to `createImageBitmap` once, sizes a canvas to the decoded dimensions, paints only the default frame, and immediately closes the bitmap exactly once. Animated GIF/WebP therefore display their static default/first frame; no timer or animation loop exists. Same-path external refresh keeps the last good canvas until replacement paints, while a path change hides old pixels immediately and stale decode completions can never paint. Decode/bridge failures remain passive. SVG is deliberately deferred: it is structured XML that can carry script and external references, so support needs an explicit sanitization and no-network policy rather than entry through this raster boundary.

View-only wikilinks are backed by a lightweight catalog flattened from the Files tree, never by `IndexRecord[]`. The catalog refreshes only for supported-file/directory structural watch events; content changes reload an already-open viewer without rebuilding semantic state. Resolution uses exact root-relative paths or case-insensitive basenames, with the shallowest path and then deterministic path order winning duplicates; autocomplete emits the shortest unambiguous spelling with the real extension. Recognized explicit-extension targets always take this navigation-only branch—even before the catalog is ready or when the target is missing—so they can never fall through to Markdown creation. Missing/removed targets and view-only heading/block suffixes remain unresolved and produce passive notices. `|display text` changes presentation only. View-only files produce no backlinks, properties, aliases, searchable content, Home/Topics/folder-page/view membership, semantic records or writes; rename-reference repair reads/writes only Markdown source notes using the pre-rename view-only catalog.

A root switch synchronously clears the old navigation resolver, catalog targets and merged picker rows while the new tree is pending. The semantic source object keeps its identity, but an old vault's catalog can never compose with the new vault's semantic snapshot.

## Bridge API (`window.yaseenDocs`, locked in GRO-2153 — Desktop A1)

The desktop app has no HTTP server (GRO-2095 D2): the renderer is sandboxed and calls the typed bridge the preload installs; `ipcMain.handle` answers from the main process (GRO-2157 deleted the server and every HTTP path). All paths are absolute POSIX paths; no jail — any absolute path is allowed. Every method rejects with a plain `BridgeError { code, message, path?, mtime? }` (`BridgeErrorCode` in `shared/types.ts`, plus `CONFLICT` which carries the disk mtime); the client's `api.ts` wraps it in `BridgeRequestError` (same fields) so the renderer can `instanceof` it.

| Bridge | Notes |
|---|---|
| `tree(root)` | `TreeResponse` — recursive; every regular file appears (YAZ-1577 D1) carrying its preview `kind` — exact `FileKind` for Markdown/text/PDF/image, `null` for a file with no in-app viewer, which the renderer hands to the OS default app; every dir shows, files or not (GRO-2022); dot-entries and `node_modules` are skipped; dirs before files, each sorted case-insensitive. Missing / not a dir → `NOT_FOUND` / `NOT_A_DIRECTORY` |
| `readFile(path)` | `FileResponse` — Markdown or approved view-only text, 10 MiB maximum. Markdown preserves the legacy Node UTF-8 decode behavior; view-only text uses fatal UTF-8 decoding, preserves a UTF-8 BOM, and rejects NUL. PDF, image and unsupported extensions → `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION` |
| `readPdf(path)` | `PdfResponse { path, data: Uint8Array, mtime, size }` — `.pdf` only, 50 MiB maximum, dedicated binary IPC for the native viewer; never base64 and never routed through `readFile`/`readAsset` |
| `readImage(path)` | `ImageResponse { path, data: Uint8Array, mime, mtime, size }` — exact absolute path only; approved raster extensions only; MIME comes from the single extension allowlist; 50 MiB maximum; never fuzzy, base64 or routed through `readFile`/`readAsset` |
| `writeFile({ path, content, expectedMtime? })` | `FileWriteResponse { path, mtime, size }` — `.md`/`.markdown` only; atomic write (`<name>.tmp-<rand>` + `rename`); parent dir must exist; if `expectedMtime` is given and the disk mtime differs → rejects `CONFLICT` with the disk `mtime` and nothing is written |
| `createDir(path)` / `createFile(path \| { path, content? })` | `CreateDirResponse { path }` / `CreateFileResponse { path, mtime, size }` — parent must exist (else `NOT_FOUND`); `.md`/`.markdown` only, created empty (else `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`; TOMBSTONE: the `.base` branch, which seeded a minimal table view, went with the format in YAZ-844); the object form's `content` (Bible B, GRO-2202) replaces that default and lands in the same write — content-at-create for a page scaffolded from a folder page (YAZ-832), no create-then-write race; `wx` write, never overwrites: exists → `ALREADY_EXISTS` |
| `pickFolder()` | `PickFolderResponse` — Electron `dialog.showOpenDialog` (`openDirectory` + `createDirectory`) parented to the calling window (`desktop/src/main/ipc/dialog.ts`). Picked → `{ path }` (no trailing `/`); dismissed → `{ cancelled: true }`; dialog failure → `PICKER_FAILED` |
| `watch(root, listener) → unsubscribe` | `WatchEvent`s: first `ready` (at once for late joiners of an already-watched root), then `add` / `change` / `unlink` (+ `mtime`) for every regular file, viewer or not (YAZ-1577 D1), and `addDir` / `unlinkDir`; dot-entries and `node_modules` are ignored. Renderer consumers filter by kind themselves (the index and rename detection take Markdown only, the view-only catalog takes `isViewOnly` only), so an event for a file with no viewer refreshes the tree and nothing else. Image events refresh Files/view-only navigation and an open viewer but never trigger an index scan. A bad root yields one `error` event and no subscription. One chokidar per root in main shared by every window (`ignoreInitial: true`, `awaitWriteFinish: { stabilityThreshold: 200 }`); a window going away drops its subscriptions |
| `index(root)` | `IndexResponse { root, records, generatedAt }` (GRO-2129; TOMBSTONE ⚡ YAZ-815: `types?` — the `.obsidian/types.json` assignments, read per call for cell-editor inference (5B, GRO-2142) — rode here for a typing rung nothing ever fed, and the read went with the field) — every markdown note under `root` as an `IndexRecord` (path/stat fields, frontmatter `properties`, `aliases` (E2, GRO-2214), `tags`/`links`/`embeds` incl. body scan — D7), sorted by path; markdown only — nothing else has ever been a record. Full scan on first call per root (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/`), watcher-incremental after, evicted after ~10 min idle. Client: `useIndex(root, watch)` fetches once per root and refetches (300 ms debounce) on markdown `add`/`change`/`unlink`, `unlinkDir` and `ready` events |
| `coldDiff(root)` | `ColdStartDiffResponse { root, scannedAt, cacheStatus, added, removed, changed } \| null` (Links E1c, GRO-2242) — the cold-start reconcile diff the persistent index cache computed for `root` (see "Property index"): `added` = on disk but not in the cache (ON-DISK stats), `removed` = in the cache but gone from disk (CACHED stats), `changed` = stat drifted (re-scanned). Null before the first `index(root)` build for the root and again after idle eviction — the E1c detector reads it AFTER the first index snapshot. Honest miss semantics: trust the three lists only when `cacheStatus === 'hit'` — on `miss`/`corrupt`/`version-mismatch` they are empty and the status says why |
| `readAsset(root, ref)` | `AssetResponse { path, mime, data, size, mtime }` (GRO-2139 amended by D10: a bridge method, never a route; `mtime` added by YAZ-879 — the read is the only honest source of `writeAsset`'s `expectedMtime` guard) — a local image under `root` as base64 + mime (from the extension) for a `data:` URL. `ref` is a wikilink target or path (`\|alias` / `#heading` stripped like the index's link extraction): it is tried root-relative first (any ref, since YAZ-1658 — `resolveAsset`, which the `app://vault` protocol also uses with a note-relative step ahead of it); else/fallback Obsidian's shortest-path rule — case-insensitive basename match anywhere under root, first hit in a breadth-first walk (a shallower match always wins) with each directory's entries sorted case-insensitively, dot-entries and `node_modules` skipped. Images (png/jpg/jpeg/gif/webp/svg/avif/bmp, `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`) and drawings (`.excalidraw` → `application/json`, `DRAWING_EXTENSIONS`, YAZ-876) only — anything else `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`; no match → `NOT_FOUND`; above 10 MiB → `TOO_LARGE` (`desktop/src/main/fs/assets.ts`). This legacy asset/card/embed pipe may read SVG, but that does not make SVG a supported Files-viewer kind; full-file image viewing uses the separate exact-path `readImage` allowlist above. |
| `writeAsset({ root, path, content, expectedMtime?, create? })` | `AssetWriteResponse { path, mtime, size }` (YAZ-876, first build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852) — the write half of the ASSET pipe: a `.excalidraw` scene-JSON sidecar (UTF-8 STRING body, `DRAWING_EXTENSIONS`) or — since YAZ-1661 (D5, reversing YAZ-876's deliberate narrowing) — an image (`Uint8Array` body, `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`; the bytes ride Electron's structured clone untouched and a `subarray` writes exactly its own range). The body's KIND picks the allowed extensions: a mismatch is `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`, any other body `BAD_REQUEST`. **Never fuzzy** — `path` is an EXPLICIT vault-relative path (an absolute one under `root` is accepted too); `readAsset`'s basename search has no counterpart here, and a target resolving outside `root` is refused `BAD_REQUEST`. Parent folders are created on the way (`mkdir -p`, so the first drawing or pasted image in a vault makes `assets/drawings/` / `assets/images/` itself — the one deliberate divergence from `writeFile`, whose parent must exist). Otherwise `file.ts`'s semantics exactly: atomic write (`<name>.tmp-<rand>` + `rename`); `expectedMtime` given and the disk mtime differs → `CONFLICT` with the disk `mtime` and nothing written; `create: true` uses `createFile`'s `wx` and **never overwrites** — an existing target is `ALREADY_EXISTS`. Content above 10 MiB → `TOO_LARGE`. Deliberately NOT a supported file: `.excalidraw` is absent from the shared classifier, so a drawing has no in-app viewer and stays invisible to the Markdown index — it lists in the tree with `kind: null` like any other file (YAZ-1577 D4) and opens in the OS default app; the handler still needs no store repair and no broadcast, since nothing (tab, recents, index record) can point at one (`desktop/src/main/fs/assets.ts`) |
| `state.get/setSettings/setSidebarCollapsed/setSidebarWidth/pushRecent/removeRecent/setFolder/setFolds/setBaseGroups/onChange` | `AppState` in the main-owned `yaseendocs.json` (GRO-2159, D9; replaces the retired renderer web-storage `mdapp.*` keys); targeted mutators so windows never clobber each other; `removeRecent` drops a dead folder from the MRU (C2, GRO-2164); the lens left this surface for `window.setIdentity` in YAZ-1628; `setFolder`'s patch is `expanded` / `lastFile` / `topicsExpanded`, each type-checked at the IPC boundary and `topicsExpanded` capped main-side (YAZ-848); `setBaseGroups` replaces one view's collapsed group keys, keyed `<pagePath>::<viewName>` — a FOLDER PAGE's `.md` path since YAZ-844 re-keyed the bucket off the retired `.base` paths (4C, GRO-2137) |
| `window.identity/setIdentity/open/duplicate/onFlush` | window identity from `?win=<id>` (GRO-2159, see "App state"); `setIdentity`'s patch carries, beside `root` / `file` / `tabs` / `rightPanel`, the per-window chrome — `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280), `sidebarLens` (YAZ-1628; one of the two lenses, anything else `BAD_REQUEST`) and `focusDirs` / `focusTopics` (YAZ-1628) — each absent = untouched; `open({ root, file })` = an independent new window (GRO-2160 — the sidebar context menu's "Open in new window", D2 GRO-2168; ⌘-click opens a background tab instead since I3, GRO-2235); `duplicate()` = same folder + file + tabs as the calling window, cascaded bounds (the ⌘⇧N gesture, GRO-2167); `onFlush(listener)` = the close/quit flush handshake — main holds the close until every listener settled, hard 5 s cap (GRO-2160, see "Multi-window") |
| `menu.onOpenFolder/onOpenRoot` | pushes from the application menu to the focused window's renderer (B3, GRO-2161): File › Open Folder… (⌘⇧O) → run the pick-folder flow; File › Open Recent chose `path` → switch the root in place (see "Menus & shortcuts") |
| `menu.onSettings` | push from Yaseen Docs › Settings… (⌘,) to the focused window's renderer (`menu:settings`, YAZ-1679): open the settings dialog — the same one the sidebar cog opens (see "App state": `settings`) |
| `link.onOpenFile/onNotice` | pushes from deep-link routing (E1, GRO-2171): `onOpenFile(path)` = a link resolved to this window (the path is guaranteed inside its root); `onNotice(message)` = a link could not be opened — show it unobtrusively, never a dialog (see "Links") |
| `file.rename({ oldPath, newPath })` / `file.onRenamed` | In-app file/folder rename or move (Links E1/E1b): validates the target, never overwrites, and for files requires a transition that cannot misrepresent unchanged bytes (same kind for Markdown/text/PDF; same image extension case-insensitively, with `.jpg`↔`.jpeg` allowed). It repairs every stored main/right path plus roots/recents/folder state in one commit, then broadcasts `{ oldPath, newPath, kind }`. Each renderer carries dirty Markdown editor buffers and retires old controllers before `useWorkspace` remaps current paths, retained mounts and histories. The normal watcher unlink+add echo heals the appropriate index/catalog feed; no extra push or watcher exists. |
| `file.repairRename({ oldPath, newPath })` | Store/tab repair for a rename that ALREADY happened on disk (Links E1c, GRO-2242 — the confirmed-banner path, see "Links": Rename → External renames). There is no fs work: the handler validates the claim — `newPath` must EXIST (its stat derives `kind`), `oldPath` must NOT (`BAD_REQUEST` — a live old path means the hypothesis was wrong; refuse, never guess), the same encoding-preserving and dot-dir rules mirror `rename` — then runs the SAME `store.renamePath` repair and pushes the SAME `file:renamed { oldPath, newPath, kind }` to every window, so tabs, viewer continuity and title/hash reuse the E1 downstream unchanged. No vault-root guard (nothing moves; a window rooted at an externally renamed folder is exactly what the repair heals) |
| `file.delete({ path })` / `file.onDeleted` | Sends the file/folder to system Trash only; no permanent fallback. Validation rejects hidden infrastructure and the calling window's vault root. One store repair removes affected main/right paths and related recents/folder state while leaving `window.root` for the existing root-missing probe, then broadcasts `{ path, kind }`. Each renderer retires matching autosave controllers before `useWorkspace` removes ownership, preventing a dirty unmount from recreating the trashed file. Referencing notes remain byte-identical. |
| `shell.reveal({ path })` | Reveal in Finder (GRO-2274 — **VS Code parity is the locked reference for this issue**). `shell.showItemInFolder`: the entry is selected **in its parent**, for files, folders and the vault root alike — deliberately NOT `shell.openPath`, which would open a folder's contents; the verb is "Reveal", and VS Code and Obsidian both behave this way. The path is stat'd first so a stale row (deleted or moved externally) rejects `NOT_FOUND` — `showItemInFolder` returns `void` and silently does nothing for a missing path, which reads to the user as a broken menu item. Read-only, so unlike `file.delete` / `file.rename` there is deliberately **no dot-entry guard and no extension guard**: any absolute path the app can already show is fair game, matching the "no jail" posture above. No store repair and no broadcast — nothing changes — but still enveloped so the `NOT_FOUND` reaches the renderer as a passive notice |
| `shell.openVsCode({ path })` | Open in VS Code (YAZ-963). `shell.openExternal('vscode://file/<path>')`, every segment percent-encoded — the OS deep link, **never a spawned process** (no install-name or PATH coupling; the OS picks which VS Code answers). Same read-only posture and the same stat: a stale path rejects `NOT_FOUND` for the passive notice. Offered on every sidebar row, blank space (= the vault root) and the tab's menu. |
| `shell.openLink({ href, sourcePath? })` | Standard Markdown link routing (YAZ-1309). Main is the only trust boundary: `http`/`https`/`mailto`/`tel`/`ftp` are canonicalized and sent to `shell.openExternal`; absolute and note-relative `file:` targets resolve from `sourcePath`, lose URL query/fragment, and go to `shell.openPath`, so the OS default app decides. Fragment-only links stay renderer-local. Empty/malformed requests, non-local file URLs, and every other protocol reject before an OS side effect. Every `window.open` is denied by `setWindowOpenHandler`; allowlisted external popup URLs are rerouted through this same function, so Electron never creates a child browser. |
| `vaultConfig.read/write/onChange` | Vault-local config in `<root>/.yaseendocs/` (Desktop J, GRO-2188) — the `.obsidian/` analogue: travels with the vault. `read(root, name)` → parsed JSON or `null` (missing folder/file, or malformed JSON — one `console.warn`, never a throw); reading NEVER creates the folder. `write(root, name, value)` `mkdir -p`s the dotfolder lazily and writes pretty-printed JSON atomically (tmp + rename); `name` must be a plain `<stem>.json` basename (separators / `..` → `BAD_REQUEST`, other extensions → `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION`). The shared vault watcher never sees dot-entries, so main runs one tiny chokidar per open-vault root scoped to the dotfolder (`desktop/src/main/vaultConfig.ts`, subscribed per `AppState.windows` root, dropped with the last window); own writes notify synchronously and their watcher echo is deduped by mtime, external edits (sync tools) are debounced — either way every window gets `vaultConfig:changed { root, name }` and filters by its own root. `.yaseendocs` never appears in the tree/sidebar, the vault index, or a folder page's contents |
| `properties.get/setProperty/removeProperty/onChange` | Vault-wide property declarations in `<root>/.yaseendocs/properties.json` (YAZ-835, succeeding the Bible A registry of GRO-2201) — the ONE surviving registry concept: what editor a frontmatter key gets and what a link column points at, for the whole vault. Read/written through vaultConfig (`desktop/src/main/properties/`, Electron-free like the index). `get(root)` → `PropertiesResponse { root, version, properties, error? }` — see "Properties" under "View modules" for the full rules (version semantics, lazy creation, validation grammar, the `types.json` clean break). Any change — in-app mutation or external edit (the vaultConfig dotfolder watcher) — broadcasts `properties:changed { root, properties }` to every window; client: `useProperties(root)` (`client/src/views/`) → `{ status, properties, error }`, live-replaced on each broadcast (a corrupt properties.json is `status: 'ready'` with `properties.error` set, not a fetch error) |
| `github.status/syncNow/setEnabled/onStatus` | Per-vault GitHub sync (YAZ-1081) — plain `git` against the vault's own repo, no GitHub API and no token of ours. **OFF BY DEFAULT and per vault**: the switch is `<root>/.yaseendocs/github.json`, read as `{ enabled: true }` EXACTLY (absent, malformed or `"true"` all mean off), so it travels with the folder and a corrupt file fails closed rather than starting background git work nobody asked for; a disabled root is completely silent — no watcher, no timers, no passes. The repo is AUTO-DETECTED, never configured here: a root with no `.git` at its own level, or no `origin`, is `off` (not an error), and `status` on an off root still reports read-only `repo { remoteUrl, branch }` for a settings panel. Every method answers the same `GithubSyncStatus { root, state, attention?, message?, repo? }` the push carries — `off` / `synced` / `pending` / `syncing` / `attention` (`no-git` / `no-identity` / `auth` / `conflict` / `error`) — so a mutation never needs a follow-up read; `status` answers from the last broadcast without touching git. Passes are EVENT-DRIVEN (`desktop/src/main/git/manager.ts`): opening or enabling a vault pulls, edits mark it `pending` at once then commit after a 30s quiet period, an offline pass arms ONE 2-min retry, and window focus / OS wake (`powerMonitor` `resume` + `unlock-screen`, wired in `main/index.ts`) pull behind a 10s cooldown. One pass at a time per root, a burst collapsing into exactly one follow-up. A pass is commit → fetch → rebase → push, and the **lossless rule** is absolute: nothing is ever stashed, and a rebase that conflicts is `--abort`ed — the working tree comes back byte-for-byte and the vault reports `attention/conflict` for a human. On quit the renderers flush FIRST, then `flushForQuit` runs the final pass, so the last edit is in the last commit. `github:status-changed` goes to EVERY window on every transition; renderers filter by `status.root` |

Bases (GRO-2097) adds its methods to `YaseenDocsApi` additively (`index(root)` and `readAsset(root, ref)` above), never routes; the drawing embed (YAZ-852) extends the same pipe the same way with `writeAsset(req)`.

Notes
- Folder picking (client, `usePickFolder`): the sidebar's "change" and the Welcome screen's Open folder… call `pickFolder()`; `{ path }` → set root + push to recents, `{ cancelled }` or a rejection → nothing. No dialog ever opens by itself — a window with no folder shows the Welcome screen (C2, GRO-2164). One dialog in flight at a time; the trigger buttons are disabled meanwhile. There is no in-app folder browser.
- Main-process writes trigger `change` events on the watcher; the client must ignore events for a path whose mtime equals the mtime it just received from its own `writeFile` (echo suppression).
- Auto-save: client debounces 500 ms after last `markdownUpdated`, also flushes on file switch / the close flush handshake (`window.onFlush` — GRO-2158/2160 — is what guarantees the last save; `beforeunload` is best effort). Only content that differs from the last loaded/saved markdown is saved (Crepe's first serialisation is a normalised rewrite and is never written on its own). Frontmatter-only external changes absorb silently (GRO-2186): an incoming `change` whose BODY matches the last loaded/saved body just replaces the autosave baseline — no reload, no conflict bar — so unsaved body edits and the caret survive a cell edit landing on that note's frontmatter from a folder page's contents block — and, on the folder page itself, its own `folder_page_settings` write (see "Cell editing", "The contents block").
- Desktop tests run chokidar with `CHOKIDAR_USEPOLLING=1` (see `desktop/vitest.config.ts`): on macOS libuv starts the FSEvents stream asynchronously, so a write right after `ready` can be missed; polling makes the tests deterministic.

## App state (`yaseendocs.json`, main-owned — GRO-2159, D9)

One user-global JSON file owned by the main process: `app.getPath('userData')/yaseendocs.json` = `~/Library/Application Support/Yaseen Docs/yaseendocs.json` (`app.setName('Yaseen Docs')` runs before anything reads userData; `--user-data-dir=<dir>` relocates it, which is how the Playwright checks seed state). Renderer web storage is not used at all. The renderer never touches the file: it reads a cache (`storage.ts` in client lib, filled by `storage.init()` before the first render) and writes through the targeted `state.*` mutators; `desktop/src/main/store.ts` owns the file. Nothing here is ever written into the vault or the markdown.

| Key | Shape | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `version` | `1` | anything else → the file is treated as corrupt (see below) |
| `settings` | `SettingsState` `{ lineSpacing, blockGap, bulletThreading, threadWidth, threadColor, theme, contentWidth, newNoteLocation, newNoteFolder, confirmDelete, commentsOrder }` | **Edited in the settings dialog** (YAZ-1679, `client/src/settings/SettingsDialog.tsx`): an Obsidian-style centred modal — ONE scrolling page of every settings section (Appearance · Editor · Files & Links · Sync) with grouped rows on the right, the section titles down the left as nav anchors (click = scroll to the section; the active one follows the scroll) under a search box, and below a nav divider the `standalone` sections that are their own page inside the dialog (Hotkeys — a reference, not settings) — opened by the sidebar-footer cog, `⌘,` or Yaseen Docs › Settings… (`menu:settings`), all three the ONE dialog App mounts. Every page, row and search hit reads from the one registry, `client/src/settings/registry.tsx` (`SETTINGS_SECTIONS`): a setting is declared once — id, label, hint, keywords, render — inside a section's GROUP (a titled sub-heading, optionally hinted, or plain rows), and each row carries `data-setting="<id>"` (the `SettingsState` field name for the eleven fields here, `githubSync` for the per-vault switch), which is how tests and e2e specs address a row. Search (`searchSettings.ts`) ranks with the `[[` picker's `matchLinkCandidates` over label + hint + keywords + group title + section title, and shows hits under a "Section › Group" breadcrumb. "Settings cog" below means this dialog. Global: one value for every folder and window; validated field by field on load (`threadWidth` ∈ 1/2/3, `threadColor` `#rrggbb` or null, `theme` ∈ system/light/dark — default `system`, so a pre-K file just gains it; `contentWidth` ∈ narrow/medium/full — default `narrow`, so older files and junk safely preserve the existing 1040px behavior; `newNoteLocation` ∈ root/current/folder — default `current` (YAZ-1643) — and `newNoteFolder` a root-relative path per `isValidNewNoteFolder` (`''` = the vault root; no leading/trailing `/`, and every segment must be one create-on-click would accept — so no empty segment, nothing starting with `.` (which subsumes `.`/`..`), no NUL: GRO-2197, because the panel used to accept `.archive` and then EVERY unresolved-link click failed on the per-segment `validateEntryName`; `shared/types.ts` holds the rule and `sidebar/createEntry.ts` is the sibling that must stay in step) — defaults, so a pre-C2 file just gains them; `confirmDelete` a boolean defaulting **true**; `commentsOrder` ∈ oldest/newest — default `oldest`, so a pre-1515 file just gains it; it is the settings-cog **Comments** control, `Oldest first` / `Newest first`, above the Files & Links section, and the comment block's own header toggle writes the same field — YAZ-1515), an invalid field falls back to `DEFAULT_SETTINGS`; `setSettings` takes a complete valid object (else `BAD_REQUEST`). `contentWidth` (YAZ-1176) is the settings-cog Narrow/Medium/Full control: App stamps it as `data-content-width` on `.app`, where one `--content-max-width` maps it to 1040px/1440px/fluid; page header, editor, folder-page contents, comments, and backlinks consume that one token and another window's change lands live through the existing `storage.subscribe` path. `newNoteLocation`/`newNoteFolder` (Files & Links, Links C2- — GRO-2240) are the settings-cog "Default location for new notes" control (Obsidian's three options; the folder input shows only for `folder` and validates on commit — invalid keeps the stored value) driving where a bare unresolved `[[link]]` creates its page (rule 23); the setting is GLOBAL but `newNoteFolder` is interpreted per-vault against each window's root. `confirmDelete` (GRO-2272 — VS Code's `explorer.confirmDelete`) gates the delete confirm sheet: it defaults ON and should stay that way, because the sheet is the ONLY guard delete has — `shell.trashItem` offers no programmatic un-trash, so there is no in-app restore behind it. Cleared by the sheet's own "Don't ask me again" and re-enabled from the settings cog (both halves are required: a one-way switch would leave hand-editing `yaseendocs.json` as the only way back). `theme` (Appearance, Desktop K — GRO-2218) is the settings-cog System/Light/Dark control: main mirrors it into `nativeTheme.themeSource` before the first window exists and on every change (`desktop/src/main/theme.ts`; new windows also get a matching `backgroundColor`, so no white flash on dark launches), and each renderer resolves it (`system` follows `prefers-color-scheme` live) to `data-theme="light"\|"dark"` on `<html>` — app.css's `[data-theme='dark']` token block — while swapping Crepe's frame/frame-dark var blocks in one managed `<style>` (`client/src/editor/crepeTheme.ts`, CSS-only: the open editor never remounts) |
| `sidebarCollapsed` (legacy top-level) | `boolean` | retired by YAZ-1280. On load it seeds only window entries that lack their own boolean; a per-window boolean wins; missing/junk → `false`. The sanitized snapshot and next persisted write omit this top-level key. |
| `sidebarWidth` | `number` | global; clamped to [180, 520] on load and on write; non-finite → 260 (YAZ-738) |
| `recents` | `RecentRoots` `[{ path, lastOpened }]` | most-recent first, de-duplicated by path, max 10 (`pushRecent(path)`, absolute path); wrong shape → `[]` |
| `windows` | `WindowEntry[]` `{ id, root, file, tabs, sidebarCollapsed, sidebarLens, focusDirs, focusTopics, bounds: { x, y, width, height } }` | one entry per open window, reopened on relaunch with bounds clamped to a live display (see "Multi-window"); malformed or duplicate-id entries dropped on load; `upsertWindow` replaces by id or appends; an entry is removed when its window closes — unless the app is quitting (⌘Q, or the last window closing, which quits), so relaunch restores the same windows. `tabs` (GRO-2232) = absolute paths left→right, with `file` doubling as the ACTIVE tab; `normalizeTabs` repairs both on load and every `setIdentity`. `sidebarCollapsed` (YAZ-1280) is an independent boolean per window: new/first/deep-link windows start open, a duplicate inherits then diverges, and legacy entries migrate from the retired top-level value as described above. `sidebarLens` (YAZ-847, per window since YAZ-1628) is `'topics' \| 'files'` on the same rule: new/first/deep-link windows start on Topics, a duplicate inherits then diverges, a root change KEEPS it (a view preference, not vault content — `setRoot` leaves it alone), `window.setIdentity` refuses anything but the two lenses (`BAD_REQUEST`), and legacy entries migrate exactly as `sidebarCollapsed` does — a v1 file's retired global `sidebarLens` seeds every window without a valid lens of its own, junk or missing (a PRE-847 file) reading as `topics`, and the next write drops the old key. `focusDirs` / `focusTopics` (YAZ-1605, per window since YAZ-1628) are Focus Mode's two path lists — the Files tree's focused DIRS and the Topics tree's focused folder PAGES, empty meaning no focus, uncapped — independent per window exactly as `sidebarCollapsed` is: new/first/deep-link windows start unfocused, a duplicate inherits both lists by value then diverges, a root change clears them (`setRoot`), and `renamePath` / `removePath` repair them as they repair `tabs`; on load they read with `tabs`' rule (relative or junk elements drop, a missing or junk list is no focus), and a pre-1628 file's per-vault `folders[root].focusDirs` / `focusTopics` are simply not read (no migration — the bucket could not say which window was focused; the next write drops them). |
| `folders[root]` | `FolderState` `{ expanded: string[], lastFile: string \| null, folds: { [file]: string[] }, baseGroups: { [pagePath::viewName]: string[] }, topicsExpanded: string[] }` | per folder; `setFolder(root, { expanded?, lastFile?, topicsExpanded? })` merges and creates the entry with defaults when missing (other keys ignored); `expanded` and `topicsExpanded` are SESSION lists since YAZ-1642 — held in the in-memory store and shared by every window on the vault, stripped from the file on every write (`toDisk`) and read as `[]` on load, so every launch starts both trees collapsed (the Sidebar likewise leaves the ancestors of the file it MOUNTS with closed; a file opened later still reveals) — while `lastFile`, `folds` and `baseGroups` persist; `topicsExpanded` (🔒 D4, YAZ-848 — the Topics tree's open folder pages) rides that SAME patch as `expanded`'s twin: one flat list of absolute PAGE paths, capped at `MAX_TOPICS_EXPANDED_PAGES` (500) client-side and again main-side, keyed by the page rather than by tree position (one entry opens the page under every parent it appears beneath) and repaired by `store.renamePath` / `store.removePath` exactly as `expanded` is, being a path-keyed bucket like it — never written into any note's frontmatter (see "The Topics sidebar"); `setFolds(root, file, keys)` replaces one file's collapsed outline fold keys (max 500; an empty list removes the file entry, the folder entry stays); `setBaseGroups(root, key, collapsed)` does the same for one view's collapsed group keys (max 200, 4C — GRO-2137). The `baseGroups` KEY is `<pagePath>::<viewName>` and since YAZ-844 that path is a folder page's own `.md` — the bucket was re-keyed off the retired `.base` paths, and it stays session chrome that is never written into the page's own card (the name kept its `base` spelling because the store's rename/delete repair, `remapBaseGroupKey` / `baseGroupGone`, is pinned to it by tests); invalid lists dropped on load |

Write semantics: every mutation updates memory, notifies `onChange` listeners synchronously and schedules one debounced (150 ms) atomic write (`<file>.tmp-<rand>` + rename, pretty-printed JSON, parent dir created on demand); `flush()` writes at once and `before-quit` holds the quit until it lands (`event.preventDefault()` + `app.exit(0)`), so nothing is lost on ⌘Q. A file that is unparsable, not an object or not `version: 1` is moved to `yaseendocs.json.corrupt-<epoch>` and the defaults are used — the bad file is never overwritten silently. Unknown top-level keys are dropped on load.

Live broadcast: after any change — from any window, or from main itself (window upserts) — every live `BrowserWindow` gets `state:changed` with the whole new state; the preload surfaces it as `state.onChange`, the client cache replaces itself and `storage.subscribe` listeners fire (`App` re-reads global settings and sidebar width). Per-window `sidebarCollapsed` — and `sidebarLens` since YAZ-1628 — is read from the boot identity and written optimistically through `window.setIdentity`, so another window's state echo cannot change it.

Per-window identity: main loads each window as `<renderer>?win=<id>` and keeps a `webContents.id → window id` lookup (`desktop/src/main/windows.ts`). `window.identity()` answers `{ id, root, file, tabs, sidebarCollapsed }` from `windows[]` for the calling window (`BAD_REQUEST` for an unregistered sender, `NOT_FOUND` once the entry is gone); `window.setIdentity({ root?, file?, tabs?, sidebarCollapsed? })` validates and merges into that entry (`id` and `bounds` cannot be changed this way). On the client, `storage.getRoot()` and `storage.getSidebarCollapsed()` are identity reads; root/file/tab helpers and the sidebar toggle write back through that same targeted identity path.

## Menus & shortcuts (B3 — GRO-2161)

The menu bar is a pure template (`desktop/src/main/menu.ts`, `buildMenuTemplate(inputs, handlers)`) so structure, accelerators and roles unit-test without Electron; `main/index.ts` applies it (`Menu.buildFromTemplate` + `setApplicationMenu`) and rebuilds it ONLY when `AppState.recents` actually changed (`subscribeMenuRebuild` compares by reference — store snapshots reuse untouched sub-objects). Item ids are stable (`menu.file.new-window`, `menu.file.open-recent.<i>`, …) so a live check (Playwright) can drive them via `Menu.getApplicationMenu().getMenuItemById(...)`. `buildContextMenuTemplate(params, actions)` is the same idiom for the right-click menu (spell suggestions → Add to Dictionary → cut/copy/paste per `editFlags`; on an `<img>` — `params.mediaType === 'image'`, which Chromium sets only for elements laid out as images, never inline `<svg>`, so sidebar icons keep the text rows — exactly two rows instead: Copy Image through the native `webContents.copyImageAt`, and Reveal in Finder through `revealVaultImage` → `revealItem`, a silent no-op for a non-vault src such as a card cover, YAZ-1666); every window's factory wires it in `main/index.ts` (YAZ-672).

"The focused window" in the table below means `pickMenuTargetWindow` (`menu.ts`, GRO-2197), not a bare `BrowserWindow.getFocusedWindow()`: the focused window when there IS one, else the window focused most recently, else any live window. macOS reports NO focused window whenever the app is not the frontmost application — a detached DevTools window, a native panel, or a programmatic `menuItem.click()` from the e2e harness — and every one of these items used to become a silent no-op in that state (measured: `tabs.spec.ts`'s ⌃Tab step failed 4 runs in 5 under CPU load because the `menu:next-tab` message was never sent at all). A menu item that quietly does nothing is the worst available answer, so the fallback IS the contract. `main/index.ts` tracks the last-focused id from `app.on('browser-window-focus')`; the picker itself is Electron-free and unit-tested.

| Item | Keys | Does |
|---|---|---|
| Yaseen Docs › Settings… | `⌘,` | sends `menu.onSettings` to the focused window's renderer, which opens the settings dialog (YAZ-1679; the renderer owns the dialog, so the gesture never touches it from main); no focused renderer → no-op |
| File › New Window | `⌘⇧N` | duplicates the FOCUSED window — same folder, same file, cascaded bounds (`window.duplicate` semantics, GRO-2167); no focused window → no-op |
| File › Open Folder… | `⌘⇧O` | sends `menu.onOpenFolder` to the focused window's renderer, which runs its normal pick-folder flow (the renderer owns root switching) |
| File › Open Recent › `<path>` | — (⌥-click = open beside) | plain click sends `menu.onOpenRoot(path)` — switch the root in place, MRU bumped by the renderer; ⌥-click opens a NEW window on that folder instead — main probes the directory first (a dead folder is pruned from the MRU and opens nothing, GRO-2211) and bumps the MRU itself. A programmatic `menuItem.click()` passes no event, so it opens in place. Empty MRU → one disabled "No Recent Folders" item |
| File › Search Vault | `⌘K` | sends `menu.onSearch` to the focused window's renderer, which focuses its sidebar search bar — un-collapsing the sidebar first when it is hidden (YAZ-804; the renderer owns the bar, so the gesture never touches it from main). The bar is ROW 2 of the chrome and lives on BOTH lenses since YAZ-847, so ⌘K behaves identically whichever tab is showing and never switches the lens; the results then replace that ACTIVE tab's body (🔒 D5 — see "The Topics sidebar") |
| File › Close Tab | `⌘W` | sends `menu.onCloseTab` to the focused window's renderer — the ⌘W ladder (see "Multi-window": Tabs): close the active tab; with zero tabs open (Welcome included) close the WINDOW through the real close path (`window.closeSelf`) |
| File › Close Window | `⌘⇧W` | role `close` — goes through the flush handshake like any close (see "Multi-window") |
| Window › Next / Previous Tab | `⌃Tab` / `⌃⇧Tab` | sends `menu.onNextTab` / `menu.onPrevTab` to the focused window's renderer — wraparound cycle over the strip's left→right order; hidden menu duplicates carry the `⌘⇧]` / `⌘⇧[` equivalents (`acceleratorWorksWhenHidden`) |
| View › Toggle Sidebar | — | sends `menu.onToggleSidebar` to the focused window's renderer, which runs the same local toggle as every other sidebar gesture; no focused renderer → no-op |
| App chrome | `⌘B` | renderer-owned sidebar toggle (YAZ-1280): only plain Cmd+B outside input/textarea/select/contenteditable and while no aria-modal tool is open. Milkdown keeps Cmd+B for bold; Yaseendraw/modal tools keep their own gesture. There is deliberately no Electron accelerator or global shortcut. |
| View › Reload / zoom roles, Edit roles, Window roles | — | stock Electron roles; dev builds add View › Toggle Developer Tools; the Window submenu has `role: 'window'` so macOS appends the window list |
| Help › Yaseen Docs on GitHub | — | `shell.openExternal(HELP_URL)` |

## Multi-window (D3/D6 — GRO-2160/2167/2168/2169)

The window manager (`desktop/src/main/windows.ts`) is Electron-free — `main/index.ts` injects window construction and display geometry as a `WindowHost`, so everything below runs under vitest with fakes.

- **Restore** (D3): on launch, one window per `AppState.windows` entry; an empty state seeds a single Welcome window (`root: null`). Restored bounds are clamped fully inside the display work area they overlap most (fully off-screen → the nearest one; oversized → shrunk to fit) — so a window from an unplugged display always lands somewhere visible. Bounds changes (`move`/`resize`) are committed to the state debounced (250 ms per burst — one `state:changed` per drag).
- **Duplicate** (GRO-2167): `⌘⇧N` / `window.duplicate()` — same folder, same file, same TABS (the copy carries the whole set), fresh id, bounds cascaded +24 px and clamped at the display edge. A Welcome window duplicates to another Welcome window.
- **Open beside** (D2, GRO-2168): a file row's context-menu "Open in new window" → `window.open({ root, file })` — an independent window on the same folder with that file open. Open Recent's ⌥-click and deep-link routing use the same `openWindow` path (default bounds, clamped). ⌘-click on a sidebar file row is NOT this gesture any more: since I3 (GRO-2235, locked) it opens a background tab in the SAME window — see Tabs below.
- **Tabs** (GRO-2232/2234/2235): `WindowEntry.tabs` is the ordered main-pane set and `file` is its active path. `normalizeTabs` repairs legacy/malformed identity; `tabs: []` iff `file: null`, the active path belongs to the set, and paths are unique. The renderer model now lives inside `client/src/workspace/useWorkspace.ts` with the right panel; it mirrors one complete workspace identity per logical change, while `folders[root].lastFile` changes only when the active main file changes. Sidebar/deep-link opens replace the current tab, ⌘-click appends in background, activation lazy-mounts one retained editor layer, drag reorders, middle-click/✕ closes, and ⌘W follows right-neighbour then left-neighbour then empty-window then close-window. Per-tab Back/Forward history remains renderer-session-only. **The strip** (`client/src/tabs/`, `.tabbar`): tabs shrink to a ~120 px floor first and then the strip SCROLLS, Obsidian-like, with the ACTIVE tab `scrollIntoView`n on every activation. Since YAZ-1471 neither the scrolling nor the reordering arithmetic is spelled here alone. The scroll is the shared `.scroll-strip` utility in `app.css`, worn beside `.tabbar` and by a folder page's view tabs too ("View chrome": Toolbar): `overflow-x: auto` with NO scrollbar (`scrollbar-width: none` is the whole hiding rule on Chromium ≥ 121, so the old `::-webkit-scrollbar` rules are gone), plus a `@keyframes strip-fade` mask driven by `animation-timeline: scroll(self inline)` — a scroll-driven fade marking whichever edge still hides tabs, and a strip that does not overflow has no timeline and so no mask at all. The insertion-slot idiom this strip invented is now `client/src/lib/dragSlot.ts` — `insertionSlot(e, i)` (the target's own horizontal midpoint) and `dropIndex(from, slot)` (the grabbed item leaves the list before it re-enters, so a slot past the grab point shifts one left) — ONE spelling for both horizontal strips, so the window's tabs and a folder page's view tabs cannot drift apart; the validated right→main insertion slots above run on it, and so does the view tabs' own reorder.
- **Right panel** (YAZ-966): `client/src/workspace/useWorkspace.ts` is the renderer's ONE owner for main tabs, right headers, both session histories, retained mounts, and lifecycle repair. The durable per-window identity is `{ open, width, items, expanded }` beside `{ tabs, file }`; legacy entries gain the default, width is clamped to 320–720 px (440 default), and normalization enforces absolute unique paths plus global-in-window exclusive ownership. Every logical mutation mirrors ONE complete `{ tabs, file, rightPanel }` identity. The panel reuses the full `Editor` and the window's existing watch/index/properties/sync feeds; one right header is expanded at most, visited expanded headers retain hidden editor layers, and plain/⌘ link navigation plus Back/Forward stay right-local. Ownership transfers call `carryEditorAcrossPane` before the source unmounts or the identity mirrors, so dirty text crosses without a second editor/cache. Entry gestures are the shared Table/Board `PageContextMenu` (`Open in right panel` LAST since YAZ-1556 — the pane-specific open sits below the three page actions every surface shares), ⌥-click or ⌥⏎ on a board card or a table name (YAZ-1557 🔒 D1 — ONE rule in `client/src/lib/openGesture.ts` for both views, mouse and keyboard: plain = current tab, ⌘ = background tab (I3), ⌥ = right panel, ⇧/⌃ = nothing, because ⇧ is the selection gesture everywhere; a plain click on a board card no longer opens anything — it SELECTS the card, 🔒 D2 below under "Views"; secondary-click, group drag, and preview remain separate), tab/header move commands, and native drag under private MIME `application/x-yaseen-workspace-page`; only validated absolute `{ path, owner: 'main' | 'right' }` payloads enter midpoint/end insertion slots, stale/wrong/external data no-op.
