The divider previews locally and persists only on commit; below 192 px hides without losing width, while narrow available space derives an overlay without rewriting identity. Rename/delete/root repair covers current paths, retained mounts, expanded choice, and histories; delete retires autosave before workspace removal, rename/transfer carries before remap, and duplicate windows deep-copy identity so later changes are independent. Automated ownership/drag/shell/lifecycle proof lives in `client/src/workspace/*.test.*`, `client/src/right-panel/RightPanel.test.tsx`, `client/src/tabs/TabBar.test.tsx`, `client/src/App.test.tsx`, and the desktop store/window/IPC tests. Headed Playwright is excluded for this feature; final acceptance uses the isolated Electron profile and focused demo vault before merge.
- **Single instance** (GRO-2160): `requestSingleInstanceLock` — a second launch quits itself; the first instance gets `second-instance`, routes any `yaseendocs://` URLs in its argv (E1), else focuses a window.
- **Close/quit flush handshake** (GRO-2160): `close` is ALWAYS intercepted — main commits the bounds, sends `app:flush`, the renderer flushes its autosaves (`window.onFlush` listeners) and acks `app:flushed`, then main `destroy()`s the window; a hung save can never block close (hard 5 s cap). A user close removes the window's state entry; the LAST window closing quits (`window-all-closed`), and quits — like ⌘Q's sequential flush of every window — keep the entries so relaunch restores them.
- **Same file in N windows** (GRO-2169 — D6/D9/A4): no dedicated sync channel — the disk-change path IS the sync. A window's save lands on disk → the shared watcher emits `change` to every window → each other window reloads silently when clean, or shows the conflict bar (Reload / Keep mine) when dirty. Echo suppression is per window: each window's own `Autosave` controller holds the mtime from ITS last `writeFile` response and only drops events matching that mtime (client `editor/Editor.tsx` — the one host left since YAZ-844 retired `BaseHost`), so window A's save is never mistaken for window B's echo. Folds are shared per file through the app state (`folders[root].folds`) — last writer wins, applied the next time the file is opened; a live window never adopts ANOTHER window's keys. ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-1342: a window's OWN silent reload does re-fold, from its LIVE session keys — both fold plugins take `seedCollapsedKeys`, a getter read on every state init rather than a set frozen at mount — so folds on unchanged lines survive an external (AI) edit to the open file and the rebuilt plugin can no longer report an empty set and wipe the persisted keys. ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-1347: that silent reload is no longer a rebuild — `applyExternalMarkdown` (`editor/external/`) parses the new body, structurally diffs it against the live doc (`diffDocs.ts`) and dispatches ONE `addToHistory: false` transaction stamped `external-edit`, so folds, caret, zoom and scroll ride ProseMirror's position mapping through ANY reword of a collapsed line, and the persisted keys re-derive from the live labels afterwards. The content-hash keys (rule 5) now serve cold start and the fallback only: a whole-document rewrite (one range ≥ 80% of the doc), an unparsable body, or a diff that fails to reproduce the parsed doc exactly falls back to the `setMarkdown` rebuild and its key-based reseed, so no edit ever behaves worse than v0.9.1. Enumeration prefixes (`12) `, `3. `, `X) `) are stripped from key labels (YAZ-1353), so a renumber done while the app is closed keeps cold-start folds too. Zoom is per window and in-memory only, never persisted. One chokidar per folder in main, shared by every window and subscription (`desktop/src/main/fs/watchers.ts`); it closes when the last subscriber leaves.

## Links (E1/E2/E3 — GRO-2171/2172/2173)

- **Format** (E1, locked D7): a note's shareable URL is its `yaseendocs://` file link — path-only, absolute, percent-encoded (`yaseendocs:///Users/me/vault/My%20note.md`), no vault id; an optional `?root=` (also a percent-encoded absolute path) overrides which folder it opens under. `shared/links.ts` (`fileLink`/`parseFileLink`) is the ONE encoding, shared by main's parser and its own `open-file` re-encode (E2); `encodeURI` plus `#` and `?` encoded on top, so either never truncates the path.
- **Copy link** — TOMBSTONE (YAZ-1554): the sidebar row menu's "Copy link" (E3 GRO-2173, a `yaseendocs://` URL; a `[[wikilink]]` since YAZ-957) was deleted. It sat directly under "Copy path" and the two were easy to confuse, and `[[` already links from inside a note. Everything that existed only for it went with it: `MenuTargets.copyLinkText`, the sidebar's `viewOnlyLinks` prop, and `linkName` on the view-only source and catalog. `linkNames` (completion.ts) remains the one spelling rule, read by sync-from-folder and adoption; main's `yaseendocs://` handler and `shared/links.ts` are untouched.
- **Routing** (E1): a link — clicked anywhere (`open-url`), handed over by Finder's Open With (`open-file`, E2 — the plain path is re-encoded through `fileLink` and rides the same pipeline), or in a second launch's argv — routes identically (`resolveLinkTarget`): (1) the open window whose root contains the file — most specific root wins, Welcome windows never match — is focused and switches to it (`link.onOpenFile`); (2) else a new window opens on the most recent recents folder containing it (`?root=` pins the effective root instead when it contains the path); (3) else on the file's parent directory. Links queue until the windows are restored on cold start (`linkQueue.ts` — macOS fires `open-url` before `ready`), so hot and cold behave the same.
- **Failure surface** (E1): an unsupported, missing or non-regular-file target — or an unparsable link — focuses a live window and shows the passive in-window notice (`link.onNotice`), never a dialog. Every supported Markdown/text/PDF/image file may route through the existing window/new-window pipeline.
- **In-window wiki-link navigation** (Links C, GRO-2192) is RENDERER-LOCAL: clicking a `[[wikilink]]` in the editor drives this window's own tabs API (`openCurrent` / `openBackground`, Editor rule 23) — no IPC, no main-process involvement; the manager's `routeToFile` (the Routing bullet above, `windows.ts`) stays the ONLY path that focuses another window or creates one.
- **Aliases** (Links E2, GRO-2214): a note's frontmatter `aliases` are extra NAMES it answers to across the whole app — the index carries them (`IndexRecord.aliases`, table below), the ONE resolver consults them LAST (see "Link resolution"), so `[[CAC]]` renders resolved (Editor rule 23) and clicking it opens the aliased page, and the `[[` picker offers the page under each alias, inserting the piped `[[Name|Alias]]` (rule 24). Two consequences pinned elsewhere: alias values are never outgoing `links`, and a rename never rewrites an alias-form link (Rename, below).
- **Rename** (E1, GRO-2194 — decision E of GRO-2096, LOCKED): an in-app rename updates links AUTOMATICALLY, no prompt (Obsidian's default). ⚡ AMENDED by YAZ-888 for NAME changes only: a changed NAME now asks first, once, in one sheet — the links still ALWAYS update, and a MOVE still never asks, so everything below is unchanged and runs on confirm ("The page title + rename confirm"). Scope since E1b (GRO-2241): FILE rename and cross-directory move, and FOLDER rename — the extension kind stays unchanged for files; E1b lifted E1's same-parent and files-only guards. Gestures: the sidebar file- OR folder-row context menu's "Rename" → inline input over the row (files: prefilled name minus extension, `validateEntryName` + the `entryPath` extension re-append via `renamedPath`; folders: raw name, no extension logic; same name = no-op; no hotkey v1); and drag a FILE row onto a folder row or the root header → move there (same pipeline; no multi-select, no folder dragging — Future). Flow, in order: (1) the originating window FLUSHES its own autosave for the file, so the buffer travels with it; (2) it snapshots the vault index BEFORE renaming (afterwards the old name no longer resolves); (3) `file.rename` renames on disk, repairs the app state and broadcasts `file:renamed` (bridge table above); (4) every window's renderer — BEFORE remapping its tab — captures a DIRTY old-path buffer, RETIRES the old autosave controller (its unmount/close flushes become no-ops: the old path can never be resurrected) and stashes the buffer under the new path, where the freshly mounted editor applies it as an UNSAVED change (`lib/renameContinuity.ts`; title/hash follow the active tab's existing effects); (5) the originating window rewrites every referencing note (`links/renameLinks.ts`): the referencing set is the index records whose `links`/`embeds` resolve — through THE shared resolver (`resolverFor`) — to the old path; per file read → rewrite → write with `expectedMtime`, one CONFLICT re-read-retry, a second conflict skips the file. Rewrites preserve `|alias` and `#heading`/`#^block`, preserve link FORM (bare→new bare name, pathed→new root-relative, explicit extension stays), cover `![[embeds]]`, skip fenced/inline code (a length-preserving mirror of the index's `stripCode`), and touch frontmatter only as whole-value exact `[[…]]` strings via `setFrontmatterProperty`. ONE key is walked DEEPER than whole-value (YAZ-864): `folder_page_settings`, the single reserved key with app-defined link semantics (🔒 Q1 — "Folder page settings"), whose `views[].order` entries and `columns.<name>.target` strings are links the index never extracted and a rename therefore used to leave dangling; they rewrite exactly as a top-level entry does (same `resolves`/`newTarget`, so the bare-vs-pathed and alias rules cannot drift), the whole key goes back through `setFrontmatterProperty` as the one door's own write shape, and the referencing-set probe learned the SAME leaves — a page whose only reference lives in there has no `links` entry to be found by, so probe and rewrite stay one construction. The key's own module owns both the key name and the list of link-bearing leaves (`folderPageSettings.ts` `mapFolderPageSettingsLinks` / `folderPageSettingsLinks`), read from the RAW value rather than the tolerant read, so a hand-written unusable shape is never normalised away by a rename; a stale entry that never resolved to the renamed page stays byte-identical, as it always did. Outcomes are PASSIVE (never a dialog): one summary notice "Updated links in N notes" (+ "; M skipped (unsaved changes)"), `ALREADY_EXISTS` and other failures land in the same link-notice; another window's dirty referencing editor falls back to the normal conflict bar — EXCEPT for a referencing note that the rename itself remaps (a folder rename moving an open dirty note), whose editor remounts, re-reads from disk and adopts the post-rewrite baseline, so the conflict never surfaces (GRO-2197 audit, deferred). Accepted residual race: a save already in flight over IPC when the rename lands can still recreate the old path — the systematic paths (debounce, unmount flush, close flush) are all covered by the retire step. ALIAS-form links are never rewritten (E2, GRO-2214): `[[CAC]]` reaching the moved file through its frontmatter `aliases` keeps resolving afterwards — the alias travels with the file — so the referencing-set probe resolves BY NAME ONLY (`resolverFor(records, root, { aliases: false })`); a note referencing the moved file only by alias is never even read. KNOWN GAP (GRO-2197 audit, deferred): the banner's N and the number of notes actually written can still disagree on a pure MOVE — N counts every note whose links RESOLVE to the old path, while the rewrite writes nothing for a bare link that stays bare, and in a pure move every bare link stays bare, so "update N links?" can be followed by "Updated links in 0 notes". Closing it needs N to become link-FORM aware, not just resolution aware. E1b bare-vs-pathed rules, LOCKED: a FOLDER rename rewrites only PATHED links/embeds (to the new root-relative path) — bare-name links keep resolving (names unchanged) and stay BYTE-IDENTICAL; a FILE move keeps a bare link bare only when the bare form still resolves to the moved file AFTERWARDS (checked through the resolver against a POST-move record set — the shallowest rule can hand the name to a duplicate), else it escalates to the pathed form (a kept bare form is never spliced, padding included). TOMBSTONE (YAZ-844): the base-embed half of this rule is gone — `resolveBasePath`, the PRE-rename TREE snapshot the rewrite used to take beside the index one, and the `tree` argument of `updateLinksAfterRename` / `countLinkReferences` all died with `![[X.base]]`. The retired engine `tree` argument stays gone: Markdown targets resolve through index records, while explicit text/PDF/image targets use the separate catalog; App may fetch `api.tree` only before the rename gate to pin that catalog. Refusals (all passive notices): existing target → `ALREADY_EXISTS`; missing target parent → `NOT_FOUND` (never a mkdir); dot-dirs and the calling window's own vault ROOT → `BAD_REQUEST` (root identity is a recents/vault question, out of E1b scope). A window rooted AT (or under) the renamed folder follows it live: its tabs prefix-remap and its local root state remaps without an identity write (main's store repair already moved `WindowEntry.root`).
- **External renames** (E1c, GRO-2242 — LOCKED ruling): a rename/move done OUTSIDE the app (Finder, terminal, sync tools) is detected and repaired too — CONFIRM-FIRST, never automatically, never a dialog (ahead of Obsidian, which does not repair external renames at all — verified). ONE hypothesis detector (`client/src/links/renameDetector.ts` `detectRenames`), TWO feeds: (a) COLD-START — after the first index snapshot the client reads `coldDiff(root)` (bridge table above) and joins `removed` × `added`; trusted only on `cacheStatus === 'hit'`; (b) WHILE-RUNNING — consecutive `useIndex` snapshots (`WikilinkIndexBridge.onSnapshot` — no new watchers, no polling), diffed into the same removed/added shape, with UNMATCHED removals and additions carried forward exactly ONE snapshot generation. That carry is load-bearing, not belt-and-braces (GRO-2197): chokidar's `awaitWriteFinish` holds an `add` back ~200 ms while `unlink` fires at once, and the index applies `unlink` synchronously but `add` behind an async `scanFile`, so the index genuinely passes through a ~100 ms window showing the removal WITHOUT the addition. The 300 ms refetch debounce usually straddles both — but when it does not, the pair splits across two generations, `detectRenames` never sees it, and the hypothesis was previously lost forever (measured: `externalRename.spec.ts` steps 1 and 3 failed ~50-100% of runs under CPU load, 0/9 idle). Carried entries are re-validated against the new snapshot first (a removal whose path came back was a save, not a rename; an addition that vanished again is dropped) and only the CURRENT generation's leftovers are carried on, so the memory is bounded to one generation. Guard rails, firm: markdown files only; join key = EXACT (size, mtime) equality (macOS rename/move preserves both); zero-byte files skip; only UNAMBIGUOUS 1:1 pairs — a signature carried by several removed or several added files skips entirely (no guessing); equal basenames (a pure move) still hypothesize — pathed links may need rewriting. Either feed lands in ONE passive app-level banner (`useExternalRenames` — the link-notice idiom with two actions, one hypothesis at a time, oldest first): "Looks like `X` became `Y` — update N links?" [Update] [Dismiss]. N is the rewrite engine's own referencing-set count (`countLinkReferences`, the dry-run twin of `updateLinksAfterRename`, over a PRE-rename snapshot synthesised by re-pathing the moved record back — `preRenameRecords`); N === 0 → no banner, nothing happens at all. Update → (1) `file.repairRename` (bridge table above — store/tabs/editors follow via the existing `file:renamed` downstream; a refused claim, e.g. the old path came back, lands as a passive notice), (2) the E1/E1b rewrite engine old→new over fresh index/tree snapshots re-pathed pre-rename, (3) the usual summary notice — always shown here (the user explicitly asked). Dismiss → the pair joins a SESSION-level dismissed set and is never re-offered (the next refetch re-detects the same diff; the set is what keeps it quiet); a handled (Updated) pair joins the same set. In-app renames echo through the watcher as identical unlink+add pairs, so every `file:renamed` push also feeds the suppression set (exact pair for files; prefix mapping for dirs, since a folder rename echoes as one pair per moved note) — own renames never banner. The DIR prefix mappings are cleared on a root switch (GRO-2197): they exist only to swallow the current vault's echoes, and left standing they sat in the per-hypothesis linear scan for the rest of the session, across vaults. (The dismissed PAIR set is session-level by ruling and deliberately survives a root switch — its keys are absolute paths.)
- **Backlinks** (Links D, GRO-2193 — decision D of GRO-2096, LOCKED): the reverse direction is RENDERER-LOCAL too. "Linked mentions (N)" is a collapsible block at the bottom of the note's own scroll, computed per render from the index snapshot the window already has through the ONE resolver (so alias-form links count) — no reverse map in main, no new IPC, no index field, no new watcher. Context snippets are read on demand for the rows actually shown. Full rules: Editor rule 25.
- **Folder pages** (YAZ-825/826/827 — decisions D1-D6 of YAZ-814, LOCKED): a page declares itself a folder page with frontmatter `folder_page: true`; a note names its parents in a `folder_pages` list. The whole model is frontmatter, and like backlinks the lookup is RENDERER-LOCAL: one pass over the index snapshot this window already holds, through the ONE resolver, memoized per records-array IDENTITY (`backlinksFor`'s WeakMap idiom) — no reverse map in main, no new IPC, no new index field, no cache-version bump, no new watcher; liveness IS the normal index refetch handing over a NEW array, which rebuilds (D2). THE CLICK RULE (D1/D3): an entry counts only when it is a string that is EXACTLY a wikilink after trim — the indexer's own frontmatter-link rule, a bare scalar tolerated as one entry exactly as `extractLinks` tolerates it — AND it resolves through that resolver (case-insensitive, alias-aware, `|alias` / `#heading` stripped, so every spelling a CLICK would follow counts and nothing else does) — AND the page it lands on carries the flag, which is STRICTLY the boolean `true` (D4: `"true"`, `1` and truthy objects are not it). Failing any leg counts as NOTHING, quietly: prose, bare names, non-strings, dangling links and links to ordinary pages just leave the note unparented, and a dangling or unflagged entry is a WAITING entry — the page shows in Uncategorized until its target both exists AND is flagged, at which point the next snapshot adopts it with no user action and no prompt. The lookup NEVER creates a file (create-on-click, rule 23, is an editor CLICK gesture — a frontmatter entry is not a click, so nothing is spawned for it), and two entries resolving to one page count ONCE. Uncategorized (D5) is every record with zero counting entries — folder pages and Home INCLUDED, because the lookup has no carve-outs; a surface subtracts whatever it already displays. Walking (D6, ⚡ amended 2026-08-25 — ruled by Yasin; the plain-words reasoning is saved verbatim as the 📖 comment on YAZ-814): the ONE shared primitive is **`guardedChildren(lookup, parent, ancestors)`** — the members of `parent` minus anyone already standing on the ancestor path. THE LAW: during a descent, children come ONLY from `guardedChildren`, never raw `pagesIn`, so forgetting the loop protection is structurally impossible instead of merely forbidden. EVERY surface is expansion-driven and recurses over `guardedChildren` ITSELF — the folder page's outline, and the Topics tree since YAZ-848 ("The Topics sidebar") — each with its own ordering and its own expansion. (TOMBSTONE, ⚡ YAZ-815: a `walkFolderPage` canonical LINEAR walk stood beside the primitive, itself built on it; nothing ever called it, and a second statement of the guard is a second place for it to drift — `guardedChildren` IS the whole law.) The guard is the ancestor PATH (the formula evaluator's `inProgress` idiom): a member already standing above the walk is skipped and that branch ends quietly, no depth cap and no throw, because belonging is text a note writes about itself and `A → B → A` is one keystroke away. Deliberately NOT a global visited set: a page reachable down two branches belongs under both and renders under each, while a page can never appear inside itself. Ripple into the folder page's own contents block (D1, shipped in YAZ-819 — see "The contents block"): its rows come from this lookup and NEVER from a raw `folder_pages.contains(link(…))` filter, which compares link targets as raw text (`expr/values.ts` — the list method's `equals` gets no resolver), so it is case-sensitive and alias-blind and would silently disagree with what clicking the same link does. Budget (D2, YAZ-827): the whole build for 1,000 pages under 25 ms — half the engine's — tripwired in the `perf` project, because every folder-page surface waits on this map and it rebuilds on every refetch. Naming (D6): files and functions carry the product's own words — folder page, pages in, uncategorized — and nothing is named "membership". Files: `client/src/links/folderPages.ts`, `client/src/links/folderPages.test.ts`, `client/src/links/folderPages.perf.test.ts`.
- The in-app `#/abs/path.md` hash (Editor rule 17) is separate and unchanged.

**YAZ-1289 rename amendment (supersedes the tombstone's final `api.tree` sentence):** the removed base-embed tree argument stays dead, and semantic Markdown targets still resolve only through index records. For a text/PDF/image file rename, App reuses a ready catalog only when it proves that exact target; a directory rename always obtains one fresh lightweight `api.tree(root)` snapshot so newly arrived descendants cannot be missed. A required snapshot failure stops before confirmation or mutation, the accepted root/generation is checked after the await, and the same pre-rename catalog is pinned through confirmation into count/rewrite. Switching roots cancels an old request silently and clears any open rename sheet. Explicit-extension links resolve through that catalog, while the referencing and written files remain Markdown records only. Post-move catalog resolution decides whether a bare duplicate spelling can remain bare or must escalate to a root-relative path. View-only target files are never read or written by link repair.

## The folder-page gestures (YAZ-840/841 — decisions D1-D5 of YAZ-817, LOCKED)

How a page BECOMES a folder page and how it stops being one. The model itself is "Links": Folder pages, the config is "Folder page settings"; this is the sidebar's half — two rows in the sidebar's ONE context menu (see the `sidebar/` entry under "Repo layout"), reached from the file tree's rows and, since YAZ-865's ⚡ amendment, from the Topics lens' page rows too ("The Topics sidebar") — and one confirm sheet.

**The toggle** (🔒 D1/D3, shaped by 🔒 D2): ONE state-aware row — "Turn into folder page" / "Turn back into normal page" — never two items, never a checkbox, on MARKDOWN FILE rows only (`fileKind(path) === 'markdown'`, the classifier the tree and index already use; folder rows and blank space cannot carry the flag). It sits BETWEEN the create group and Rename. The row has its OWN `MenuTargets` field (GRO-2296), and the DIRECTION TRAVELS WITH THE PATH into the click handler so the label and effect cannot diverge after the menu closes. FORWARD writes the flag and — only when the page has no `folder_page_settings` key yet — the default declaration, through `turnIntoFolderPage(content)` = `bornFolderPage(properties)` over the file's own frontmatter (YAZ-1513, ⚡ REVERSING 🔒 D1 on purpose — "The defaults" under "Folder page settings" has the rule and the tombstone): write-if-missing, so a page turned back and forward again keeps the settings it had and seeds nothing. TOMBSTONE (🔒 D1 of YAZ-841): FORWARD used to write exactly `folder_page: true` and NOTHING else. A flagged page whose settings list no usable views still renders Q7's `DEFAULT_VIEWS`. REVERSE (YAZ-1022) restores the first outline document to the Markdown body, removes that active `outline` value so a later forward migration cannot duplicate it, and removes `folder_page` in the SAME whole-file transform. Every other `folder_page_settings` value and every member note's own `folder_pages` entry stays untouched, so the visible content, columns, views, and memberships survive the round trip. Both paths share `transformFile`'s read → pure transform → `expectedMtime` write → one fresh-byte recomputation on conflict; `writeProperty` is its one-key forward specialization. A second conflict throws, and malformed reverse-owned YAML refuses the change rather than half-applying it. There is no new IPC, backend transaction, backup, or provenance record. Failures use the sidebar's passive notice and name the failed direction. **The label's state costs nothing**: the flag is read at MENU-OPEN time from the window's already-running index snapshot, pinned into menu state, with no second feed or fetch. Before the first index lands, a stale forward label can only produce the writer's existing no-op; the next right-click reflects the index.

**The confirm rule** (🔒 D5): forward NEVER confirms — it adds one key, and this very row undoes it. Reverse ALWAYS does, because it changes what a whole page MEANS to everything pointing at it. `ConfirmTurnBack` is our own sheet and never a native dialog, MIRRORING `ConfirmDelete`'s behaviour exactly — initial focus on CANCEL (a stray Enter arriving from the tree changes nothing), Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal` labelled by its own text — with two deliberate omissions: NO "Don't ask me again" (delete earns one because it repeats and is unrecoverable; this is neither, and a silent version of the one gesture that unparents a whole folder page is not worth the keystroke it saves) and the confirm button is NOT `--danger` (nothing is destroyed here — 🔒 D3 — and the copy says so; red would contradict its own text). A mirror, not a shared shell with `ConfirmDelete`: the two differ in confirm label, danger styling, labelling id AND confirm payload (delete reports its checkbox), and four knobs plus a checkbox slot is more shell than either sheet is worth. Buttons are Cancel / **Turn back**. The copy is LOCKED VERBATIM and comes from a pure, separately tested function — `deleteConfirmMessage`'s twin, so the component around it stays trivial:

> Turn 'a.md' back into a normal page? Pages that belong to it keep their entries — any that belong nowhere else will appear in Uncategorized until this is a folder page again. Nothing is deleted.

The page is named by `basename`, extension and all, the way the delete sheet names its target, so the two sheets in this folder speak with one voice.

**New folder page** (🔒 D4 + D1): the create group reads **New note · New folder page · New folder** (it read "New note · New folder page · New base · New folder" until YAZ-844 retired the format) — directly after the note it is a kind of, and it CREATES BESIDE the right-clicked row like the rest of that group, which is what keeps it from drifting down into the act-on-this-row toggle. It is a THIRD `EntryKind` (`folderPage`, beside `file` and `dir` — it was the fourth until YAZ-844 deleted `base`) riding the SHARED inline-create flow rather than a flag beside `file`: same `validateEntryName`, same `targetDirFor` placement (file row → its folder, folder row → inside it, blank space → the vault root), same `.md` extension rule, same open-after-create, same inline error, the input's only difference being its placeholder. "Same" is proven, not asserted: the test captures New note's OWN error text for a bad name and requires the folder page's to be that exact string. Birth is ATOMIC content-at-create — one `createNewNote(path, newFolderPageProperties())` → `createFile({ path, content })` (bridge table above), so the file is born with what every folder page is born with and nothing else — the flag plus the default `status` declaration (`bornFolderPage({})`, YAZ-1513 — "The defaults" under "Folder page settings"), no body, no `folder_pages` — and there is no create-then-write race; the key is again `FOLDER_PAGE_KEY`, one source of truth with the toggle and `isFolderPage`, and the settings key string stays `folderPageSettings.ts`'s own (🔒 Q1) because the builder lives there. TOMBSTONE (🔒 D1 of YAZ-841, reversed on purpose by YAZ-1513): the file used to be born `---\nfolder_page: true\n---\n` — the flag and NOTHING else, no settings block. Not to be confused with "Birth from a folder page" ("Folder page settings" Q5/Q6), which is the other direction: that gesture makes an ORDINARY page that BELONGS to F and never writes the flag, this one writes the flag and never writes `folder_pages`. The ONE place they meet is a FLAGGED folder-page row in the Topics lens (⚡ 8H, YAZ-869): "New note" there IS the birth-from-a-declaration gesture, and "New folder page" there writes BOTH keys — the flag, because it is a folder page, and the one `folder_pages` entry that nests it under the topic it was made from. Everywhere else — every Files row, every leaf Topics row, blank space — both items are exactly as described above. See "The Topics sidebar": The row menu.

**A second entry point** (🔒 D2, ⚡ amended by YAZ-888/YAZ-883): D2 anticipated a doc title row whose ⋯ menu would take the toggle as a second way in. The title row LANDED (⚡ YAZ-888) and so did the properties panel beneath it (⚡ YAZ-883), and NEITHER carries a ⋯ menu — the title is an inline rename, and the panel is typed rows over a raw-YAML fallback (⚡ YAZ-884) whose own affordances are already per-key, with no room for a page-wide second one. So the toggle's one entry point remains the sidebar's context menu, exactly as it is. What survives of D2 is its RULE, not its host: whenever a second entry point does land it calls 4A's same handler and nobody reimplements the gesture. Until then there is no ⋯ menu to plan around.

Files: `client/src/sidebar/ContextMenu.tsx`, `client/src/sidebar/Sidebar.tsx` (the menu targets, `setFolderPageFlag` / `toggleFolderPage`, the `folderPage` create branch), `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmTurnBack.tsx` (`turnBackConfirmMessage` + the sheet), `client/src/sidebar/createEntry.ts` + `CreateInline.tsx` (the THIRD `EntryKind` — `base` was deleted with the format in YAZ-844), with `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmTurnBack.test.tsx`, `client/src/sidebar/ContextMenu.test.tsx` and `client/src/sidebar/Sidebar.test.tsx`; the flag key is `FOLDER_PAGE_KEY` in `client/src/links/folderPages.ts`.

## The contents block (YAZ-819/820 — decisions D1-D4 of YAZ-818, LOCKED)

What a folder page SHOWS. The model is "Links": Folder pages (who belongs), the config is "Folder page settings" (how it looks), the sidebar's half is "The folder-page gestures" (how a page becomes one); this is the surface — the members rendered under the page's own body in today's fully interactive views, the outline first among them. It is the ONLY place `ViewsPane` is mounted in the app since the `.base` retirement (YAZ-844, Editor rule 19's tombstone), so everything the views section describes now describes this block — and since YAZ-846 the component says so: `folderPage` is a REQUIRED prop and there is no other mode left in the file.

**Placement** (🔒 D1): between the Crepe mount and the comment stream (the backlinks, until YAZ-1472 slid its block in between) inside the note's own scroller — `.page-title` (block ZERO since ⚡ YAZ-888), `.frontmatter-panel` (block ONE since ⚡ YAZ-883), `.editor-mount` (the Crepe host), then `.folder-page-contents`, then `.comments` (the comment stream, YAZ-1472 — see "Comments"), then "Linked mentions" (Editor rule 25) — so it scrolls WITH the note exactly like backlinks, never a panel and never a second pane. It repeats `.editor-instance`'s content column byte for byte (backlinks.css's rule) and gives `.views-pane` its whole geometry — since YAZ-861 the pane declares NO column and no fill-the-window height of its own to neutralise, the one mount being this block, so the table grows and the NOTE scrolls rather than a second vertical scroller opening inside the page; `.view-table-wrap` keeps its own `overflow: auto` from views.css on purpose, so a table wider than the note's column scrolls SIDEWAYS in place instead of dragging the whole page with it. One hairline is the entire chrome: no chip and no title row of its own — the page's NAME is block zero (⚡ YAZ-888) and its frontmatter is block one (⚡ YAZ-883), both carried by every page, and a folder page adds nothing to either. A page that does not carry the strict `folder_page: true` flag renders NOTHING here at all (`isFolderPage`, 🔒 D4 of YAZ-814), and so does one whose window has no index snapshot yet — quiet, never a placeholder.

**Rows** (🔒 D2): `folderPagesLookup(records, resolve).pagesIn(path)` — the members, and nobody else. NEVER a `folder_pages.contains(link(…))` filter, which compares link targets as raw text (case-sensitive, alias-blind) and would silently disagree with what clicking the same link does ("Links": Folder pages, D1). The rows are a SUBSET of the vault, which is the trap this decision closes: the engine RUNS over the members but RESOLVES over the whole vault, through `RunOptions.resolve` (YAZ-819's one engine addition, see "Engine") fed `resolverFor(folderPage.vaultRecords, root)` — WITH the root since YAZ-846, which is what makes it the very resolver instance the wikilink surfaces hold — so a link cell pointing at a page that is not a member still resolves and still renders as a link instead of dead text, pinned by its own test. The link PICKERS get the same whole-vault snapshot (`FolderPageMode.vaultRecords`) for the same reason. The feed is the window's ONE `WikilinkResolveSource` — App-owned, fed by `WikilinkIndexBridge`, the same snapshot the wikilink decorations, the `[[` picker and backlinks read — so this block can never disagree with the links above it and costs no fetch, no watcher and no IPC of its own; a re-subscribe re-reads the whole feed, and an unchanged snapshot keeps the previous object, so index churn elsewhere in the vault costs no render (BacklinksSection's idiom). The snapshot is always already in hand here, which is why `ViewsPane` has no loading or error state left to show: YAZ-846 amputated `indexStatus` / `indexError` along with every other prop this host does not pass.

**The adapter** (🔒 D3): `ViewsPane` stays ONE component and learns nothing about folder pages beyond one prop bundle (`FolderPageMode`: the settings, the vault snapshot, the create function, the background-open) — REQUIRED since YAZ-846, because there is no second caller to make it optional for. The host builds the def IN MEMORY from `folderPageSettings(record).views` — with the settings' `formulas` beside them since YAZ-745, since a `formula.<name>` level is answered off `def.formulas` and nowhere else — and hands it over as a `ParsedViews` — round-tripped through the ONE parser (`folderPageViewSet`: `parseViews(stringify({ formulas, views, defaultView }))`, `stringify` skipping the undefined keys), never a hand-built object, because a `ParsedViews` carries the yaml `Document` that `updateViews` writes every config edit into and a hand-made shell would take the first `doc.setIn` down with it. A `views` block that will not parse at all falls back to `DEFAULT_VIEWS` (report-don't-block: a hand-edited card can never take the note's editor with it). No SET-LEVEL `filters` are ever put in the def (🔒 Q3, "Folder page settings") — the views inside it carry their own `views[i].filters` since YAZ-1218. Every config change — sort, group-by, column set and widths, row height, summaries, the outline's `order` — arrives as `onChange(parseViews(serializeViews(updateViews(…))))` and leaves as EXACTLY ONE `folder_page_settings` write through the one door (`writeFolderPageSettings` → `writeProperty` → the page's own open editor absorbs the frontmatter-only `change` silently, GRO-2186); a failed write shows `Could not save the folder page's settings: <message>` above the block and never a dialog. Our own write returns through the index, so the def is rebuilt only when the CARD's views actually moved: a JSON stamp of `settings.views`, `settings.formulas` AND `settings.defaultView` — every half of what `folderPageViewSet` builds, so an edited formula, and since YAZ-1471 an externally edited `defaultView`, rebuilds exactly as an edited view does — is held against the one the live def was built from and an unchanged stamp rebuilds nothing — the write-echo guard, JSON identity being the honest comparison because every settings read hands back a fresh copy. All three stamps (the index `stamp`, the open file's `fileStamp`, and the `pending` echo a local write pushes) are spelled by ONE `stampOf` helper over that same tuple, because two stamps that were not comparable byte for byte would make the catch-up read every write as an external edit. CELL edits are untouched by all of this: `EditableCell` writes the MEMBER's own card through `writeProperty`, exactly as it always has (5B) — the settings door is for the folder page's own configuration and nothing else. **New** births through the DECLARATION rather than the filter seed (🔒 Q5/Q6): `FolderPageMode.create` runs `newPageFromFolderPage` (scaffold ← template ← seed, `folder_pages: ["[[F]]"]` last) and parks the page through `memberFolder` — the settings' `folder`, created level by level through `ensureFolder`, or beside the folder page itself when there is none — which is the SAME pair the Topics row menu's "New note" runs since ⚡ YAZ-869, one rule and never two copies; the 5D seed still rides along, so a group "+" seeds its group here too, and the name is always the free `Untitled` (the outline's create row, the one caller that ever typed one, died in YAZ-903 — and `create`'s `name` argument with it). Which view is ACTIVE is SESSION state (`ViewsPane`'s own `active`) and never reaches the card — SWITCHING writes nothing, ever; the STARTING view is the one thing that persists (YAZ-1104): `folder_page_settings.defaultView` names the view a fresh open lands on (absent or stale → the first view, silently), written ONLY by the explicit `Page → Default view` gesture at the bottom of the properties menu, `First view` clearing the key. ⚡ AMENDED by 🔒 D4 (YAZ-1471) — the BEHAVIOUR above is unchanged, only its "through its OWN door" wording is superseded: `defaultView` now rides IN the def (`ViewSet.defaultView?: string`, built in by `folderPageViewSet` and stamped with the rest), so the select writes through the ordinary `onUpdate` `Mutate` like every other config edit and `onChange` carries `defaultView: next.def.defaultView` into the same single write. `FolderPageMode.setDefaultView` — the second door — is DELETED: one door means a rename can carry the saved START and a delete can clear it IN THE SAME WRITE that moves the views, which two doors could only have done as two writes racing each other. For the same reason `FolderPageMode.setColumns` writes the LIVE def's `views` and `defaultView` (`parsed.def`), never the index SNAPSHOT it also holds — the snapshot can be behind by an unechoed local write, and reading it would let a column declaration clobber an in-flight default-view choice or sort edit (YAZ-1234's two-gestures data loss, closed again here). **View CRUD is this block's gesture** since 🔒 D0 (YAZ-1471) re-ruled 🔒 rule 4 (YAZ-819) and the "view CRUD is not this block's gesture" clause of 🔒 D3 (YAZ-818): the tabs reorder, rename, duplicate, delete and add ("View chrome": Toolbar) — each ONE `update` through this same one door, and none of them touching which view is active. It was SWITCH-ONLY (`ViewTabs readOnly`: no "+", no rename / duplicate / delete / move) from YAZ-846 until YAZ-1471. The Filter button is offered on every rows-bearing view since YAZ-1218 (YAZ-1226-1229; the outline, a document, has none) — it edits `views[i].filters` ONLY: the set IS the lookup, so there is still no set-level filter to offer (🔒 Q3, amended). Collapsed groups persist as they always did, in the main-owned `baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]` bucket — keyed by the folder page's own `.md` path now that there is no `.base` file to key on — never in the page's own frontmatter (4C). That bucket is keyed by view NAME, and since YAZ-1471 a name can change (and a view can go) in one gesture, so `ViewsPane.moveCollapsed` moves with it (YAZ-1493): a RENAME carries the entry from the old key to the new one and a DELETE prunes it, in both the live state and the store — otherwise the renamed view would spring open, the dead key would leak, and a view later given that same name would inherit a stranger's collapsed groups. A session-only page (`thisFile === null`) keys by index and needs no carrying, and a rootless window carries the live state only. the folder-page e2e drives the whole arc end to end (a `groupBy` set through the Sort menu is ONE settings write, a collapsed section survives quit → relaunch on disk), which is where YAZ-744's grouped-table coverage lives since the format retired. **The key inside that bucket is itself a persisted contract**: `groupKeyOf` — `∅` for the trailing "No value" group, `v:<render(key)>` otherwise (the prefix keeps a rendered value from colliding with the sentinel) — and for an INNER group (YAZ-745) `nestedGroupKeyOf`, its outer's key and its own joined by the unit separator U+001F, so the same inner value under two outers collapses independently. Because both go to disk the encoding cannot be quietly changed, and the separator is spelled as an ESCAPE (`\u001f`) everywhere it is asserted, never as a raw byte in a source file. YAZ-1102's `desktop/e2e/nestedGroups.spec.ts` drives the two-level arc over its own `fixtures/nested-vault` — the two shapes the real vault has, a FORMULA outer level climbing function pages to their top (`parent` when there is one, the function itself when the key is present-but-empty) and two PLAIN columns on a table wide enough to scroll: both levels rendering with the merge rule visible, an outer collapse folding a whole branch while an inner collapse survives underneath it as those two key kinds across quit → relaunch, the per-level sticky label at the table's right edge, and a single-level `groupBy` still flat. YAZ-1177 extends that same fixture and journey to Board: direct merged cards precede the stacked child sections, each collapse scope is independent, a child `+` seeds both levels, and a cross-outer child drag survives a durable reread.

**The outline** (YAZ-820, REWRITTEN by YAZ-867's arc — YAZ-900/901/902/903): the folder page's first default skin (`DEFAULT_VIEWS` = `outline`, then `table`) and the one view type that only exists here. (It used to fall back to the 4A placeholder row list without a folder page behind it — an outline of WHAT? — and since YAZ-846 made `folderPage` required there is no such mount left to fall back FROM; the guard that survives is on `thisFile`, and a null one still keeps the placeholder rows.) **The outline IS a document** (🔒 D2, YAZ-900): ONE free-form markdown bullet list, held whole as the string `views[i].outline` inside the folder page's own `folder_page_settings` — not a list of members with an order beside it. `views/outlineDoc.ts` owns that string's grammar and nothing else reads it as markdown: depth is RELATIVE indentation (the outliner's own `unifySiblingMarkers` rule — a wider indent is one level down however wide, tabs count as four spaces, `-` / `*` / `+` are one bullet), serialising picks ONE spelling (`- ` at four spaces per level) so a document this app writes is byte-stable through a round trip, and a rename walks INTO it through `mapOutlineLinks`, whose leaf is a LINE that is exactly a wikilink and never a link sitting inside prose ("Links": Rename). The user types into it: `OutlineEditor` (YAZ-901, 🔒 F3) is a SECOND, small Milkdown instance — the note editor's own `createCrepe()`, its outliner plugins (bullet glyphs, Tab / Shift-Tab, guide lines, folding) and its wikilink surfaces (live-preview decorations, the `[[` picker, click navigation), locked by `editor/outline/bulletsOnly.ts` to a document that is exactly one bullet list. Not one plugin is copied: the reuse IS the feature. It is SEEDED ONCE, at mount, through that same grammar (no bullets means one empty bullet, so there is something to click into) — each line's text ARMOURED on the way in by the grammar's own `escapeBlockStart` (YAZ-964/973: text that merely LOOKS like a block — `1. Title`, `# x`, a footnote definition — stays the literal text the grammar promises instead of re-parsing into a node the lock silently drops; `escapeOutlineMarkdown` hands the same one rule to the paste door, `editor/outlinePaste.ts`, so no door re-derives it) and GUARDED after the create (YAZ-974): fewer bullets back than went in means Milkdown could not hold the seed, so the editor goes read-only and reports once through `onSeedLoss` — the loss is shown, never written back as the truth — — and since ⚡ YAZ-1356 a LATER `markdown` is a DIFF over the live editor (`applyExternalMarkdown`, YAZ-1347's own path, so folds, caret and scroll ride ProseMirror's position mapping; the seed's grammar and the YAZ-974 guard run at this door too): the disk moving under an open folder page shows in place, an append from sync-from-folder or adoption shows without a remount (YAZ-954's re-seed is retired), and TYPING WINS — a live document the caller has not been told about yet skips the snapshot, judged in the save serialisation the listener reports (`getMarkdownForSave`, never raw `getMarkdown()`), and the apply's own emission is that same known string, so it is not an edit and is never written back. `OutlineView` adopts an external document as `prev` (render-phase, the panel's idiom) and RECONCILE re-asks on the document as well as the snapshot, because a snapshot lands as two renders. Its `onChange` is the note editor's save idiom minus the disk: Crepe's ~200ms `markdownUpdated` debounce plus the 500ms `useAutosave` uses, flushed on unmount so switching views never drops the last keystroke, with the caller owning the write. Its wikilink surfaces are fed the window's ONE feed through `FolderPageMode` — `wikilinks`, `wikilinkCandidates` and the `nav` contract `Editor` assembles for the note itself, threaded App → `Editor` → `FolderPageContents` → `ViewsPane`, and `nav` is MEMOISED there because a new identity remounts the editor and a remount costs the caret and the undo history.

**The link line IS the belonging** (🔒 E1, YAZ-902), which is the click rule and no second rule: a LINE whose text trims to EXACTLY one resolving wikilink means that page belongs to this folder page. Prose, a link inside prose, a bare name and a dangling link are TEXT and mean nothing — the outline is free-form, and unsaid is not unmeant; two lines naming one page (however they spell it) are ONE belonging, and depth means nothing to belonging at all. Every belonging write still lands on the MEMBER's own `folder_pages`, one key on one page through the shared `writeProperty` (🔒 child-declared, YAZ-825) — never on the folder page, never inside the document itself. **Auto-tag, sheet-gated un-tag**: each committed edit diffs the LAST-WRITTEN document against the new one (`diffOutlineBelonging`, the view holding `prev` — nothing re-reads disk for it, because a diff against the wrong string would tag and un-tag the wrong pages). A page the document now names is TAGGED at once, idempotently through the RESOLVER (a page already belonging — spelled through an alias, a different case, an absolute path — is not written at all), except this folder page itself, which can never become its own member: the one exclusion the retired add row also made. A page the document has STOPPED naming is only ASKED, through `ConfirmRemoveMember`, the very sheet the × has opened since YAZ-820; several dropped in ONE edit are asked one sheet at a time, in document order, rather than merged into a single dialog — the copy is locked verbatim and names ONE page. **Cancel KEEPS the belonging** (🔒 ruled on YAZ-903) and does NOT put the text back itself — adoption writes the line back at the END of the document on the very next pass (YAZ-1152 superseding the second half of that ruling: the page used to show up in the read-only appended section instead). Either way `prev` advances to the new text, so a question answered once is never asked again on the next debounce. Failures land in `Could not update the page's folder pages: <message>` above the block, passive as ever.

**Adoption** (🔒 YAZ-1152) is the "tagged elsewhere still shows" rule, kept by the DOCUMENT itself: a member whose page the CURRENT document does not NAME is WRITTEN INTO it — depth-0 `[[link]]` lines appended at the END, alphabetical by basename through the same collator, each spelled by `linkNames` (the shortest spelling that resolves BACK to that member, shared with sync-from-folder: one note, one spelling) — through `OutlineView`'s `commit`, the one door, so ONE `folder_page_settings` write carries the lot and the belonging pass tags nothing that already belongs. It is where a page tagged from somewhere else lands, and where a member arriving on the next snapshot lands. It is IDEMPOTENT: the todo is recomputed inside the effect against the document as of the LAST commit — a ref advanced synchronously, never a render's stale copy — which is what makes React's double-invoked effects adopt exactly once. FOUR GUARDS hold it back, and together they are the whole of the "the document is never rewritten under the user" promise: a remove sheet still standing, an un-tag still IN FLIGHT (the index has not echoed the removal, so the page is still a member — a FAILED un-tag drops the hold at once and the line comes straight back), the editor holding the CARET (a rewrite would cost it; the block's own `onBlur` wakes the held pass), and a LOSSY seed (YAZ-974: read-only means read-only). Cancelling a remove sheet therefore RESTORES the line, by construction rather than by putting the text back. **`order` lazy-migrates**: a folder page with no `outline` yet is seeded with its [D5] `order` frozen into a document (`fromOrder`: the entries verbatim, stale ones included — the outline is free text and nothing judges them — then every unlisted member alphabetically, the arrangement `orderedMembers` produced, live). The first edit writes `views[i].outline` AND DELETES `views[i].order` in the SAME single `folder_page_settings` write, through the very door the retired drag wrote `order` through, landing on the FIRST outline view because that is the one the seed was read from. After that the [D5] list is read here by nobody. **Reconcile** (YAZ-1357): the commit path's diff is doc-vs-doc, so a line that starts RESOLVING with no edit — the page created by clicking that very link, by AI, by Obsidian, by a rename — never tagged. Every snapshot now re-asks the link rule against the current document and TAGS (only tags — an un-tag stays behind the sheet) what it names and the lookup does not. Both tag paths go through one door that HOLDS a written page until the index echoes its card, so a snapshot arriving between the write and the echo never writes it twice; a failed write drops the hold at once.

TOMBSTONE (YAZ-903, superseding 🔒 D4 of YAZ-818 — "rows are pages, and **Enter never commits free text**"): the outline used to be a derived list of member ROWS in `orderedMembers` order, never free text and never a bullet the user typed, with a PICKER-ONLY add row at the bottom (`OutlineAddRow`: "+ Link a page…", the shared `matchLinkCandidates` ranking, Enter picking the first real page or doing nothing at all, and one explicit `+ Create '<name>' here` row that had to be clicked), a depth-0 drag that rewrote `order` as `[[wikilinks]]`, and NESTED AUTO-EXPANSION — chevrons descending through `guardedChildren`'s ancestor-path guard, each level ordered by its own folder page's settings. All of it is gone, deliberately: the outline is TEXT now, and what is belonging is the link line. Nested auto-expansion is DROPPED with it (the locked scoping decision) — a folder-page link INSIDE the editor is a plain wikilink whose click navigates, with no decoration widget of its own for the glyph or the count, both of which the appended section wore until YAZ-1152 retired it, and only the Topics tree wears now. Birth from the outline is the toolbar's New and whatever create affordance the `[[` picker itself offers; "+ Create 'X' here" died with the row that offered it, and `FolderPageMode.create`'s `name` argument with it. The × died with the appended row it hovered over (YAZ-1152) — a link line dropped from the document is what asks now — but its sheet survives untouched: `ConfirmRemoveMember` — our own sheet, never a native dialog, MIRRORING `ConfirmTurnBack` (which mirrors `ConfirmDelete`) rather than sharing a shell: initial focus on CANCEL so a stray Enter changes nothing, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal` labelled by its own text, with the same two omissions — no "Don't ask me again", and the confirm button is NOT `--danger`, because nothing is destroyed here and the copy says so. Buttons are Cancel / **Remove**. Pages are named by `basename`, no extension — the spelling the rows and the `[[…]]` entry itself carry (the sidebar's two sheets name FILES, extension and all; this one names PAGES). The copy is LOCKED VERBATIM and comes from a pure, separately tested function (`removeMemberMessage`), in two forms — with other folder pages left:

> Remove 'Win Rate' from 'KPIs'? The page is not deleted — its file stays put. It remains in: Funnel Stages, Metrics.

and when this was its last one:

> Remove 'Win Rate' from 'KPIs'? The page is not deleted — its file stays put. It has no other folder pages, so it moves to Uncategorized.

Two engine-facing consequences of an outline view, both in `ViewsPane` and nowhere else: its `order` is the [D5] MEMBER sequence and NOT a column list, so it is STRIPPED before the run — left in, `propertyKeys` would hand those wikilinks to the value pass, every row's `values` would come back empty and the toolbar's search (which matches over exactly those values) would hide the whole outline; everything else the view says (sort, limit, `groupBy`) still runs. The strip OUTLIVES the [D5] list it was written for (YAZ-903 retires `order` on the first edit): an un-migrated card still carries one. The DOCUMENT needs no strip of its own — `propertyKeys` reads `view.order` and nothing else, so `view.outline`, being a string, cannot reach the value pass however long it grows. And the Properties menu is not offered while an outline is showing (`noProperties`), because every gesture in it rewrites `view.order` and one click would silently overwrite the locked ordering with property keys.

**Typing** (🔒 Q8, "Folder page settings"): rungs 1 and 2 of the editor ladder are WIRED here. Rung 1 is the folder page's OWN `folder_page_settings.columns`, view-scoped (`columnKindIn`, YAZ-819). Rung 2 is the vault-wide declarations in `.yaseendocs/properties.json` (YAZ-846): App owns ONE `useProperties(root)` exactly as it owns the ONE `WikilinkResolveSource` — one fetch per window, threaded App → `Editor` → `FolderPageContents` → `ViewsPane` → `TableView` / `CardsView` / `ListView`, never a `useProperties` per surface — and it carries the link/multi-link `target` that narrows the picker. A corrupt `properties.json` is not a failure here: it arrives as a `properties.error`, renders its own passive line above the rows, and the column simply falls through. There is no rung below those two: a key nobody declares falls from rung 2 straight to the note's own YAML value and the dominant-kind inference. TOMBSTONE (⚡ YAZ-815, ruled by Yasin): a RUNG 3 for `.obsidian/types.json` sat there, permanently UNWIRED because its data rode `IndexResponse` and this surface reads the window's link snapshot rather than fetching an index of its own (🔒 D2). A rung with no feed is not a rung, and a foreign app's file is not our schema — the rung, `columnTyping`'s `types` parameter and the read behind them are all gone.

**Sync from folder** (YAZ-950): the toolbar's one extra button, on the DOCUMENT skin only — a disk folder's notes appended to this page as `[[links]]`. It writes ORDINARY membership and invents no second kind: approving appends depth-0 link lines through `OutlineView`'s `commit`, the outline's one door, so the belonging pass already there writes each newly-linked note's own `folder_pages` entry exactly as typing the link by hand would (🔒 YAZ-825 stands). One-way and additive — notes in the folder the page does not list are offered; pages listed that are NOT in the folder are never touched, which is the point of topics being independent of disk folders. The folder is asked EVERY time and never remembered (Yasin's ruling), so one page can pull from several; `folder_page_settings.folder` is untouched and keeps its own meaning. The answer stops at the folder's OWN notes (never its subtree), skips non-markdown and the page itself, judges already-listed by where a line RESOLVES (`outlineLinkTargets`, so an alias or `folder/name` spelling counts), and appends the SHORTEST unambiguous name (`linkNames`, shared with adoption — one note, one spelling). Nothing happens in the background and nothing reconciles the FOLDER: a continuous disk pass would re-add whatever a remove sheet just took out. (Adoption reconciles MEMBERS into the document, YAZ-1152 — a different fact, held back by that very sheet, and it never reaches into a disk folder.) Only the NEW lines are serialized — a whole-document round trip would re-spell the user's markers and drop their prose — and the editor RE-SEEDS on this write, since it is seeded once at mount and would otherwise leave the append on disk unseen.

Files: `client/src/views/FolderPageContents.tsx` + `folderPageContents.css` (+ `FolderPageContents.test.tsx`), `client/src/views/ViewsPane.tsx` (`FolderPageMode`, the outline routing, the order-stripped run), `client/src/App.tsx` + `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (the window's ONE `useProperties(root)` and the thread down to the block — typing rung 2), `client/src/views/view/OutlineView.tsx`, `client/src/views/view/OutlineEditor.tsx` + `outlineEditor.css` (+ `OutlineEditor.test.tsx`), `client/src/editor/outline/bulletsOnly.ts` (the lock, + its test), `client/src/views/outlineDoc.ts` and `client/src/views/outlineSync.ts` (+ their tests), `client/src/views/folderSync.ts` and `client/src/views/view/SyncFromFolder.tsx` (+ their tests, YAZ-950), `client/src/views/view/ConfirmRemoveMember.tsx` (`removeMemberMessage` + the sheet) with `client/src/views/view/OutlineView.test.tsx` (which stubs the editor — a real Crepe in jsdom is `OutlineEditor.test.tsx`'s job, and `FolderPageContents.test.tsx` / `Editor.test.tsx` stub it for the same reason), `client/src/views/view/icons.tsx` (`FolderPageGlyph`, which moved out of `sidebar/Tree.tsx` when YAZ-844 left the folder page its only wearer), `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (the mount, between the Crepe host and the comment stream), `desktop/e2e/folderPages.spec.ts`, `desktop/e2e/folderSync.spec.ts`.

## The page title + rename confirm (YAZ-888 — LOCKED, ⚡ amending decision E of GRO-2096)

What every page shows above its body, and the one beat a rename now earns. The model is "Links": Rename (the pipeline, unchanged); this is the surface that names a page and the rule that guards renaming it.

**The title IS the file name** (🔒): `stripExt(basename(path))`, never a frontmatter `title` — the whole link system resolves pages by NAME, and aliases already cover alternate display names (E2, GRO-2214). It is BLOCK ZERO of the note's own scroller (`.page-title`, above the properties panel and `.editor-mount`), so it scrolls with the note like every other block there, inside `.editor-instance`'s content column at the editor's own h1 scale. React-side chrome and NEVER a ProseMirror node: the note's markdown round-trips past it byte-identically, and an in-document `# Heading` stays a block of the note like any other — syncing the two is deliberately out of scope, as is a `title` property.

**ONE DOOR** (🔒): every rename gesture in the app — the sidebar's inline rename, its drag-move, and now the title — reaches `App` as `(oldPath, newPath)`, and the rule is asked there and nowhere else (`requestRename` in front of `renameFile`, which stays the executor). THE RULE: a changed NAME asks first (`isNameChange` = the basenames differ), a MOVE runs silently exactly as it always has — a confirm on every drag would be hostile, and bare links keep resolving across a move anyway. Files and FOLDERS alike: a folder's name is a name. No surface reimplements this, which is why the sidebar needed no change to inherit it.

**The copy** (🔒 LOCKED verbatim, `renameConfirmMessage`, pure and separately tested like `deleteConfirmMessage` / `turnBackConfirmMessage`):

> Rename 'Old Note' to 'New Note'? Links in 3 notes will be updated.

singular at 1, and for a page nobody links to:

> Rename 'Old Note' to 'New Note'? No other notes link to it.

Names are PAGE names (basename minus extension) — the title's own spelling, not the file's. N is `countLinkReferences` over the window's OWN index snapshot: synchronous, no fetch, so the sheet opens in the same frame as the gesture, and it counts the very set the rewrite walks (settings-only references included, YAZ-864). Kind is asked of that same snapshot — a markdown file IS a record, a folder never is. The rewrite re-reads a FRESH snapshot inside `renameFile`, so the copy promises what WILL be updated rather than an exact receipt. NO Obsidian-style 3-way: links ALWAYS update on confirm.

**The sheet** (`ConfirmRename`) is a MIRROR of `ConfirmTurnBack` — see that file for why these sheets are copied rather than shelled: our own sheet and never a native dialog, initial focus on CANCEL, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, `dialog` + `aria-modal` labelled by its own text, Cancel / **Rename**, NOT `--danger` (nothing is destroyed), and no "Don't ask me again" (the gesture that silently rewrites N notes is exactly the one that earns a beat). It is rendered by App, not the sidebar, because the door is App's. ONE deliberate departure, load-bearing: the keys are bound to the OVERLAY, not to `window`. This is the first sheet a KEYSTROKE can open, and React flushes its effects inside that very keydown's dispatch — a window listener hears the Enter that opened the sheet and confirms before the sheet is read. The other half of that same hazard lives in the inputs: an Enter that commits MUST `preventDefault`, or the keystroke's default activation lands on the freshly focused Cancel button and cancels what it just asked for (both `RenameInline` and the title input do; the e2e caught this).

**The Home guard** (🔒): the page that answers `[[Home]]` — through the window's own resolver, never a path check (🔒 D1, YAZ-821), so an aliased or nested Home is still Home — renders a PLAIN, non-editable title. Clicking it says, in the app's standing passive notice and never a dialog: `Home anchors this vault — it keeps its name.` `[[Home]]` is the vault's hard-coded front door (`ensureHome.ts`), so renaming the page behind it would simply spawn a fresh empty Home beside it. Repointing Home is future work; the SIDEBAR's Rename is not blocked (one guard, on the surface that invites the edit).

**Editing** copies `RenameInline`'s patterns, because a title edit IS a rename: click swaps the heading for an input prefilled with the current name, and LEAVING the field commits (YAZ-1553: click-away, Enter, ArrowDown and Cmd-Tab all reach the one `onBlur` door → `renamedPath(path, name, 'file')` → App's door; a `settled` ref makes Chromium's last blur on removal inert), Esc is the only discard, ArrowDown then hands focus to the editor below (`focusEditor`, the same call the mount ends with). The sidebar's `RenameInline` follows the same three verbs (open/leave/discard). An empty/whitespace name never commits and neither does the unchanged one; a name `validateEntryName` rejects lands in the passive notice, exactly where `ALREADY_EXISTS` and every other failure already land — the title never grows an error slot of its own.

**Known + accepted (v1)**: committing a rename REMOUNTS the editor (`CrepeHost key={file.path}`); the dirty buffer survives through `takeRenameBuffer` (Rename, step 4), and the scroll position and caret reset. Do not "fix" this by keeping the mount alive across the path change.

Files: `client/src/editor/PageTitle.tsx` (+ `PageTitle.test.tsx`), `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmRename.tsx` (`renameConfirmMessage`, `isNameChange`, `pageName`, the sheet) + `ConfirmRename.test.tsx`, `client/src/App.tsx` (`requestRename` / `confirmRename` in front of `renameFile`, the sheet's one render site) + `App.test.tsx`, `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (block zero, the Home fact off the resolve source, ArrowDown), `client/src/sidebar/RenameInline.tsx` (the Enter `preventDefault`), `client/src/app.css` (`.page-title*`), `desktop/e2e/title.spec.ts`.

## The properties panel (YAZ-883, typed rows YAZ-884 — LOCKED)

What every page shows BETWEEN its title and its body: its own frontmatter. The model is the frontmatter primitives (`shared/frontmatter.ts`, Editor rule 1) and the write dance is `writeProperty`'s ("Cell editing"); this is the surface.

**TWO MODES, one surface** (🔒 ⚡ YAZ-884). TYPED ROWS are the DEFAULT expanded view: one row per top-level frontmatter key, each with the editor its type asks for. RAW YAML (⚡ YAZ-883) is the FALLBACK underneath them, reached by the panel's one mode control — "Edit as YAML" / "Edit as rows" — and it stays honest by staying literal. Every rule below headed VERBATIM / VALIDATE-BLOCKS-SAVE / *Editing* is the raw mode's; the typed rows' own rules follow them.

**Placement**: BLOCK ONE of the note's own scroller (`.frontmatter-panel`, between `.page-title` and `.editor-mount`), so it scrolls WITH the note like every other block there, in `.editor-instance`'s content column. React-side chrome and never a ProseMirror node, exactly like the title above it.

**VERBATIM** (🔒): a save writes the user's own bytes back through `replaceFrontmatter` — NEVER a parse→reformat. Comments, key order, quoting styles, blank lines and indentation all survive a round trip that only meant to change one value; the FRAME is the file's (opening fence, `---` vs `...` terminator, the line endings around them) and the body is untouched. One trailing newline is supplied when the text lacks it and never a second when it has one, so an unchanged interior round-trips to the identical string and the write is skipped — identity never touches disk, the same rule as the editor's autosave and `writeProperty`.

**VALIDATE-BLOCKS-SAVE** (🔒): what the panel DOES enforce is that the block still parses. `parseFrontmatter` runs over the candidate CONTENT (fences and all, never a hand-wrapped copy) and an `error` blocks the write outright, because a block that will not parse would corrupt every index that reads it (`shared/frontmatter.ts`'s header) and the user would find out through a page that silently lost its properties. Valid-yet-weird YAML saves untouched — the rule is "it parses", not "we like it".

**The write**: read the file FRESH (`api.readFile`), `replaceFrontmatter`, write with `expectedMtime`, and on `CONFLICT` re-read once and retry — the user's YAML wins over a concurrent edit, whose BODY is preserved because only the block is rewritten; a second conflict throws. The page's own open editor absorbs the resulting frontmatter-only `change` silently (`absorbFrontmatterOnly`, GRO-2186), so the panel talks to the editor not at all.

**Its own error surface** (🔒): bad YAML, a `FrontmatterWriteError`, a failed key write, a failed declaration and a failed bridge call all land on ONE inline line inside the body (`Not valid YAML: …` / `Could not save the properties: …` / `Could not delete "x": …` / `Could not set the type of "x": …`), never a dialog and never App's passive notice — that notice is for gestures with no place of their own to speak, and this one has one. A rejected save reverts nothing: the text stays there to fix. The one exception is a typed row's own optimistic failure, which `EditableCell` already shows in place as its "Save failed" chip (5B) — the panel does not double-report it.

**The header**: the backlinks / view-group collapsible idiom — one `<button aria-expanded>` with the chevron rotated −90° while collapsed — reading **Properties**, with the top-level key count when the block parses to at least one (`Properties (4)`; 0 or unparseable → no count, never a guessed one). COLLAPSED by default and deliberately SUBORDINATE to the title above it (small, muted): most pages have nothing to say here. A page with NO frontmatter reads **Add properties** instead — the act rather than the noun, ONE control and not two — and opens on an empty row list carrying the same two acts every page's panel carries.

**Editing (raw mode)**: a monospace `<textarea>` holding the raw interior (`frontmatterInterior`, so the fences are the app's business and never the user's). Text differing from disk is DIRTY, which is the only thing that puts **Save** / **Cancel** on screen; Esc reverts to the disk text (the textarea owns the key while focused, so it never reaches the window's other listeners). Open state AND mode are session-only component state, never persisted — neither an open properties panel nor the mode it was left in is part of a note's identity (BacklinksSection's rule).

**The rows (YAZ-1417):** one row per top-level key in the block's own order, with a type icon, property-name button and shared `EditableCell` value. Clicking the name opens configuration; clicking the value opens its typed editor. Empty values have a quiet placeholder. The footer retains Add property and raw YAML access. Above the rows sits the views' own `ColumnSearch` (YAZ-1473) whenever the expanded typed view has at least one row: it filters rows by KEY — trimmed, case-insensitive substring, block order kept, Reserved/YAML-chipped rows included — while the header count stays the block's total and the footer stays put; no match shows "No properties found."; the query is session-only component state that resets when the panel collapses, when Add property starts and when the mode toggles, so a new row is never born hidden; typing never writes; raw mode, unparseable blocks and empty pages show no box.

**Folder context (YAZ-1417):** the panel subscribes to the existing link feed and resolves folder-page memberships. A sole folder is selected automatically; multiple memberships require an explicit context. That folder's declaration wins, then legacy vault declarations, then the note's YAML value, then text, through the existing `columnTyping`/`cellEditor` ladder. Human-readable existing keys such as `Status` can carry local definitions. New declarations are folder-specific; there is no new shared Status vocabulary.

**A row with no editor offers nothing but its chip** (🔒): the app's RESERVED keys — `folder_page`, `folder_page_settings` and, since YAZ-1472, `comments`, spelled from `FOLDER_PAGE_KEY` / `SETTINGS_KEY` / `COMMENTS_KEY` (the last exported by `shared/comments.ts`, the one place that string lives) and never a local literal, gathered since YAZ-1513 into `links/reservedKeys.ts` `RESERVED_KEYS` (the one list this panel and the views' column delete both read — "Folder page settings": Column typing) — render read-only with a **Reserved** chip, because each already has its own door (the sidebar's turn-into/turn-back, the folder page's settings, the Comments block below the note) and a second one would be a second gesture to keep in step. Values no editor can hold without LYING about them — nested maps, multi-line scalars, and lists carrying either — render read-only with a **YAML** chip pointing at raw mode. Neither kind offers the type control or the delete; a lossy editor is never the answer, and the fallback underneath is.

**Per-key writes are surgical** (🔒): a row's edit, an add and a delete all go through `writeProperty(path, key, value)` — the one-key writer, reused whole and never duplicated — so the rest of the block stays byte-identical, comments included; `undefined` deletes the key (the same semantics the sidebar's turn-back uses). A DELETE takes the deleted key's own leading comment with it: a comment sitting directly above a property describes that property, and `setFrontmatterProperty` treats it as part of the pair. The panel then applies the SAME one-key transform to its own copy of the file, so the raw fallback can never show a block a typed edit already left behind and a later raw Save cannot silently revert it. **Add** takes a name and a value: the name is checked against `PROPERTY_NAME` and reported in the registry's OWN words (`property names are snake_case (…)`), a duplicate is refused outright, and the first value is shaped by the kind the key will be read back at (a declared `number` gets a number, a declared `list` a one-item list) — the registry is the authority, not the typing.

**Editing a definition (YAZ-1417):** both note and view property settings use the shared definition editor and explicit Save/Cancel. `writeFolderColumn(path, key, next, base)` reads fresh bytes and modifies only that column inside the settings map, preserving other columns, views and unknown metadata. Absent settings are created without injecting default views. The captured base is compared against current known definition fields; stale same-column changes are rejected with a reopen message. The check repeats after an mtime conflict. A definition edit never bulk-renames or rewrites note values.

**Select options and ordering (YAZ-1417):** `select` stores a scalar and `multi-select` a list. `PropertyDecl.options` stores exact labels in manual order; new options start empty. Optional `optionSort` is `manual`, `ascending` or `descending`, absent meaning Manual. One shared helper computes natural, case-insensitive display order without mutating the manual array. Settings, value/filter pickers and default Board groups consume that order. Alphabetical modes hide drag grips; Manual supports keyboard and full-row dragging with a stable insertion boundary. Unknown stored values remain visible. Renaming/removing an option changes metadata only, as the UI explains.

**Value persistence (YAZ-1417):** Select uses an anchored searchable popup; Multi-select saves each choice and stays open. Per-cell writes serialize so slower earlier requests cannot overwrite the final selection. Optimistic state survives intermediate index updates while writes are pending; failed writes revert the picker to the supplied durable value and show the error. Property and value popovers are portaled outside document zoom; anchored popovers refit as content size changes.

**The dirty draft never loses** (🔒): switching modes asks NOTHING and discards nothing, because the toggle back to typed rows is DISABLED while an unsaved raw draft stands (its tooltip says why — "Save or cancel your YAML edits first"). The simplest honest rule: no dialog, no silent discard. Typed writes land immediately, so typed → raw is always free. A block that will not PARSE has no rows to show, so raw is the whole surface and the toggle is not rendered at all.

**Live truth** (⚡ amended by YAZ-1356): the panel's disk text is derived from the `file.content` prop — since YAZ-1356 CrepeHost's own `disk` state, the whole-file bytes last seen on disk, fed by the watcher path in BOTH the frontmatter-only absorb and the body reload, because `useFile`'s content is frozen at mount and a fresh `file` would remount Crepe — re-derived whenever that prop moves unless a dirty draft is standing (typed text is never thrown away by a load), and replaced by what it WROTE after its own save — compared against the prop it last saw, so its own write never reads as an external change and bounces back. KNOWN + ACCEPTED (v1): `useFile` loads a path ONCE and CrepeHost handles external changes without touching that object, so a frontmatter change made ELSEWHERE reaches the panel on the next open of the page, not the same instant. The panel is already written to follow the prop the moment it becomes live; do not "fix" this by giving the panel a watcher or a read of its own.

Files: `client/src/editor/FrontmatterPanel.tsx` (+ `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`), `shared/frontmatter.ts` (`replaceFrontmatter`, `frontmatterInterior`, `setFrontmatterProperty`) with `client/src/views/frontmatterWrite.test.ts`, `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (the one-key write), `client/src/views/editorType.ts` (the one ladder), `client/src/views/view/EditableCell.tsx` (the shared typed editors + the `onCommit` seam the panel writes through), `client/src/views/folderPageSettings.ts` (`SETTINGS_KEY`, exported for the RESERVED list) and `shared/comments.ts` (`COMMENTS_KEY`, likewise — YAZ-1472), `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (block one, and the `root` / `properties` / `wikilinks` it threads in), `client/src/app.css` (`.frontmatter-panel*`), `desktop/e2e/properties.spec.ts`.

## Comments (YAZ-1472 — decisions D1-D17 of its scoping, LOCKED)

What every page carries UNDER its body: its own comment stream, Linear's shape inside the note's own scroller. The model is `shared/comments.ts` (whose header doc is the spec), the write dance is `transformFile`'s ("Cell editing"), the surface is `client/src/comments/`; this section is the rules.

**Storage IS the note** (🔒 D1/D2): ONE reserved frontmatter key, `comments`, holding a YAML list of maps — so a comment travels with the note through every sync, rename, copy and Obsidian, and a vault carries no second file to keep in step. A sidecar and a body section were both REJECTED: the sidecar would be the one thing in a vault the note itself does not carry, and a body section would be text the editor could reflow, fold and re-serialise. The list is FLAT: a reply carries `reply_to: <id>` naming its TOP-LEVEL parent, a thread is exactly ONE level deep, and a reply to a reply is filed under the root (`addComment` re-roots it, and a hand-nested one is read the same way) — deeper nesting is what makes comment trees unreadable, and Linear stops at one level too. The spelling lives in ONE place, `COMMENTS_KEY` in `shared/comments.ts`: the block, the index scan and the properties panel all import it, and there is no local literal anywhere.

**The schema** (🔒 D11 — key order as written on disk): `id` (8 hex, `crypto.randomUUID().slice(0, 8)` — enough for one note; a hand-written id that is all digits must be QUOTED, or YAML reads a number and the entry is skipped — the app's writer quotes it), `n` (🔒 D15 — the comment's NUMBER, worn like an id: a top-level comment is one past the highest top-level `n` on the page, shown `#3`; a reply is one past the highest among its parent's replies, shown `#3.1`; handed out once on create and NEVER reassigned — a delete leaves a gap, the way an issue tracker does, so "see comment #3" written anywhere keeps pointing at the same comment;
