absent on a hand-written entry, which shows a dot), `at` (ISO UTC at SECONDS precision, `2026-09-11T18:22:31Z`, so it sorts as a plain string), `reply_to?` (the top-level parent's id, never a reply's), `by?` (WHO wrote it, declared by the WRITER and never inferred — 🔒 D3: the app writes NO `by` for the person at the keyboard, because a file carries no authorship and a guessed one would be a lie; an agent editing the file writes `by: agent` — and the `yaseendocs` command writes it for its caller, `agent` unless `--by` says otherwise, YAZ-1617 D5), `title?` (a one-line heading; blank means no key at all — a title is a key only when it says something), `edited?` (stamped by `editComment`, same format as `at`), `body` LAST (plain text, trailing whitespace trimmed on write and nothing else; a multi-line body serialises as a block scalar). `addComment` writes `id, n, at, reply_to, by, title, body` — `by` only when the writer declares one (the command does; the UI never); `editComment` strips and re-appends `title`, `edited`, `body` so they land last whether it is the first stamp or a re-stamp, and every other key — `by` included — rides through in its place. **Carry what you do not understand** (🔒): unknown keys on an entry and unknown entries in the list are spread through EVERY write byte-for-byte, never rebuilt from the typed subset — the list is the user's (and an agent's) data, and a writer that only knows its own fields must not eat theirs. Deleting the LAST comment deletes the KEY (🔒 D7 — never a `comments: []` left behind: a key with nothing to say is no key, the folder-page turn-back's rule), and deleting a comment takes every reply to it in the same write. On screen the order is CHRONOLOGICAL by `at`, oldest first, stable for equal stamps — or newest-first by the ROOT's `at` when `SettingsState.commentsOrder` is `newest` (YAZ-1515: a global reading preference, one state-driven **Oldest first** / **Newest first** toggle left of fold-all, hidden below two threads; replies inside a thread always read oldest-first; the composer stays at the BOTTOM of the block under both orders — Yasin's call on the demo; under newest-first a new comment therefore lands at the top of the list, away from the box); the FILE is never reordered — a write appends or edits in place, so a hand-arranged list keeps its arrangement and a diff shows one entry.

**Placement** (🔒 D4): the SIXTH block of the Markdown host's scroller — `.page-title`, `.frontmatter-panel`, `.editor-mount`, `.folder-page-contents` (when the page carries the flag), then `.comments`, then "Linked mentions", which stays LAST (Editor rule 25) — so it scrolls WITH the note, inside `.editor-instance`'s content column (backlinks.css's rule, byte for byte), never a panel and never a pane. Unlike the backlinks it is ALWAYS rendered: the composer is the door to the first comment, and a block that appeared only once something was in it would have no way in. It opens EXPANDED on a page that has comments and COLLAPSED on one that has none (🔒 E, YAZ-1515 — decided once at mount, `readComments(file.content).length > 0`; a `foreign` / `invalid` block has nothing to read and starts collapsed too), so on an empty page the header is the door to the composer; nothing follows the count afterwards. It is a VIEW over the same disk truth the properties panel reads — `file.content` is CrepeHost's `disk` state (YAZ-1356), re-derived whenever that prop moves — with no watcher of its own, no read of its own and no IPC (the panel's "Live truth" rule). The header's collapse and every fold are SESSION chrome, never persisted — an open comment stream is not part of a note's identity (BacklinksSection's rule).

**Writes** (🔒 D8): every mutation — add, reply, edit, delete — is exactly ONE `transformFile(path, fresh => …)` ("Cell editing"): the change is computed on the FRESH bytes, lands with `expectedMtime`, and on `CONFLICT` re-reads and recomputes once (a second conflict throws) — so another window, an agent appending and a cell edit from a folder page can all interleave with a comment and nobody's entry is lost. `transformFile` returns `{ mtime, content }` since YAZ-1472 (the transformed bytes, or the fresh read when the transform was a no-op) and the block ADOPTS exactly what landed — never an optimistic copy of its own — holding it against the `file.content` prop it last saw, so its own write never reads as an external change. The watcher echo is frontmatter-only, so it lands through `absorbFrontmatterOnly` → `setDisk` (GRO-2186): an open, DIRTY note never sees a conflict bar from a comment, its unsaved body edits and caret survive, and the block recognises the echo as the bytes it already holds. A failed write is ONE line at the bottom of the block (`Could not save the comment: <message>`) — never a dialog, never App's passive notice — and the draft stays in the box to retry.

**Shapes** (🔒 D10, `commentsShape`): what the key holds is one of FOUR — `absent` (no key, or a bare `comments:`), `list` (a YAML sequence, empty included), `foreign` (a value of the user's OWN under the name — an Obsidian vault may already use `comments` for a scalar or a map), `invalid` (the whole block does not parse). Only `absent` and `list` are OURS to write: `foreign` and `invalid` make EVERY mutation throw `CommentsShapeError` BEFORE any write; the block shows the header and one muted line saying which (`This note's comments property isn't a comment list, so nothing can be added here.` / `The properties block doesn't parse. Fix it in Properties to comment here.`) and no composer — nothing is ever overwritten, because a comment feature that could eat a user's own property would be worse than none. Within a list, MALFORMED entries (not a map, or missing a string `id`, `at` or `body`) are kept on write and skipped on screen; an EMPTY-string body is a comment and still renders; an ORPHAN reply (its parent gone) shows at top level in time order; a hand-made `reply_to` CYCLE never loops — its earliest member stands in as the root; an `at` that will not parse shows as written rather than as "just now".

**Bodies are Markdown, rendered read-only, sanitised** (🔒 D9, `comments/markdown.ts`): GitHub-flavoured — tables, task lists, strikethrough, fenced code, and single newlines are line breaks, the way a typed comment reads. `marked` (gfm, breaks) → DOMPurify (the `html` profile, with `style` / `form` / `input` / `button` / `select` / `textarea` forbidden on top — NO form control survives, a task box is drawn as a glyph, ☑ / ☐) → `innerHTML`, so a body — yours or an agent's — can never run script or reach outside its box. A link in a body opens through `shell.openLink` like the editor's own (one delegated click handler on the block) — never by navigating the app window. Both libraries were ALREADY in the tree through the drawing engine and are now declared DIRECT dependencies of `client/` (a transitive dependency is not a dependency). This is the ONLY place the app turns Markdown into HTML rather than into a ProseMirror document — a second renderer would be a second set of rules to keep in step. The composer is a plain `<textarea>`: what you type is what the file holds.

**The UI** (🔒 D12 — the parts that are contracts): the header is the properties panel's control (chevron, muted label, plain count) at the backlinks header's size, with NO hairline above it (YAZ-1675 — the body flows straight into the header; Linked mentions and the contents block keep theirs) — chevron + **Comments (N)**, N counting every comment and reply, absent at 0, collapsing the rest — with the tools grouped at its right (`comments__tools`, one `margin-left: auto`): FIRST the order toggle (YAZ-1515 — `Oldest first` / `Newest first`, the CURRENT order as its label, the action as its tooltip, shown only with two or more threads), then ONE fold-all button, state-driven the way the views' groups toggle is: **Collapse all** while anything is open, **Expand all** once everything is folded (comments AND reply groups, together). ONE card per thread (on `--bg` — white in the light theme, so dark mode follows): the parent on top, an **N replies** fold, the replies one level in behind a guide line (the outline's, hung under the parent's dot), and a **Reply…** row at the bottom of a threaded card that is ONE line until focused (Linear's row); a comment with no replies yet offers **Reply** on hover instead. The header text NEVER moves (🔒 D16) and, since ⚡ YAZ-1516, NEVER clips — a long title or first line WRAPS and the number, stamp, writer and actions stay on its first line: a titled comment keeps its TITLE in the header row and folds its body under it (chevron, `aria-expanded`); a title-less ONE-LINER is its own header row — it wraps, has no chevron, nothing below, nothing to fold, and keeps the fold's seat so the numbers line up; a title-less multi-line comment keeps its FIRST line (Markdown marker stripped, `commentSplit`, its inline Markdown rendered by `commentInlineHtml` — same renderer, same sanitiser) in the header permanently and folds only the rest. Fold-all counts only what can fold, and is not shown when nothing can. The stamp is relative (`lib/relativeTime`, a minute's resolution) with the absolute time in its tooltip; `(edited)` follows it, and the WRITER shows as `(agent)` after the time in that same quiet voice, because the mark already says it: the avatar's seat holds the comment's NUMBER (`#3`, a reply `#3.1`, a dot when there is none), indigo-soft for you and violet on the `--agent` / `--agent-soft` tokens for a declared writer (a voice that is not yours, deliberately never the accent) — no icons. ⌘Enter submits and Esc cancels — or clears a draft that has no Cancel — with the textarea owning Esc while focused, the properties panel's way. Delete ASKS first (🔒 D17): the app's own sheet, a mirror of the sidebar's `ConfirmDelete` — focus on Cancel, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, click-away cancels, red Delete — and NO "Don't ask me again", because a comment has no Trash behind it and the sheet is the only undo; the copy names the number and, on a parent, the replies that go with it. Copy, LOCKED: **Comments**, `Leave a comment…`, `Reply…`, `Title (optional)`, **Comment** / **Reply** / **Save** / **Cancel**, `⌘ Enter`.

**Never a property** (🔒 D5/D6): `scanFile` DELETES `properties[COMMENTS_KEY]` right after `parseFrontmatter` — comments are CONTENT, not a property — so the key is never in the index, never in its cache, never a view column, never a link source (a `[[link]]` inside a comment is text, not a mention, and never a backlink). `CACHE_VERSION` 2 → **3** (the GRO-2230 rule: the extraction semantics moved, so every old snapshot degrades to one rescan, and the fingerprint pin in `cache.test.ts` moved with it). In the properties panel `comments` joins `folder_page` and `folder_page_settings` as RESERVED — chipped, read-only, no type control, no delete — spelled from `COMMENTS_KEY`, because it has its own door (the block below the note) and a second one would be a second gesture to keep in step; raw mode still shows the bytes, as it does for every key.

**Testing** (🔒 D13): unit tests — `client/src/comments/*.test.ts(x)` (the model's contract lives at `client/src/comments/comments.test.ts` so vitest's client project collects it), `scan.test.ts` / `cache.test.ts` (the drop and the version pin) and `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx` (the chip) — plus typecheck and a pointed real-app pass on an isolated profile. NO Playwright suite for this feature (the standing no-marathons rule).

**The door** (🔒 YAZ-1617 D1–D5): an agent leaves a comment through `yaseendocs` — the app's own Electron run as plain Node (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, VS Code's `code` pattern): a shell shim at `Contents/Resources/bin/yaseendocs` runs `app.asar/out/main/cli.js`, a second main entry that never imports `electron`. Never the app window, never the single-instance lock, no Node install, no checkout. It writes through the SAME `addComment` / `editComment` / `deleteComment`, guarded the SAME way as D8 on node fs (`transformOnDisk`: fresh read, `expectedMtime`, one retry on CONFLICT, a second conflict refuses). Four verbs — `comment`, `comments [--json]`, `edit`, `delete` — a comment is NAMED the way the app shows it, `#3` / `#3.1` (or bare `3`), or by its id, and a name that matches nothing is REFUSED with nothing written (🔒 YAZ-1627: the model forgives an unknown `reply_to` in a hand-written file; the command never creates one) — and `--help` IS the contract: the only documentation an agent reads, so its wording is UI copy. `comment` writes `by: agent` unless `--by` says otherwise (choosing the tool IS the declaration; D3 stands — the UI still writes no `by`); `edit` and `delete` REFUSE a comment with no `by` — a person's comment is the app's alone (D17 asks first there; a script cannot ask). Exit codes 0 done · 1 refused or failed · 2 usage. **The handshake** (🔒 D2): right-click a Markdown page — sidebar row in either lens, or its tab — › **Copy for Agent** puts three lines on the clipboard (`shared/agentInstructions.ts` `agentPrompt`): the page's path, that the app has a command line for its pages, and the command's full path with `--help`. It names NO verb, ever: `--help` owns capabilities, so a verb added later changes nothing here. Main composes it (`shell:agent-prompt`, reveal's read-only posture: `NOT_FOUND` for a vanished page, `UNSUPPORTED_EXTENSION` for a non-Markdown file) because only main knows where the command lives — the quoted shim path when packaged, `node "<out/main>/cli.js"` in dev; the renderer writes the clipboard exactly as Copy path does (`client/src/lib/copyForAgent.ts`, one helper for both surfaces) and the notice says **Copied for agent**. Rejected and NOT to be added back: a PATH installer (🔒 D3 — the path travels with the text; on Apple Silicon `/usr/local/bin` means admin prompts for no gain), per-vault `AGENTS.md` (loads only when the agent STARTS inside the vault), writing into global agent files (imprinting config unasked), vault detection (the handshake states the fact; the command works on the file), the `yaseendocs://comment` deep link (no receipt; a link anywhere could write).

Files: `shared/comments.ts` (the model, `COMMENTS_KEY`), `shared/agentInstructions.ts` (the handshake), `desktop/src/cli/cli.ts` + `index.ts` + `cli.test.ts` (the command), `desktop/build/bin/yaseendocs` (the shim, mode 755), `desktop/src/main/ipc/agent.ts` (`shell:agent-prompt`), `client/src/lib/copyForAgent.ts`, `client/src/comments/CommentsSection.tsx` + `comments.css` (the block), `client/src/comments/markdown.ts` (`commentHtml` / `commentSummary`), `client/src/comments/comments.test.ts`, `client/src/views/writeProperty.ts` (`transformFile`'s `{ mtime, content }` return), `client/src/hooks/useAutosave.ts` (`absorbFrontmatterOnly`, unchanged — relied on), `client/src/editor/Editor.tsx` (the mount, between the folder page's contents and "Linked mentions"), `client/src/editor/FrontmatterPanel.tsx` (the RESERVED entry), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` (the drop) + `cache.ts` (`CACHE_VERSION` 3), `client/src/app.css` (`--agent` / `--agent-soft`), `client/src/lib/relativeTime.ts`, `client/package.json` (`marked`, `dompurify`).

## The Topics sidebar (YAZ-847/848/849 — decisions D1-D7 of YAZ-821, LOCKED)

How you BROWSE folder pages. The model is "Links": Folder pages (who belongs), the config is "Folder page settings" (how a page is set up), the surface inside a page is "The contents block" (what one folder page shows), the file tree's half is "The folder-page gestures" (how a page becomes one); this is the sidebar's OTHER reading of the vault — by MEANING rather than by the folders on disk. `TopicsTree` and `Tree` are deliberately two readings of ONE vault and not two kinds of list: same `.tree__row` family, same `8 + depth * 14` indent, same two open handlers.

**The chrome stack** (🔒 D4/D5, YAZ-847): the sidebar's chrome is two rows above one shared body. ROW 1 is the lens tablist — **Topics ⇄ Files**, rendered in `SIDEBAR_LENSES` order so the DEFAULT lens leads. ROW 2 is the persistent search bar of YAZ-801 (🔒 YAZ-797), untouched by any of this: still ALWAYS visible, on BOTH lenses, loading/error/empty vault included, and YAZ-750's filter affordance still has its seam beside it — the lens row was added ABOVE the bar precisely so that neither the bar nor that seam had to move. Files is today's file explorer byte for byte, now behind a tab. The tabs carry `role="tab"` + `aria-selected` and nothing else — no `aria-controls`, no `tabpanel` — because the body below is SHARED with the flat search results and belongs to neither lens while a query is typed.

**The lens is App's, and it is WINDOW IDENTITY** (🔒 D4, amended by YAZ-1628): one flag, `WindowEntry.sidebarLens`, beside per-window `sidebarCollapsed` — it was global `AppState.sidebarLens` from YAZ-847 until YAZ-1628 moved it, so two windows on one vault now browse on different lenses. It lives in App rather than in the Sidebar because the Sidebar is mounted `key={root}` and only while it is open, so a sidebar-local lens would reset on every collapse/reopen and every root switch. The tabs are not per-folder view state — hence no per-root keying and no `FolderState` entry, and a root switch keeps the lens. App owns the value and passes it down with `onLensChange`; `changeLens` writes through `window.setIdentity` and then mirrors locally, and another window's switch never reaches this one — `storage.subscribe` re-reads settings and width only, never the lens. `window.setIdentity` refuses anything but the two lenses (`BAD_REQUEST`). THE UPGRADE PATH: a pre-847 `yaseendocs.json` has no `sidebarLens` key at all and a pre-1628 one has only the global key; the store's load sanitize seeds every window that has no valid lens of its own from that global value — missing and junk read as `topics` — and omits the old key, so the next write completes the migration (the YAZ-1280 `sidebarCollapsed` shape) and an existing user keeps the lens they had, a first-time one landing on Topics; pinned in `store.test.ts` (the legacy seed, and a valid file with the key omitted everywhere) and in `lenses.spec.ts`, which boots the real app on exactly such a seed.

**Search across the lenses** (🔒 D5), the whole rule in one sentence: a typed query replaces the ACTIVE tab's body, the tabs row stays visible and clickable, and switching lenses mid-search keeps the query. Everything else about the bar — the flat ranked list, the keyboard, Esc, ⌘K — is the search contract under the `sidebar/` entry in "Repo layout", unchanged.

**The roots rule** (🔒 D2): Home leads — whatever `[[Home]]` resolves to through the WINDOW's resolver, alias-aware and case-insensitive, every spelling a CLICK would follow — and ONLY when that page carries the strict `folder_page: true` flag. An ordinary note called Home IS Home (🔒 D1 below) but gets no tree row, and offering to make one is the card's question, not this rule's. Then every OTHER folder page the lookup gives no parents (`folderPagesOf(path).length === 0`), path-sorted, so a vault with no Home still shows all of its top-level topics rather than nothing at all. Home is NOT required to be parentless: a Home that says it belongs somewhere leads the tree anyway AND still renders under its parent — the same "a page belongs under every parent" rule the descent follows — and it can never appear inside ITSELF, because the descent's ancestor path starts at the root it came from. The rule is a pure exported function (`topicRoots`) so it is pinned on its own.

**Row anatomy** (🔒 D3, ⚡ amended by YAZ-870): the row IS the open gesture the file tree taught (click opens; ⌘-click opens a background tab of this window, I3) — and since YAZ-870 a FOLDER-PAGE row also UNFOLDS in the same gesture, add-only: a second click never folds (navigation must not close the tree under you), a ⌘-click leaves the tree alone ("not now" means the sidebar too), and a page with nothing under it records nothing. The chevron is its OWN hit target — expanding a topic is not opening it, and the chevron alone folds. A folder page wears `FolderPageGlyph` and a member count; a leaf wears neither, and no chevron either. THE HONESTY SPLIT, and it is deliberate: the COUNT is the page's DIRECT members (`pagesIn`) — the honest fact about the page, so a member hidden from THIS branch by the loop guard is still counted where it belongs — while the CHEVRON asks the GUARDED question (`guardedChildren`), so it never opens onto nothing. The two are meant to disagree inside a loop, and the loop test is what pins it: with `A ↔ B` expanded from Home, B shows a count of 1 and offers no chevron at all, because its only member is A, already standing above it.

**Descent** (⚡ D6 amendment on YAZ-814, ruled by Yasin): children come ONLY from `guardedChildren(lookup, parent, ancestors)` — never raw `pagesIn` — which is what makes `A → B → A` end quietly at any depth while a page reachable down two branches still renders under BOTH parents. The primitive, the ancestor-PATH guard and the reasoning behind "not a global visited set" all live under "Links": Folder pages → Walking; this tree is one of the expansion-driven surfaces that recurses over the primitive ITSELF — since ⚡ YAZ-815 retired the linear walker there is no other kind — with its own ordering and its own expansion. Each level is ordered by ITS OWN folder page's settings (`orderedMembers`, the [D5] rule's ONE place — "Folder page settings"), because the order lives on the page that owns the members and never on whoever happens to be showing them: Home's `order` says nothing about what is inside Mid.

**Expansion** (🔒 D4): keyed by PAGE PATH, never by tree position — expanding Metrics under one parent expands it under EVERY parent, which is the mockup's behaviour and the honest one, because the user opened the PAGE. It persists per vault in the main-owned `folders[root].topicsExpanded`, `expanded`'s TWIN in every respect: one flat list of absolute paths, riding the SAME `setFolder` patch, capped at `MAX_TOPICS_EXPANDED_PAGES` (500 — `folds`' cap, for a bucket of the same kind) client-side AND again main-side, and repaired by `store.renamePath` / `store.removePath` exactly as `expanded` and the fold keys are, because it is a path-keyed bucket like them. Never written into any note's frontmatter. The write-back is idempotent by construction: the first render after a mount holds exactly what it just read, and re-sending it would make main commit, write and broadcast for nothing. The SET is the SIDEBAR's since ⚡ YAZ-873 and `TopicsTree` is CONTROLLED (`expanded` + `onExpandedChange`, the tree computing the next set and handing it up): the restore, the write-back and the vault keying moved up to the owner, which needs the same set for the lens row's expand/collapse-all button — so the expansion now outlives the tree's own mount and a lens switch is even less of a teardown than before. `allExpandableTopics(records, lookup, resolve)` is that button's answer, exported beside `topicRoots` and walking the same descent the rows are drawn from. Uncategorized's own open/closed state is SESSION-only, still owned by the tree and deliberately NOT in that bucket — the bucket holds page paths and is repaired as such, and Uncategorized is not a page.

**Focus Mode** (YAZ-1605, both lenses): right-click a folder row (Files) or a folder-page row (Topics) — or a shift-selection holding it — and "Focus on folder" / "Focus on N folders" / "Focus on topic" / "Focus on N topics" narrows that lens to those rows. The focus is a per-lens path LIST in the WINDOW's identity (`WindowEntry.focusDirs` / `focusTopics`, per window since YAZ-1628 — `sidebarCollapsed`'s rule, so two windows on one vault focus independently; empty = off, uncapped; ⌘⇧N inherits both lists by value, a root change clears them, `renamePath` / `removePath` repair them through the windows map like `tabs`), restored on mount through `window.identity` and written back idempotently through `window.setIdentity` like the expansions are through `setFolder` — never the per-vault bucket, so another window's state broadcast cannot move it. FILES: `focusRoots(tree, focus)` in `treeState.ts` draws the focused dirs as the TOP rows in tree order, OUTERMOST only (a focused dir inside a focused dir draws once, under its parent); `Tree.tsx` is untouched — it just gets those nodes. TOPICS: `topicRoots(records, lookup, resolve, focus)` is the ONE door — the focused folder pages are the roots in the chosen order, `allExpandableTopics` walks only them, and Uncategorized hides while the focus RESOLVED (a focus that names no folder page any more falls through to the full rule, Uncategorized included); a nested focused topic is also its own root, as a diamond page already is; Home is never a focus target. SHARED, in `Sidebar.tsx`: `MenuTargets.focusPaths` is its OWN field (GRO-2296), computed by `focusable()` — dirs (Files) / non-Home folder pages (Topics) out of the right-clicked row or, inside a 2+ selection that holds it, the ordered selection (files in a selection are simply not focusable, as folders are not openable for `openTabPaths`); `focusOn(paths)` REPLACES the list (multi never stacks) and opens each row; the eye (`.sidebar__focus-off`, accent, aria-label "Exit focus mode") renders ONLY while the ACTIVE lens is focused, one slot left of the chevrons, gone with them while a query is typed, and one click empties that lens' list; the expand/collapse-all button measures and acts on the dirs ON SCREEN (`shownDirs`), so folds outside a focus are exactly as they were when it ends; a focused target that leaves the tree / index (deleted, moved out, unflagged) is PRUNED from the list in an effect — the last one leaving ends the focus, never an empty tree under a lit eye (the `topicRecords.length === 0` guard keeps the empty pre-index snapshot from clearing a restored Topics focus); a Files reveal whose target is outside every focused dir exits the focus first. Search stays global; blank-space creates still land in the vault root. Not in v1: a shortcut, a breadcrumb, focus inside search results, stacked focus. Files: `client/src/lib/treeState.ts` (`focusRoots`, `findDirNode`), `client/src/sidebar/{Sidebar,TopicsTree,ContextMenu}.tsx`, `client/src/app.css`; tests in `treeState.test.ts`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` (`focus mode (YAZ-1605)`), `TopicsTree.test.tsx`, `ContextMenu.test.tsx`, `store.test.ts`, `windows.test.ts`, `ipc/window.test.ts`, `storage.test.ts`.

**Uncategorized** (🔒 D7, the locked DEVIATION from the mockup; mini-Files hierarchy added by YAZ-956, directory actions added by YAZ-1080): a muted row at the bottom that EXPANDS IN PLACE — never a virtual page, never a main-pane view (that idea is parked on YAZ-824). Its set is the lookup's carve-out-free `uncategorized()` MINUS whatever the guarded Topics descent already draws, and the subtraction is THIS surface's to make: the lookup has no carve-outs by ruling ("Links": Folder pages, D5 — a surface subtracts whatever it already displays). The resulting pages are projected through their existing vault-relative `IndexRecord.folder` into a RECURSIVE, PRUNED mini version of Files: root pages remain direct children; only folder branches containing an Uncategorized page exist; directories precede pages and both sort case-insensitively at every level. The projection is renderer-local and recomputes from each live index snapshot — no schema, IPC, watcher, cache or filesystem scan of its own. Its disk-folder rows reuse the Files visual contract (`tree__row tree__row--dir`, chevrons, `8 + depth * 14`, hover/focus): normal click and Left/Right fold only that branch, while right-click reports the existing `MenuRow { type: 'dir', path }` on the folder's absolute path and opens the sidebar's ONE menu with the applicable Files directory actions — Reveal in Finder, Open in VS Code, Copy path, New note, New folder page, New folder, Rename and Delete. File-only actions stay absent; `RenameInline` replaces the folder row and `CreateInline` renders beneath it through the same handlers Files uses. Disk folders still are not page-open targets, filesystem drag/move rows, topic drag sources, topic drop targets or topic parents. Page rows retain the Topics contract at every disk depth — open/preview/commit, active state, file menu, inline create/rename, and topic drag with a null source because disk ancestry is not topic belonging. Every populated disk branch starts open; collapsed paths are a session-only set owned by the current `TopicsTree` mount, distinct from page-path `topicsExpanded`: closing/reopening the outer section preserves them, while a true lens remount resets them. A folder-anchored create and "Show in sidebar" for a nested Uncategorized page reopen only the required collapsed disk branch before drawing the input/flash. An empty folder created from this menu can disappear from the PRUNED Topics projection until it contains an Uncategorized note; it remains on disk and visible in Files. Up/Down walks all visible folder and page rows. The Topics expand/collapse-all command continues to affect folder pages only.

**Home** (🔒 D1/D2 + the ⚡ amendment on YAZ-797, shipped in YAZ-849): HOME IS IDENTITY, NOT A PATH — whatever `[[Home]]` resolves to IS the vault's Home, established through the window's own resolver and never through a path check. A `home.md` in a subfolder, or any page aliased `Home`, already is it and nothing is created. That includes an ORDINARY, unflagged page called Home: it IS Home, the user has simply not flagged it, so the routine must neither overwrite it nor spawn a rival — while the roots rule above still gives such a page no tree ROW. The two answers differ on purpose: they are different questions asked by different surfaces, and an ordinary-page Home therefore shows nothing in the tree yet still BLOCKS creation. When NOTHING answers, the ⚡ amendment splits on ADOPTION — whether `<root>/.yaseendocs/` exists: an ADOPTED vault gets `<root>/Home.md` created AUTOMATICALLY on vault open, carrying exactly what any new folder page is born with — the flag plus the default `status` declaration, `ensureHome.ts` creating through `newFolderPageProperties()`, the SAME builder "New folder page" births through (YAZ-1513, "The defaults" under "Folder page settings"), so this birth and that one can never drift. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-849): Home used to carry `folder_page: true` and nothing else (`HOME_CONTENT` = `seedContent({ [FOLDER_PAGE_KEY]: true })`); the "same builder" rule is the part that survived; an UN-ADOPTED folder is not written into AT ALL, and the Topics lens shows the offer card instead, whose one button runs the very same create. There is never a silent fallback to the Files lens: an un-adopted vault with no Home lands on Topics with the card ABOVE the tree, replacing nothing — the roots and Uncategorized render underneath it exactly as they would.

ADOPTION is probed READ-ONLY, through `api.tree(<root>/.yaseendocs)`: the dotfolder is invisible to the tree, the index and the watcher but NOT to a direct bridge call at an absolute path (`scaffold.ts`'s template read is the standing precedent), and `fs:tree` is the one existing call that distinguishes a MISSING directory (`NOT_FOUND`, from `requireDir`) from an existing one — `vaultConfig.read` cannot, collapsing "no dotfolder" and "no such config file" into the same absent. So: no new IPC, nothing created, and the answer honest. EVERY error class falls to the write-nothing branch — `NOT_FOUND` is the ordinary never-adopted, and `FORBIDDEN` / `NOT_A_DIRECTORY` / `IO_ERROR` are not proof of adoption either — because the safe side is always the card, which writes nothing until the user clicks.

The TRIGGER (`useEnsureHome`) is APP's, not the sidebar's and not the tree's, because Home is born ON VAULT OPEN: a collapsed sidebar, or a window sitting on the Files lens, must not be able to skip it. It fires the moment the window's index first lands — "index-ready" is `source.resolve !== null`, the same readiness every wikilink surface reads and the only honest one, since before it an empty snapshot would say "no Home" about a vault nobody has looked at yet — and a ref keyed by ROOT makes it exactly ONCE PER ROOT PER SESSION, because index refetches poke that subscriber constantly and must never re-run the decision. IDEMPOTENT three times over: the resolver answers before anything is written; the create is 4B's content-at-create call, whose `wx` flag never overwrites; and the double-open RACE is therefore BENIGN — an `ALREADY_EXISTS` rejection means the other window won and the file is there, which is the outcome we wanted, so it reports `exists`. Deleting Home re-creates it on the next OPEN of an adopted vault, and never while anything still answers `[[Home]]`. The card's LIVE half is asked here on the tree's own feed, so the card retires the moment a Home appears — an unflagged one included — while `unadopted` stays true afterwards, because it is a durable fact about the FOLDER and not about Home. Failures take the sidebar's standing route for file ops: the passive notice (`Can't create Home: <message>`), never a dialog.

**Feed** (🔒 D6): the window's ONE `WikilinkResolveSource` — the same records + resolver pair backlinks, the contents block, the `[[` picker and the folder-page toggle read — subscribed once and re-read WHOLE on each poke, with an unchanged snapshot keeping the previous object so index churn that changed nothing here costs no render (BacklinksSection's idiom). No fetch, no watcher and no IPC of this lens' own; liveness IS the normal index refetch. Before the first index lands the feed is empty and this renders NOTHING — not even the Uncategorized row, which would otherwise flash "0" over a vault it has not seen yet.

**Plain DOM, no virtualization** (🔒 D6): every row of an expanded branch is mounted, exactly as the file tree mounts every row of an expanded directory. `TableView`'s windowing is a real precedent in this codebase and is deliberately NOT taken here — a topic tree's mounted set is bounded by what the USER opened, not by the size of the vault — and it stays parked until a real vault is found where it hurts.

**The row menu** (YAZ-865, amended by YAZ-1080): a PAGE row's right-click opens the SAME `ContextMenu` a Files FILE row opens, on the page's own path — Open in new window, Reveal in Finder, Open in VS Code, Copy path, New note, New folder page, the state-aware folder-page toggle, Rename and Delete in the menu's one locked order. `TopicsTree` reports a shared `MenuRow`; the Sidebar owns the one menu and every target/pipeline, so the two lenses cannot drift. On a flagged folder-page row, New note/New folder page keep YAZ-869's declaration path (`newPageFromFolderPage`, member parking and `folder_pages` last); leaf Topics rows keep plain create-beside. New folder remains absent from PAGE rows because a disk folder is never topic belonging.

**The YAZ-1080 disk-folder amendment:** an Uncategorized disk-folder row reports `{ type: 'dir', path: <absolute folder> }` through that same callback. It gets exactly the applicable Files directory actions — Reveal in Finder, Open in VS Code, Copy path, New note, New folder page, New folder, Rename and Delete — while file-only Open in new window and folder-page toggle remain absent by the existing `MenuTargets` rules. Its create target is the directory itself; `RenameInline` replaces the folder row and `CreateInline` renders once beneath it at child depth. A collapsed target reopens before the input appears. The folder remains outside filesystem drag/move and topic membership gestures.

The Uncategorized HEADER and offer card still name no row and therefore fall through to the root-targeted blank-space menu; Topics PAGE rows still omit New folder. A page rendered under multiple topic parents draws only the first matching inline input, preventing competing autofocus. The belonging gestures remain on pages: folder-page contents and page-row topic drag; disk folders never become topic parents.

**The drag** (YAZ-959, scope locked in its comments — engine YAZ-990, gesture YAZ-991): a row dragged onto a FOLDER-PAGE row changes what the page BELONGS to, never where its file lives. The file tree's HTML5 idiom and the same `tree__row--drop` highlight, and the view owns not one rule of its own: `canDrop` (`sidebar/topicsMove.ts`) refuses non-folder-page targets, self, the row's current parent and the child's own descendants — descending through `guardedChildren` only, so a loop the user already wrote ends the walk — and only a row the engine accepts even HAS a `dragover` handler, so the browser refuses the rest natively. The SOURCE is the parent the dragged OCCURRENCE renders under — a page under two topics is dragged out of whichever row you grabbed — and null at the root and in Uncategorized, where the drop GAINS a belonging instead of swapping one. The pinned Home and the Uncategorized header are outside the gesture on BOTH sides (a drop onto Home would be root-drop in disguise; v1 has no root-drop, no reorder — order stays the outline's — no multi-select and no alt-drag). The drop writes NOTHING: it opens `ConfirmMove` (the `ConfirmRemoveMember` mirror — focus on Cancel so a stray Enter moves nothing, Esc/Enter, click-away, in-app never native), and only the confirm runs `performMove`: ONE `folder_pages` write on the member's own frontmatter — the old parent filtered out through the click rule, the new one appended, prose and every other parent surviving verbatim — deliberately NOT tag-then-untag, whose two writes would compute from one stale snapshot and clobber each other. No optimistic re-render: the moved row arrives with the index echo, reliable since YAZ-986. A failed write takes the sidebar's standing route, the passive notice — never a second dialog on top of the one just dismissed. **Plus the source outline** (YAZ-1364, 🔒 D4 of YAZ-1357): when the source topic's outline names the moved page, `performMove` drops exactly the lines the × un-tag counts (`dropOutlineLinks`) in one `folder_page_settings` write on the source's own card — because the outline is the truth about belonging and the reconcile pass would otherwise tag the page straight back. The destination is still never written; adoption appends there on its next open.

Files: `client/src/sidebar/TopicsTree.tsx` (the tree + the exported `topicRoots` and `allExpandableTopics`), `client/src/sidebar/topicsMove.ts` (the drag engine: `canDrop`, `performMove`) + `client/src/sidebar/topicsMove.test.ts`, `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmMove.tsx` (`moveConfirmMessage` + the sheet) + `client/src/sidebar/ConfirmMove.test.tsx` and `client/src/sidebar/ensureHome.ts` (`HOME_LINK`, `HOME_CONTENT`, `isAdopted`, `createHome`, `ensureHome`, `useEnsureHome`) with `client/src/sidebar/TopicsTree.test.tsx` + `client/src/sidebar/ensureHome.test.ts`; `client/src/sidebar/Sidebar.tsx` (the lens row, the body switch, and `openTopicsMenu` — the Topics row's way into the one `ContextMenu`) + `client/src/sidebar/Sidebar.test.tsx`; `client/src/sidebar/createEntry.ts` (`MenuRow`, the least a right-clicked row has to say — a `TreeNode` and a Topics row both satisfy it, which is what lets `targetDirFor` serve both trees); `client/src/sidebar/RenameInline.tsx` + `client/src/sidebar/CreateInline.tsx` (reused verbatim by the Topics rows, never re-implemented); `client/src/App.tsx` (`sidebarLens`, `changeLens`, `useEnsureHome`); `client/src/lib/storage.ts`, `desktop/src/main/store.ts`, `desktop/src/main/ipc/window.ts` and `desktop/src/main/ipc/state.ts` (`setSidebarLens` through `window.setIdentity` since YAZ-1628, the `topicsExpanded` patch and its cap, the load sanitize and its lens migration); `shared/types.ts` (`SidebarLens` / `SIDEBAR_LENSES` / `isSidebarLens` / `FolderState.topicsExpanded` / `MAX_TOPICS_EXPANDED_PAGES`); `client/src/app.css` (the `.tree__count`, `.tree--uncategorized` and `.topics-offer` rules); `desktop/e2e/lenses.spec.ts`, `desktop/e2e/topics.spec.ts`, `desktop/e2e/topicsDrag.spec.ts`.

## The migration (YAZ-853 — decisions D1-D6 of YAZ-822, LOCKED)

`tools/migrateFolderPages.mjs` — the ONE-SHOT script that turns a `page_type` vault into the folder-page model, and the only place the retired type system is still spoken. `node tools/migrateFolderPages.mjs --vault <path>` is a **DRY RUN by default**; `--apply` is the only thing that writes (`--dry-run` is the explicit no-op form). Plain ESM run straight by node — no build step — reading `shared/frontmatter.ts` and the app's own property vocabulary through Node's type stripping (22.6+, on by default from 22.18), so the migration and the app can never disagree about what a frontmatter write does; the resulting `MODULE_TYPELESS_PACKAGE_JSON` warning is muted at the one call site and every other warning still goes through.

**The gates.** (1) It REFUSES TO RUN unless the vault is a git repo with a CLEAN working tree (🔒 D1) — the undo button for everything below is `git checkout .`, so there must be something to check out back to; the tree is re-verified immediately before the first write, because the scan takes time. Our own `migration-report.md` is the single path the cleanliness test forgives, since a dry run writes it by ruling (🔒 D6) and must not then block the `--apply` that follows. (2) `--apply` refuses outright while the plan holds any BLOCKER. (3) Writes are PER-KEY through `setFrontmatterProperty`, so untouched keys, comments, key order, quoting and the whole body survive byte for byte; a note whose frontmatter will not parse is never written to, only reported. (4) NOTHING is ever overwritten: a move onto an occupied name stops that one file, a folder page whose name already answers is ADOPTED (flag + Home entry) rather than replaced, and Home is never created while anything already answers `[[Home]]`. IDEMPOTENT by construction — a second run over a migrated vault finds nothing left to do.

**What it does**, in the order the report lists it: CARDS (🔒 D3) — `page_type: x` becomes a `folder_pages` entry `[[<Plural>]]` and the key is deleted; `channels:` / `functions:` link arrays MERGE into `folder_pages` (order preserved, de-duplicated the way a click de-duplicates) and are deleted; the `function:` TEXT field goes only where a `functions:` links field also existed; every other relation is untouched. FOLDER PAGES (🔒 D4) — one per distinct `page_type`, named from `.yaseendocs/types.json` (`pluralName` → `displayName` → an irregulars map → a regular pluralizer), carrying the flag, `folder_pages: ["[[Home]]"]` and the type's registry properties as `folder_page_settings` columns; Home is created with an outline `order` over them; templates follow the new names and `types.json` is deleted once everything is out of it. DISK (🔒 D5) — the deep `functions/<sub>/` trees flatten, the `channels/<sub>/` bins dissolve into their pages' typed folders, emptied directories go, and the retired `.base` files go with them. REPORT (🔒 D6) — printed AND written to `<vault>/migration-report.md`, per-transform counts with FULL lists.

**The four post-checks**, run straight after `--apply` and appended to the report (a failure exits non-zero): (1) no `page_type` key remains anywhere; (2) every `folder_pages` entry the migration wrote resolves — through a resolver asking exactly what a CLICK asks — to a page carrying the strict flag; (3) each folder page's member count equals the old per-type count plus whoever already pointed there; (4) every pre-existing body `[[wikilink]]` still resolves to the same page it did before. Tests: `tools/migrateFolderPages.test.mjs` — 41 cases in the `tools` vitest project, each building a real vault, `git init`-ing it and spawning the real CLI end to end (no mocks, no alias, no jsdom).

## Packaging (F1 — GRO-2175)

`npm run desktop:build` = electron-vite build + `electron-builder --mac` with the config in `desktop/package.json` `"build"`: appId `com.yasinarshad.yaseendocs`, productName `Yaseen Docs`, targets `dmg` + `dir` for arm64 ONLY, `identity: null` (ad-hoc signature — no Developer-ID, no notarization, locked), category productivity, icon `desktop/build/icon.icns` (placeholder). Output: `desktop/dist-app/mac-arm64/Yaseen Docs.app` + `desktop/dist-app/Yaseen Docs-0.1.0-arm64.dmg` (`${productName}-${version}-arm64.dmg` — the version is the ROOT package.json's, injected via `-c.extraMetadata.version`). The bundle's Info.plist claims: `protocols` registers the `yaseendocs` scheme with LaunchServices (so OS-level link opens route to the app), and `fileAssociations` lists `.md`/`.markdown` with role Editor, rank **Alternate** — Yaseen Docs appears in Finder's Open With but never steals the default handler. Packaged files are `out/**` only. No auto-update, no Intel/universal, no Windows/Linux (locked — Future issues).

## Editor rules (client)

1. **Frontmatter**: on load, `splitFrontmatter(content)` → `{ frontmatter, body }`; only `body` goes into Crepe. On save, write `frontmatter + getMarkdownForSave(crepe)`. Frontmatter is re-prepended byte-identically (Crepe would otherwise turn `---` YAML into `***` + paragraph + setext heading underline).
2. **Crepe construction**: always via `createCrepe()` — features come ONLY from `src/editor/featureConfig.ts` (`ENABLED_FEATURES` / `DISABLED_FEATURES`, every `CrepeFeature` classified; ImageBlock, TopBar, AI off; `featureConfig.test.ts` asserts the running editor loads exactly the allowlist), list_item content `block+` (extended from GFM task item schema), `markdownUpdated` listener wired. The allowlist says which features load; **`featureConfigs` configures one of them**. Its ONE entry is `[CrepeFeature.BlockEdit]`: `listGroup.orderedList = null` removes Crepe's stock Ordered List row (YAZ-1329), while optional `buildMenu` appends Drawing (YAZ-877); neither changes the feature SET. **The Drawing slash item** (`src/editor/drawingMenu.ts`, second build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852): Crepe builds its stock groups and then hands the `GroupBuilder` to `buildMenu`, so Drawing is APPENDED to the existing `advanced` group (Image / Code / Table / Math) of Crepe's OWN menu — never a parallel slash plugin, so filtering, keyboard navigation and hiding all come for free. HOST-SUPPLIED CREATOR: the row exists only when `createCrepe({ drawing })` provides `create()` (Editor wires `() => createDrawing(root)` plus the window's passive `onNotice`) — absent → NO Drawing row is added, while Ordered List remains absent in both cases. `onRun` matches the stock items' mechanism as far as it can: `clearTextInCurrentBlockCommand` first (the same opening move every stock item makes — it clears the block the `/query` was typed into), then, where a stock item would call a schema command, a `tr.insertText` of the embed — because a drawing embed is PLAIN MARKDOWN TEXT (rule 23's `![[…]]`, no schema change, no serializer change, round-trip byte-identical outside the one inserted line). Crepe's `onRun` is synchronous and the write is not, so the view is re-read after the await and the text goes at the LIVE selection; a failed create inserts nothing and goes to the passive notice, never a dialog. The file itself: `createDrawing()` (`src/drawings/`) writes a valid EMPTY Excalidraw scene (`{ type: 'excalidraw', version: 2, source: 'yaseen-docs', elements: [], appState: {}, files: {} }`, pretty-printed + trailing newline) to `assets/drawings/Drawing YYYY-MM-DD HH.mm.ss.excalidraw` through `writeAsset` with `create: true` — the drawings home is made by `writeAsset`'s own `mkdir -p`, never scaffolded, and NEVER OVERWRITES: a same-second collision comes back `ALREADY_EXISTS` and the bounded loop retries ` 2`, ` 3`, … (any other failure propagates to the notice). It returns the BASENAME, which is all the embed needs — `readAsset`'s shortest-path rule finds it anywhere under the root. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` step 1 (the spec the preview YAZ-878 extended in steps 2-4, rule 27, and the modal YAZ-879 extended in step 5, rule 28).
3. **Save post-processing**: `postProcessMarkdown()` un-escapes `\[\[` → `[[` (wikilinks/embeds) and restores the visible same-line numeric bullet spelling armored by rule 8.
4. **Replacing content in a live instance** (external change / Reload): `setMarkdown(crepe, md)` → `replaceAll(md, true)` from `@milkdown/kit/utils`, keeping focus and caret. TAB SWITCHES never remount (GRO-2234): every VISITED tab keeps its editor mounted in its own layer, inactive layers hidden via `visibility: hidden` — never `display: none`, which would reset the scroller's scrollTop — so scroll position, caret, undo history and the unsaved buffer all survive a switch. Opening a DIFFERENT file in the same tab still remounts the Crepe host (`key={path}`): the old file's editor unmounts (→ autosave flush) and the previous file stays on screen until the next one has loaded.
5. **Outline folding** (GRO-2011): `createOutlineFolding()` (`src/editor/outline/`) is registered in `createCrepe()`. Parent `list_item`s (those owning a nested `bullet_list`/`ordered_list`) get a widget `<button class="outline-toggle" aria-expanded>` at the start of their content (one stroked SVG chevron sized by `--fold-chevron-size`, default 14px, rotated −90° via CSS when collapsed — GRO-2093; the 18px box is unchanged so strip/gate geometry holds); collapsing adds `data-outline-folded="true"` to EVERY nested list of the item (CSS `display:none`) — mixed markers at one indent (`* a` then `- b`) are unified on load (rule 8, GRO-2112); should sibling lists still arise (e.g. bullet + ordered), all of them fold (GRO-2031). Toggles are metadata-only transactions (`tr.docChanged === false`) — the listener never fires `markdownUpdated`, `getMarkdownForSave()` is unchanged, and the file's mtime is untouched. Fold keys = FNV-1a hash of the item's first-block text + occurrence index (`outlineFoldKeys.ts`), persisted via `storage.getFolds/setFolds`. Fold transactions carry one of: a position (toggle), `fold-all` / `unfold-all`, `{ set, collapsed }` (guide-line click: direct parents on collapse, their full parent-item subtree on expansion), `undo-fold`; the multi-item ones remember the previous collapsed set so ⌘Z reverts them as one step.
6. **Accepted lossy normalisation** (standard remark-stringify behaviour; content preserved, formatting normalised): `-`/`+` bullets → `*` (alternating `-` for adjacent sibling lists), tabs → 2-space indent, structural `1)` ↔ `1.` ordered markers swap/renumber (but rule 8's same-line numeric bullet text keeps its visible delimiter), setext → ATX headings, two-space hard breaks → `\`, trailing whitespace stripped, `___` → `***`, indented code → fenced, tables re-padded, lazy blockquote continuation gets `> `, `_`/`*`/`[`/`=`/`&` escaped in text where ambiguous, bare URLs/emails → `<autolink>`, file always ends with a single `\n`. First save of an untouched file WILL rewrite the file in this normalised form.
7. **Outliner keymap** (GRO-2012, `src/editor/outline/listCommands.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` with keymap priority 100): Tab = indent (no-op on a first sibling — never inserts spaces), Shift-Tab = outdent (level 1 → paragraph; following siblings nest under the lifted item), Enter at the end of a parent item = new FIRST child (after the subtree when the parent is folded), Enter on an empty item = outdent / leave the list, Backspace at the start of an item = join into the previous paragraph (empty parent: children take its place; non-empty parent: no-op). Multi-item selection + Tab/Shift-Tab moves all selected items.
8. **List-item round trip** (`src/editor/listItemRoundTrip.ts`): an empty bullet/ordered item is written as a bare marker (`*` / `1.`), never `* <br />` — `<br />` opens a CommonMark HTML block that swallows the item's nested children on reload. Parsing gives an item whose nested list starts on a later line (`* ` + children, Obsidian's empty parent) an empty leading paragraph. **Same-line numeric bullet text** (YAZ-1329): a numeric delimiter immediately after a bullet marker (`* 6) text` / `* 6. text`, any bullet spelling or indent) is temporarily escaped before CommonMark parses it, so it remains the bullet's paragraph text rather than becoming a nested ordered list; save removes that boundary escape and preserves the visible digits/delimiter. Real ordered Markdown on its own line remains structural. Fenced code, decimals, prose and non-immediate numeric text are untouched. Before parsing, `normalizeEmptyItems()` also rewrites legacy `* <br />` lines to bare markers and an empty task `* [ ] ` / `* [x]` to `* [ ] <br />` (remark alone reads `[ ]` as text and would write `* \[ ]`); on save `stripEmptyTaskBreaks()` writes it back as `* [ ]` — `<br />` never reaches the disk. **Mixed markers** (GRO-2112): the same pass runs `unifySiblingMarkers()` — `-`, `*` and `+` are one bullet: bullets at one indent parse as ONE list (the first sibling's marker wins on load; a blank line starts a new list, as in Obsidian). CommonMark would otherwise start a new list on every marker change. Line rules live in the function's docblock. Disk is untouched by loading; the first save writes `*` throughout (rule 6) as before. **Empty nested item** (YAZ-1357): the same pass runs `separateEmptyNestedItems()` LAST — a text item followed on the very next line by a DEEPER bare marker gets one blank line between them, because CommonMark forbids an empty item from interrupting a paragraph (without it `* a` + `  *` reads back as the text `a *`, and the outline seed's `-` spelling as a setext heading the bullets-only schema drops whole, which is what tripped the YAZ-974 guard). The blank line is what Milkdown writes back, so the file converges on it; fenced code is untouched.

9. **Depth bullet glyphs** (GRO-2013, `src/editor/outline/bullets.css`): CSS only — Crepe's bullet svg is hidden and `.label.bullet::after` draws ● / ○ / ■ by nesting depth of `.milkdown-list-item-block` (repeating every three levels; ordered levels count), `--list-indent: 2.15em`, marker colour 78% of the text colour, all from Crepe theme tokens. Heading-first items (`* # Part 1`) offset the label row by the heading's margin and size it to the heading's line box (`--list-label-offset/height`, shared with the fold chevron). **Done tasks read as done** (YAZ-1514, 🔒 D1–D4): the same file strikes and dims (`--list-done-color`, 55% of the text colour) the FIRST line (a textblock — a first-child code block or table is left alone) of any `li.list-item` whose label carries Milkdown's own `checked` class — so `Mod-Enter`, the ☑ click and a `[x]` written by Obsidian all look identical; nested children keep their own state, a second paragraph in the item stays normal, and `~~` is NEVER written to the file. Markdown untouched.
10. **Typography** (GRO-2009, `src/app.css`): Obsidian defaults — system font stack, 16px / 1.5 body, heading scale 1.802 / 1.602 / 1.424 / 1.266 / 1.125 / 1em at weight 600 (h1 700), line-height 1.3 — set through Crepe's `--crepe-font-*` / `--crepe-base-font-size` custom properties on `.editor-instance .milkdown`.

11. **Underline** (GRO-2028, `src/editor/marks/underline.ts`): ProseMirror mark `underline` (`<u>` in the DOM). On disk it is obsidian-underline's inline HTML `<u>text</u>`: a `$remark` plugin wraps each mdast `html("<u>")` … `html("</u>")` pair (nearest match, any depth, inside any inline parent such as `**…**`) into an `underline` mdast node after parsing, and registers the remark-stringify handler that writes it back as `<u>` + children + `</u>` — so `a <u>b</u> c` round-trips byte-identically. Unmatched tags and every other inline HTML keep going through Milkdown's `html` atom node unchanged. Not a Crepe feature (allowlist untouched, no toolbar button).

12. **Zoom into a bullet** (GRO-2029, `src/editor/outline/zoom.ts` + `zoom.css`): view state ONLY, like folding — the plugin holds the zoomed `list_item` position (mapped through every transaction; cleared if the item is deleted), and decorations hide every block off the path root → zoomed item (`outline-zoom-hidden`), strip the ancestors' glyph/chevron/first block (`outline-zoom-ancestor`, a folded ancestor renders expanded without touching fold state) and render a breadcrumb widget (`File name › Ancestor › … › Item`, labels = first-block text truncated at 40 chars; crumbs zoom to that ancestor, the file crumb zooms out fully). Triggers: click the bullet glyph (`.label-wrapper`; task checkboxes keep toggling), `Mod-.`, `Mod-Shift-.` (see Keyboard). Zooming dispatches a metadata-only transaction: never `markdownUpdated`, disk untouched, fold state unaffected. Not persisted — switching files remounts the editor and clears it, and an external file change (watcher → `replaceAll`) replaces the doc and clears it too. `createCrepe({ zoom: { fileName } })`; allowlist untouched. **History** (GRO-2091): every zoom change (glyph, crumb, `Mod-.`, `Mod-Shift-.`, zoom-out included) pushes one in-memory entry `history.state = { mdappZoom: { file, key } }` with the URL untouched (2-arg `pushState`; `key` = fold-key scheme over ALL list items, `null` = zoomed out), and `popstate` restores that level without pushing — so Back / Forward walk zoom levels. Entries of another file are ignored (left behind by a file switch → a Back press can be silent), an unresolvable key zooms out, the original page entry (no zoom state) = zoomed out.

13. **List guide lines** (GRO-2030, `src/editor/outline/guideLines.ts` + `.css`): every nested list (`li.list-item > .children > .content-dom > ul/ol`) gets a CSS `::before` strip — 10px wide, absolutely positioned (`-1 * (--list-indent / 2 + 10px)`) so its centred 1px line runs under the parent's glyph centre; zero layout shift, folded lists take their line with them, colours from `currentColor` (14%, hover 35%). Pseudo boxes hit-test as the list element, so a pointer left of the list's border box = the strip: the plugin's `handleDOMEvents` turns mousedown into `toggleOutlineFoldChildren(listPos)` (GRO-2107/YAZ-1317: any direct parent expanded collapses the direct parents; when all are collapsed, the click unfolds every parent item in their full subtree; the line's owner and leaves remain untouched; an all-leaf line is a swallowed no-op; markdown/mtime untouched, caret unmoved) and mousemove into the `outline-guide-hover` highlight (element `:hover` would light up while editing text inside).

### Clipboard input and paste modes (YAZ-1394)

Normal Paste keeps Milkdown’s supported rich HTML preference, document typography, existing fake-bullet outline conversion, and Markdown interpretation when only plain text is available. `editor/clipboardPaste.ts`, registered after `outlinePaste`, converts external root-level `p / br… / p` separators into one empty paragraph per standalone break. A break-containing paragraph otherwise acquires an extra visual line. This preserves intentional blank spacing rather than deleting it; inline breaks, nested blocks, explicit empty paragraphs, and unmarked internal `data-pm-slice` HTML are untouched. Our marked copy-out separators are restored to empty paragraphs first, including within internal slices and nested lists, preserving attributes and slice metadata. The upstream Google Docs wrapper cleanup runs first. This rule is based on a reproduced clipboard shape; it is not a promise to normalize arbitrary source HTML.

Native Edit and right-click menus expose **Paste as → Plain text / Markdown**; Plain text uses **⌘⇧V**. Main captures text once per gesture and sends `ClipboardPasteRequest` to that window. Plain text inserts characters literally, preserves spaces and line breaks (CRLF/CR become LF), and does not parse Markdown. Markdown explicitly parses that text with the existing schema, even when the clipboard also has HTML; open slice boundaries let inline content remain in its surrounding sentence. Both modes apply existing paste rules (including table repair and the folder-page outline lock), replace the active selection in one undoable transaction, and leave empty clipboard text as a no-op. In code, text remains literal.

**Normal pasted numbering (YAZ-1429):** external numbered lists become ordinary text with visible labels, so Enter cannot continue or renumber them. `editor/clipboardNumbers.ts` converts HTML ordered-list markers before DOM parsing, honoring numeric start, reverse order and explicit item values while retaining rich inline content, nested blocks and empty parent labels. Foreign `data-pm-slice` context is removed so it cannot recreate automatic numbering. Plain-text-only paste uses the existing remark AST and each item's source position to preserve original labels/punctuation, repeated numbers and task markers; code nodes remain literal. Mixed fake-bullet outlines use the same conversion, paste constraints and undo boundary. Saved ordered Markdown, Number children and explicit Paste as Markdown remain unchanged. Newly copied app HTML carries `data-yaseendocs-clipboard` alongside ProseMirror's slice metadata so intentional internal lists round-trip. This identifier exists only in clipboard HTML, never in note files. A text-only copy has no source identity and follows normal external-paste rules; explicit Markdown is the deliberate override. Existing notes are not migrated. Source-specific CSS counters are outside this normalization contract.

Each Crepe view subscribes through `menu.onPasteAs`, claims only its own focused surface, and unsubscribes on destroy. CodeMirror and ordinary inputs use the private validated native `insertText` fallback, retaining their own caret and edit handling. Read-only Crepe views cannot be changed by either path. No clipboard writes, mode flags, async clipboard reads, new dependency, or changed save serializer are involved in paste handling. `clipboardPaste.test.ts`, native menu, preload bridge, and window IPC tests cover these boundaries; native input undo and the actual source-app clipboard still require a pointed manual check when validating a release.

### Clipboard output (YAZ-938, YAZ-1389, YAZ-1411, YAZ-1443)

Normal Copy/Cut provides readable `text/plain` and rich `text/html` (YAZ-1443). `editor/clipboardPlainText.ts` reads the selected ProseMirror structure rather than Markdown source. Paragraph boundaries contribute one newline and each explicit empty paragraph contributes one additional blank line; repeated/leading/trailing selected empty rows, inline breaks, code whitespace and literal punctuation remain intact. Headings/marks/links copy their visible text without Markdown syntax; links retain their target in HTML and explicit Markdown. Lists retain readable labels, nesting and task state, and table cells use tabs. A single text block selected inside open list wrappers copies its selected words without inventing a bullet; selections spanning parent and nested children keep their hierarchy. No global whitespace collapse or note migration is involved.

Edit and right-click menus expose **Copy as → Plain text / Markdown**. Both write text only, removing rich formats so destinations cannot select HTML instead. Plain text matches normal Copy’s readable text. Markdown uses the existing source serializer, including intentional syntax and empty-paragraph markers. Each Crepe subscribes through `menu.onCopyAs`, claims only its focused selection (including read-only selections), and unsubscribes on destroy. Focused CodeMirror uses its public editor-state selection API, preserving all nonempty ranges and line breaks as literal code in both explicit modes. Preload falls back to selected input/textarea or focused DOM text; password fields and empty selections leave the clipboard unchanged. Main validates the registered live sender, main frame, renderer URL, current target and bounded string before the private `clipboard.writeText` call. No arbitrary clipboard writer is exposed through the renderer API.

For rich HTML (YAZ-1411), the existing clipboard-only DOM serializer reuses paragraph rendering/attributes and marks, sets paragraph margins to zero, and exports each empty paragraph as a standalone BR carrying `data-mdapp-empty-paragraph`. Actual Google Docs testing showed that it drops `p > br` empty paragraphs but preserves a standalone BR as an empty paragraph; our paste adapter restores the marked BR before ProseMirror parsing. The app clipboard identity and ProseMirror slice metadata remain intact for intentional internal lists. No placeholder spaces or invisible characters are introduced. Default HTML, the live editor and the save serializer are unchanged by the plain-text fix. Destination apps can apply their own formatting; real-app review remains useful. Clipboard copy/paste integration tests and native menu, preload and private IPC tests cover these contracts.

## Keyboard (client)

All bindings are `$shortcut` keymaps registered in `createCrepe()` with priority 100 (Crepe's own keymaps are 50), so they run first and fall through (`return false`) when they do not apply. `Mod` = ⌘ on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere (ProseMirror decides by `navigator.platform`). App-level window & tab shortcuts (⌘⇧N / ⌘⇧O / ⌘W / ⌘⇧W / ⌃Tab / ⌃⇧Tab) live in the application menu — see "Menus & shortcuts".

| Keys | Where | Does | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Tab` | caret/selection in list items | indent (sink) the item(s); no-op on a first sibling — never inserts spaces | GRO-2012 `listCommands.ts` |
| `Shift-Tab` | list items | outdent (lift); level 1 → paragraph; following siblings nest under the lifted item | GRO-2012 (Crepe default, kept) |
| `Enter` | end of a parent item | new FIRST child (after the folded subtree when the parent is collapsed) | GRO-2012 |
| `Enter` | empty item (no children) | outdent; at level 1 leaves the list as a paragraph | GRO-2012 |
| `Backspace` | start of an item's first block | join into the previous paragraph / parent item; empty parent: children take its place; non-empty parent: no-op | GRO-2012 |
| `Mod-Enter` | list item(s) whose first block touches the selection | cycle bullet → `[ ]` → `[x]` → bullet, each item from its own state; a `[x]` item's first line renders struck + dimmed (rule 9, CSS only — the file stays `[x]`); outside lists falls through (table exit / CodeMirror exit keep theirs) | GRO-2027 `hotkeys.ts` · YAZ-1514 `bullets.css` |
| `Mod-ArrowUp` / `Mod-ArrowDown` | caret in a list item, or in a heading section outside lists | fold / unfold that item (`setOutlineFoldAtSelection`, same meta-only transaction as the chevron — persisted, ⌘Z-revertible; leaf or already in that state = consumed no-op), or the innermost heading section at the caret (rule 29, `headingHotkeys.ts`); outside both falls through to the native document-start/end jump | GRO-2092 `hotkeys.ts`, YAZ-1140 |
| `Mod-Shift-u` | anywhere | fold every parent bullet and foldable H1-H3 heading section in one metadata-only transaction; both plugins persist through the app state's `folders[root].folds` | `foldAllHotkeys.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-i` | anywhere | unfold every parent bullet and foldable H1-H3 heading section in one metadata-only transaction | `foldAllHotkeys.ts` |
| `Mod-z` | a fold, a zoom or a heading fold was the latest VIEW action (no doc change since) | revert exactly that one step — an individual fold, a combined bullet+heading fold-all/unfold-all (both exact prior sets in one transaction), or a zoom. All transaction kinds carry `VIEW_ACTION_META` (`viewActions.ts`); each plugin drops its pending undo when a foreign kind fires, while `document-fold` deliberately keeps both halves of the combined action eligible. Otherwise falls through to history's undo. Plugin-appended transactions (e.g. Crepe's trailing paragraph) don't break eligibility | GRO-2075 `outlineFolding.ts`, GRO-2091 `zoom.ts`, YAZ-1140 `headingFolding.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-x` | selection | toggle strikethrough (Obsidian binding; Crepe's `Mod-Alt-x` still works) | GRO-2027 |
| `Mod-u` | selection | toggle underline (`<u>…</u>` on disk) | GRO-2028 `marks/underline.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-h` | selection or caret | toggle the YELLOW highlight (`==…==` on disk): lit if any of the selection carries yellow → remove it, else apply it (edge whitespace excluded); at a caret flips the stored mark for what is typed next; the toolbar's yellow dot is the same command | YAZ-1480 `marks/highlight.ts` |
| `Mod-.` | caret in a list item | zoom into that item (view-only; breadcrumbs appear); outside lists falls through | GRO-2029 `outline/zoom.ts` |
| `Mod-Shift-.` | while zoomed | zoom out one level (parent item, or fully at top level); not zoomed falls through | GRO-2029 |
| `Shift-Tab` / `Mod-[` | while zoomed, on the zoomed item or a direct child | no-op (lifting would escape the zoomed subtree); everywhere else outdents as usual | GRO-2029 |
| `↑` / `↓` / `Enter` / `Esc` | while the `[[` link picker is open | move the highlight (wraparound) / insert the highlighted suggestion as plain `[[name]]` text / dismiss leaving the typed text; with the picker CLOSED all four fall through — the picker's keymap is registered before the outliner's so its priority-100 Enter wins the tie only while open (rule 24) | GRO-2191 `wikilink/wikilinkPicker.ts` |

14. **Multi-block drag** (GRO-2019, `src/editor/multiBlockDrag.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()`): dragging the block handle while a multi-block selection contains the grabbed block moves the WHOLE selection. A document-capture mousedown expands the selection to whole sibling blocks (`expandedBlockRange`: deepest level containing both ends — sibling `list_item`s inside a nested list, not the outer list) and suppresses Crepe's single-block selection replacement; a capture dragstart hands ProseMirror a fully CLOSED slice (an open slice would splice into the target paragraph) via `dataTransfer` + `view.dragging`; after the drop an `appendTransaction` (on `uiEvent: 'drop'`) deletes the merged empty shell the move-deletion leaves at the origin, walking up wrappers it was the only child of. Drop X-position controls nesting depth (ProseMirror `dropPoint`). A grab OUTSIDE the selection never arms the plugin — Crepe's single-block drag is untouched. Folded parents drag with their hidden children.
15. **Block handle gate** (GRO-2081, `src/editor/blockHandleGate.ts`): while the pointer is inside a guide-line strip band or a fold chevron's box, `.milkdown-block-handle` gets `mdapp-handle-muted` (pointer-events none + invisible) so those clicks land; muting pauses during drags. Band geometry is imported from `guideLines.ts` — one source of truth. Crepe's `blockHandle.shouldShow` config is typed upstream but never consumed; do not reach for it.
16. **Hotkey reference** (GRO-2067, `src/settings/hotkeys.ts` — the Settings › Hotkeys page since YAZ-1679 — a `standalone` section of the dialog, its own page under the nav divider rather than a stretch of the scrolling settings page — which retired the keyboard button beside the settings cog): the page lists every binding, one group per table, from the exported `HOTKEYS`/`VIEW_HOTKEYS`/`WINDOW_HOTKEYS`/`MOUSE_TIPS` lists, and the settings search indexes every key and label so "close tab" finds the table (`WINDOW_HOTKEYS` = the app-menu window & tab shortcuts — ⌘⇧N, ⌘⇧O, ⌘, Settings (YAZ-1679), ⌘W Close Tab, ⌘⇧W Close Window, ⌃Tab/⌃⇧Tab and ⌘⇧]/⌘⇧[ Next/Previous Tab — plus the ⌥-click Open Recent open-beside tip; `MOUSE_TIPS` carries the editor wiki-link click conventions — click = current tab with create-on-missing, Links C GRO-2192 — the SHARED "⌘-click file or link" background-tab line (sidebar I3 + editor links, one convention) and the right-click-for-Open-in-new-window tip) — update them together with any keymap or menu-accelerator change (`hotkeys.test.ts` pins the set).
17. **URL** (GRO-2069, `src/lib/urlHash.ts`): the open file is mirrored as `#/abs/path.md` via `history.replaceState` (never pushState); on boot a hash file wins over the folder's remembered `lastFile` and may live OUTSIDE the current root (the sidebar's stale-file validation skips out-of-root paths). No file → no hash. The shareable `yaseendocs://` link is separate — see "Links".

18. **Bullet threading** (GRO-2094, `src/editor/outline/bulletThreading.ts` + `.css`): view-only decorations derived from `selection.$from` on every update — `outline-thread-node` on each list_item on the root → caret path (glyph takes the app accent `--accent` via `--list-marker-color` on its `.label-wrapper`; Crepe's frame `--crepe-color-primary` is #333 and would read as black), and inside each NESTED list on the path `outline-thread-seg` on items 0…pathIndex plus `outline-thread-stop` on the path child (segment cut at its glyph centre, elbow into the bullet). Top-level lists have no guide line and get no segments (Logseq-identical). 2px `--accent` `li::before` at the guide-line x with `pointer-events: none` (a pseudo hit-tests as its element; guide-line click-to-fold needs the UL as target). No state, no persistence, never `markdownUpdated`. Drawn only under `.app[data-threading='on']` — the dialog's Bullet threading › "Show" row (default On, `settings.bulletThreading` in the app state); the dialog's Bullet threading › "Line width" (1/2/3px) and "Line colour" (picker + Default = accent) rows feed `--thread-width` / `--thread-color` (GRO-2109). Caret semantics: the path is that of `selection.$from` — a NodeSelection on an item threads to its parent, a multi-item range threads the first item.

19. **Base files** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2125, deleted in YAZ-844): `.base` is no longer a file the app opens. `BaseHost` (the main-pane host with its view/raw-YAML modes, its own autosave baseline and the "opening a base and doing nothing never writes" invariant) is gone, `Editor` has no `.base` branch: it dispatches supported files to their Markdown/text/PDF/image capability owners, while a `.base` on disk remains unsupported: invisible to the index, not openable in-app, not creatable — it lists in the tree with `kind: null` (YAZ-1577 D4) and clicking it hands it to the OS. What survives is the ENGINE, the views, `ViewsPane` and the schema types (`viewSchema.ts`'s `parseViews` / `serializeViews` / `updateViews`), all of them folder-page internals now: the views live inside a folder page's `folder_page_settings` and render in its contents block, where the behaviour lives on ("The contents block"). The 1B normalisation note travels with them — `yaml`'s `indentSeq` is document-global, so a views block mixing sequence-indent styles is re-indented to one style on its first REAL save. What the `.base` file's byte-identical round trip becomes here is narrower and stated honestly: the page's BODY is untouched and every other frontmatter key survives, because `writeFolderPageSettings` goes through the one-key writer — but the settings block itself is rebuilt from the parsed value, so comments and hand-formatting INSIDE `folder_page_settings` do not survive an edit to it.

20. **Base embeds** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2145, deleted in YAZ-844): `![[X.base]]` / `![[X.base#View]]` no longer renders anything. The whole `src/editor/baseEmbed/` folder — the `$prose` decoration plugin, the slot store, the `BaseEmbed` widget and `resolveBase`'s shortest-path resolution over `api.tree` — is gone, and with it the read-only embed chrome's only production caller — `ViewsPane`'s `readOnly` and `initialView` props outlived it by one unit and were amputated in YAZ-846 (see "View chrome"). An `![[X.base]]` left in a note is now just an ordinary embed whose target does not exist: the text stays byte-identical, nothing renders under it. A folder page's contents block is where views are read now, and it is not embeddable anywhere else — one page, one block, always its own members ("The contents block").

21. **`base` code blocks** — TOMBSTONE (GRO-2146, deleted in YAZ-844): a fenced block with `language === 'base'` is a plain CodeMirror code block again. `src/editor/baseCodeBlock/` — the `$view` replacement for `code_block`, its slot store, the `BaseCodeBlock` widget and the raw-YAML toggle — is gone, `createCrepe` no longer takes `baseCodeBlocks`, and every language now delegates to Crepe's stock block. The text on disk was always the exact code-block text, so nothing about any existing note changed except that the block stops rendering as a view. Views live on a folder page's card now, edited through the toolbar rather than through YAML by hand (rule 19's tombstone, "The contents block").

22. **Typed-page scaffolds & the new-entity flow** — TOMBSTONE (Bible B GRO-2202, deleted in YAZ-836): the registry scaffold, `page_type`, per-type templates, the starter `All <pluralName>.base`, `createType` / `NewTypeDialog` and the sidebar's "New ▸ <type>" submenu are all gone; birth-from-a-declaration now lives on the FOLDER PAGE ("Folder page settings": Birth from a folder page — `scaffoldFromFolderPage` / `newPageFromFolderPage`, templates re-keyed to `<root>/.yaseendocs/templates/<folder page basename>.md`), the still-valid vault-wide property declarations moved to `.yaseendocs/properties.json` ("Properties"), and the views toolbar's New button is the plain 5D filter-derived seed again ("New note") — except inside a folder page, where YAZ-819 re-pointed it at the folder page's own declaration ("The contents block"). The number is kept because rules 23-26 and the `// rule N` citations in the code index into this list; `ensureFolder` is the one helper of `src/views/scaffold.ts` that outlived the type half (`memberFolder`, the parking rule both birth surfaces ask, was built on it in YAZ-869).

23. **Wikilinks in the editor** (Links A, GRO-2190, `src/editor/wikilink/`): `[[target]]` renders Obsidian-live-preview style via INLINE DECORATIONS only (`wikilinkPlugin.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` — never a Crepe feature, the allowlist is untouched; never a schema/serializer change, so the text round-trips byte-identically — `roundtrip.test.ts` pins alias / heading / `#^block` / adjacent / inside-bold / unicode-and-spaces / unclosed-`[[` forms). Caret OUTSIDE the match: the `[[` `]]` brackets (plus `target|` before an alias and each `#`) get `wikilink__syntax` (CSS `display: none`); visible text carries `wikilink` (accent colour); `[[t|alias]]` shows only the alias; `[[t#h]]` shows `t > h` (each post-`#` segment gets `wikilink__sub`, whose `::before` draws the separator). Resolved vs unresolved comes from the ONE resolver (`resolverFor`, see "Link resolution"): an unresolvable target dims via `wikilink--unresolved`; an empty target (`[[#h]]`) is a same-file link, never dimmed. A match whose DISPLAY would be empty (`[[Note|]]`, `[[|]]`, `[[#]]`) gets NO decorations at all and stays raw and editable (GRO-2197): hiding every part of it would erase the text from the page and leave no `.wikilink` span for the click handler either. Caret INSIDE or immediately adjacent (selection overlapping the match, boundaries INCLUSIVE): that match's decorations drop — raw editable syntax; this is also why `display: none` hiding is arrow-safe: the caret never crosses hidden text because at a boundary the match is already raw. Excluded: `![[…]]` embeds (they stay plain text — the base-embed widget that used to claim some of them died in YAZ-844, rule 20's tombstone) and code (`code_block` nodes and inline-`code` marks — mirrors the index's `stripCode`). Resolution reaches the plugin through a per-window `WikilinkResolveSource` (`createCrepe({ wikilinks })`): App owns one stable source and `WikilinkIndexBridge` feeds it from `useIndex`; an index update swaps the resolve function and pokes each subscribed editor with a META transaction — decorations recompute live, the editor never remounts, the doc never changes, `markdownUpdated` never fires.
