Interaction (Links C, GRO-2192, `wikilinkClick.ts` + `createFromLink.ts`, wired as `createCrepe({ wikilinkNav })` — App threads this window's tabs API + its notice setter through `Editor`): collapsed `.wikilink` spans get `cursor: pointer` and navigate on MOUSEDOWN with the default prevented — BEFORE the caret could land in the match and flash the raw syntax, so the reveal stays caret-adjacency/keyboard-only (exactly Obsidian's live-preview model: click navigates; to edit, place the caret adjacent and arrow in). Plain click → the CURRENT tab (`openCurrent` — an already-open path activates its tab); ⌘-click → a BACKGROUND tab (`openBackground` — appended, never activated, focus untouched; already-open → no-op); clicks on REVEALED raw text never navigate (a revealed match has no decoration span — that IS editing); Alt/Shift/Ctrl-modified clicks keep their defaults (future gestures — note ⌥ is now the right-panel key on folder-page Table/Board rows, YAZ-1557, so a future ⌥-click on a link should mean the same). An UNRESOLVED link CREATES its page first (`createFromLink`), then opens by the same gesture: `|alias`/`#…` stripped (`linkPageName` — the ONE strip with the decorations); `[[#h]]` (empty target, same-file) is a no-op — the heading jump is GRO-2239; a bare target creates under the "Default location for new notes" setting (C2-, GRO-2240): `current` (default, YAZ-1643) → the SOURCE page's folder — the page the link was clicked or typed in, so a right-panel or folder-page editor creates beside ITSELF, never beside the main tab (a source outside the vault → the root), `root` → the vault root, `folder` → `<root>/<newNoteFolder>` — App resolves it (`newNoteBase`) behind a stable ref-backed `newNoteFolderFor(sourcePath)` getter threaded through `Editor` into `wikilinkNav` as `createFolder` (each editor binds its OWN path) and read at CLICK time, so settings changes land live without remounting any editor; a pathed `[[Sub/Page]]` (or `[[/Page]]`) is an EXPLICIT AIM: always root-relative whatever the setting (Obsidian's behavior), with missing parents — the base FOLDER of the setting included — created level by level (scaffold's `ensureFolder`); `.md` appended unless already markdown (mirror of `entryPath`); ALREADY_EXISTS just opens it (the creation race is benign); invalid names (per-segment `validateEntryName`) and create failures surface via the passive link-notice — never a dialog. Two more notices, both GRO-2197: a click landing in the PRE-INDEX window (no resolver yet, so every link still renders resolved and creating anything could shadow an existing note) is still swallowed, but now SAYS so instead of doing nothing at all; and a ⌘-click that CREATES a page names it in a notice, because a background tab is by definition not shown and the gesture otherwise looks like nothing happened. The watcher-driven index refetch then flips unresolved→resolved styling everywhere on its own (the live restyle above).

24. **The `[[` link picker** (Links B, GRO-2191, `src/editor/wikilink/wikilinkPicker.ts` + `.css`): typing `[[` in any non-code textblock opens a suggestion popup over every markdown note in the vault; typing filters, ↑/↓ navigate (wraparound), Enter or click INSERTS PLAIN TEXT `[[name]]` (replacing `[[fragment` up to the caret — never a schema/doc-structure change; the rule-23 decorations restyle it on their next pass, raw while the caret still touches it), Esc dismisses leaving the typed text — and that same `[[` stays closed while typing continues (a fresh `[[` reopens). Candidates come from the index through the shared matcher (`links/completion.ts` — the ONE `[[…]]` matcher with EditableCell's link editors). Matching is case-insensitive over the candidate NAME with the typed fragment trimmed at its ENDS (internal spaces stay significant), RANKED exact → prefix → substring and stable within each bucket, capped at 8 AFTER ranking (GRO-2197 — so `[[cac` puts the note actually named `CAC` first instead of whatever the path order happened to reach first). Ranking means the full candidate list is scanned every keystroke, which the per-candidate lowercase precomputed at construction pays for; the 5,000-record smoke in `completion.perf.test.ts` (the `perf` project) is the budget guard: `linkCandidates(records)` gives each note its SHORTEST unambiguous name — duplicate basenames follow the resolver's shallowest-depth rule, so the bare name belongs to the shallowest match and every other duplicate shows AND inserts its root-relative `folder/basename`. **Aliases** (E2, GRO-2214): a note with frontmatter `aliases` also gets ONE row per alias — typed as the alias, shown as `CAC — Customer Acquisition Cost` (the note half is that note's own unambiguous name, so duplicate aliases are folder-disambiguated by construction) and INSERTING the piped `[[Customer Acquisition Cost|CAC]]`, which targets the note and displays the alias. The piped form is exact whoever else claims the alias, so an alias row is unambiguous exactly like a folder-disambiguated duplicate; a row matches on its own name only (never on the note half of its label). Two alias rows are skipped outright (GRO-2197): one whose alias IS the note's own chosen name (it would read `CAC — CAC` and insert the pointless `[[CAC|CAC]]`), and one whose alias contains `[` or `]` (its piped insert would re-parse as the inner link and point somewhere else entirely). A non-empty fragment nothing matches offers ONE "Create" row: it inserts `[[typed text]]` as-is AND creates the page right there (YAZ-1357, 🔒 D3 revised — Yasin's ruling that a picked Create shows up in the sidebar at once: `createFromLink` under `nav.createFolder()`, the same placement as create-on-click — beside the page being typed in (YAZ-1643), no navigation, a passive `Created "…"` notice; a create failure is said through the same notice; without a nav the row only inserts). A hand-typed dangling link is still created on click (rule 23's Links C interaction); typing `|` is alias entry: the suggestions close and typing continues as plain text. Anatomy: a `$prose` plugin owns the state (session recomputed per transaction from doc + selection; candidate updates poke a meta transaction — same live-update pattern as rule 23) and the row DOM (`role="listbox"`/`option`, mousedown-preventDefault, theme tokens); `SlashProvider` (`@milkdown/kit/plugin/slash`) positions the popup at the caret and toggles `data-show` (its one-char `trigger` cannot express `[[`, so it runs a custom `shouldShow` over the plugin state; closing hides directly — the provider skips meta-only transactions). Keys ride `wikilinkPickerKeymap` (`$shortcut`, priority 100 — see "Keyboard"): every command declines (`return false`) while the picker is closed, so ↑/↓/Enter/Esc are NEVER swallowed then; the Enter tie with the outliner's priority-100 Enter breaks by addition order — `createCrepe()` MUST `use` this keymap before `outlinerKeymap` (`wikilinkPicker.test.ts` pins both directions). Per-window plumbing mirrors rule 23: `createCrepe({ wikilinkCandidates })`, App owns one `WikilinkCandidateSource`, `WikilinkIndexBridge` feeds it `linkCandidates(records)` per ready snapshot. No new GLOBAL hotkeys — the Hotkeys page (rule 16) is untouched.

25. **Backlinks — "Linked mentions"** (Links D, GRO-2193, `src/links/backlinks.ts` + `BacklinksSection.tsx` + `backlinks.css`; decision D of GRO-2096 and the GRO-2193 placement thread, both LOCKED). **Storage**: none — the referencing set is computed CLIENT-SIDE in ONE pass over the Markdown index snapshot the window already holds (`backlinksFor`), through THE shared semantic resolver (`resolverFor`, see "Link resolution"). View-only files never enter that snapshot or resolver and therefore never receive or contribute backlinks. No reverse map in the main process, no new IPC, no new `IndexRecord` field, and no watcher of its own: auto-refresh rides the existing 300 ms-debounced index refetch. A mention is a Markdown record's `links` **or** `embeds` entry resolving to the open Markdown path — an `![[embed]]` mentions its target exactly like a `[[link]]` — with the open note itself excluded, ONE entry per referencing note however many mentions it holds, path-sorted. Alias-awareness is free (E2, GRO-2214): a note linking `[[CAC]]` IS a linked mention of the page whose frontmatter aliases it. Results are memoized per (records array identity, path) in a WeakMap (`resolverFor`'s idiom), so one snapshot costs one pass per open path however many Markdown tab layers are mounted. **Placement**: a collapsible block at the BOTTOM of a Markdown note, INSIDE the editor's scroller — `.editor-host` holds `.page-title` (block ZERO, ⚡ YAZ-888), `.frontmatter-panel` (block ONE, ⚡ YAZ-883), `.editor-mount` (where the Crepe instance lands), the folder page's contents, the comment stream, and then the section — so it scrolls WITH the note, Obsidian's in-document mode. No right panel, no sidebar section; one per Markdown tab. Since YAZ-819 the folder page's contents block sits between the Crepe mount and this one when the page carries the flag ("The contents block", 🔒 D1), since YAZ-1472 the comment stream sits between that block and this one on EVERY page ("Comments", 🔒 D4 — the sixth block), and since YAZ-888 the page title sits above the mount with the properties panel under it (⚡ YAZ-883), this is the Markdown host's LAST block, and it stays last; text/PDF dispatch before this host and mount none of it. **Header**: "Linked mentions (N)" with the view-group chevron (rotated −90° while collapsed), COLLAPSED by default, N always visible — and N === 0 renders NOTHING at all (quiet over complete: Obsidian's in-document mode keeps an empty block, we do not). **Snippets, on demand**: an entry only mounts while the section is EXPANDED and reads its Markdown file from that mount (`fs:read`) — collapsed costs zero reads, and the index stores no positions. A snippet is the mention's own LINE — one snippet per line however many mentions it holds (GRO-2197) — whitespace-trimmed and windowed to ~120 chars around the FIRST mention with `…` on the clipped sides, at most 5 lines per note. The line reads exactly as the editor renders it: every wikilink on it is shown as its DISPLAY text through the shared `linkDisplayText` (`[[Note|CAC]]` → `CAC`, `[[Note#H]]` → `Note > H`, embeds and the empty-display forms of rule 23 stay raw), never the raw `[[…]]` source, and EVERY mention of the open note on that line is highlighted (`<mark class="backlinks__match">` per range, so a line mentioning it twice is one row with two marks; a mention pushed outside the window by the ~120-char clip drops, the centred one never does). Code is skipped through the rename engine's length-preserving `maskCode` (the index's `stripCode` mirror), and frontmatter lines scan like any other — the index reads links there too, though only as whole-value exact `[[…]]` strings, so a frontmatter line CAN highlight a mention the index did not count (GRO-2197 audit, deferred). A skeleton bar shows while a read is in flight; a FAILED read leaves the entry standing WITHOUT snippets — never a dialog, never a notice. **Live**: a fresh snapshot recomputes N (and drops the whole section when the last mention goes); an EXPANDED entry re-reads its snippets only when its own record's `mtime` moved — changed files only, so unrelated vault activity costs no reads. **Clicks**: plain → the CURRENT tab (`openCurrent`), ⌘ → a BACKGROUND tab (`openBackground`) — the same model as wiki-link clicks (rule 23) and sidebar rows; entry title and snippet rows behave identically (title = basename, tooltip = the full path). **Collapse state** is per-component and in-memory: since every visited Markdown tab keeps its editor layer mounted (rule 4) it is effectively per tab for the session, and it is never persisted — an open backlinks block is not part of a note's identity. **Feed**: the per-window `WikilinkResolveSource` carries the semantic resolver AND the Markdown snapshot it was built from (`update(resolve, records)`, fed by `WikilinkIndexBridge` — one semantic snapshot feed for decorations, the Markdown picker rows and backlinks alike), while the view-only catalog remains a separate navigation-only source; the section subscribes only to the semantic source, so index updates never re-render App or remount an editor.

26. **Block handle menu + manual numbering** (YAZ-726/728/729/793/1329, `src/editor/blockHandleMenu.ts`, `outline/numberChildren.ts`, `outline/numberChildrenRow.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()`): right-click on the 6-dot handle opens a `.ctx-menu.ctx-menu--editor` popup mounted in the editor's own parent (no overlay; closes on outside mousedown, Escape, scroll, window blur and any doc change). Rows are DATA from a `RowProvider` called at open time with the probe target (`blockHandleTarget.handleTargetPos`, shared with multi-block drag) — the plugin never knows what a row does. First row: "Number children" ↔ "Bullet children" flips EVERY direct child list of the hovered `list_item` between `bullet_list` and `ordered_list` in one transaction (one undo step); it REPLACES the child list's marker type, never stacks a bullet and number on one row; grandchildren untouched; disabled (still shown) when the item has no direct child list. List commands NEVER read or write paragraph text — hand-typed same-line `* 1) Setup` is literal text under rule 8. **This is the only editor conversion command that creates numbering**: `createCrepe()` removes upstream's `1. ` `wrapInOrderedListInputRule` and `Mod-Alt-7` `orderedListKeymap`, and configures BlockEdit without the slash-menu Ordered List row; `- ` / `* ` bullet rules stay (E1). Explicit ordered Markdown still loads/persists because it is the command's disk format and valid imported/pasted content. Right-button mousedown/mouseup on the handle are stopped at document capture so Crepe's NodeSelection + focus never fire for a right-click.

27. **Drawing previews in the page** (YAZ-878, third build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852, `src/editor/drawing/drawingPreview.ts` + `.css`, registered in `createCrepe()` only when `opts.drawingPreview` supplies the vault root): an `![[Sketch.excalidraw]]` embed RENDERS AS THE SCENE. **DECORATIONS ONLY** (🔒 the same rule as 23): the embed stays plain markdown TEXT — no schema node, no serializer, no node view — so round trip is byte-identical (`roundtrip.test.ts` plus a guard in the plugin's own test). ProseMirror has no replace-a-range-with-a-widget decoration, so the mechanism is the honest pair the codebase already uses: a `Decoration.inline` over the whole match carrying `drawing-embed__syntax` (CSS `display: none`, `wikilink__syntax`'s exact mechanism) plus a `Decoration.widget` at the match START (`side: -1`, `contenteditable="false"`, `key` = target + state so PM reuses the DOM node and a settled preview never flickers). **REVEAL** is rule 23's, with boundaries **EXCLUSIVE** instead of inclusive: a selection strictly INSIDE the match drops both decorations (raw, editable syntax), while the caret sitting immediately before or after it keeps the preview — because YAZ-877's insert leaves the caret exactly there and an inclusive rule would hide the preview the insert exists to show. Boundary behaviour stays sane either way: an arrow key off an edge steps inside and reveals, and Backspace at the right edge eats the final `]`, un-matching the embed and leaving what a plain-text delete leaves. **ONLY `.excalidraw` TARGETS** (`isDrawingTarget`, case-insensitive suffix): every other embed (`![[img.png]]`), every link, and anything inside a code block or an inline-`code` run is untouched. The target goes to `readAsset` **RAW** — path or bare basename — because that is where the resolution rule lives (YAZ-876); the renderer never joins paths. **STATES**: *loading* is a quiet empty frame (no spinner — nothing to jitter); *ready* is the SVG inside that same frame (`max-width: 100%`, `max-height: 24em`, `cursor: pointer` only when a click handler exists) — one shared frame so the wait never jumps into the render AND an empty drawing, which exports to a near-nothing SVG, is still a visible clickable line; *broken* — missing file, unreadable bytes, not a scene, a render that threw — is a small INERT chip (“Broken drawing”) and the embed text **stays visible and editable**, because a drawing whose file is gone must remain a line the user can fix by hand. Nothing is ever repaired or rewritten. **THE DEPENDENCY** (`@excalidraw/excalidraw`, PINNED 0.18.1, the one addition of this unit): loaded ONLY through a dynamic `import()` on the first preview render (`drawings/renderScene.ts`), so the editor's main chunk never carries it and a note with no drawing embed loads none of it. 🔒 **OFFLINE**: the renderer must NEVER reach a CDN, and Excalidraw falls back to esm.sh whenever `window.EXCALIDRAW_ASSET_PATH` fails — so that path is set to the app's OWN copy of the package's asset folder (`app://yaseen/excalidraw-assets/`) BEFORE the module is imported. The copy is made at build time by `excalidrawAssets()` in `desktop/electron.vite.config.ts` (from node_modules into `out/renderer/`, and served from node_modules by a dev-server middleware) — never committed, and carried into the packaged app by electron-builder's existing `out/**`. Fonts are fetched at all only for scenes containing TEXT; a text-free scene asks for nothing. **REFRESH**: scenes are cached per target for the life of ONE editor mount; a `DrawingFeed` poke (`drawings/drawingFeed.ts` — the `WikilinkResolveSource` subscribe/poke idiom with no state, threaded as `drawingPreview.feed`) drops that target's entry and rebuilds the decorations, which re-read and re-render it. Since YAZ-879 `CrepeHost` creates exactly ONE feed per editor mount and gives it to both the preview plugin and the modal, so a save redraws every preview of that target in place — no remount, no reload, no document change. Without a feed previews still render, they just never refresh short of a remount. **CLICK**: a rendered preview calls `drawingPreview.onOpenDrawing(target)` and swallows the mousedown so the caret never lands in the match; Editor threads it to the modal (rule 28), and a mount that supplies neither wire keeps inert previews. CSS note: every rule in `drawingPreview.css` is scoped under `.milkdown`, because Crepe's own reset carries `.milkdown * { margin: 0; padding: 0 }` at equal specificity and loads later — an unscoped class silently loses all spacing. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` steps 2-4 (previews render and the raw text is not duplicated; a drawn scene WITH TEXT renders with its font inlined from a `data:` URL and **zero** http(s) requests leave the app; a corrupted sidecar reopens as the broken chip with the note still byte-identical).

28. **The drawing modal** (YAZ-879, fourth build unit of the Excalidraw embed YAZ-852, `src/drawings/DrawingModal.tsx` + `drawingModal.css` + `ExcalidrawSurface.tsx` + `saveDrawing.ts`): clicking a rendered preview opens the scene FULL SIZE, edits it, and Save writes the sidecar back. 🔒 **A MODAL, NEVER AN INLINE NODE VIEW**: the embed is plain text under decorations (rule 27), so there is no node to host an editor in, and a drawing canvas inside the text column would fight the caret for every key it owns. It is React BESIDE the editor — the `FolderPageContents` / `PageTitle` precedent: `CrepeHost` holds `openDrawing: string | null`, the preview's `drawingPreview.onOpenDrawing` sets it, the modal is keyed by target and unmounted (never hidden) on close. 🔒 **ONE ENGINE SEAM**: `ExcalidrawSurface` is the ONLY component that mounts `<Excalidraw>`, the only place a scene is serialized, and the only place "changed" is defined; the chrome — overlay, Save, dirty, discard strip, error line — imports nothing but that component and its types, so YAZ-868's swap to yaseendraw rewrites one file. The boundary is pinned as SOURCE in `DrawingModal.test.tsx` (a runtime assertion would pass with a second hidden import). Everything is LAZY: the module through `renderScene.ts`'s one `loadExcalidraw()`, the stylesheet through the surface's own dynamic CSS import — the entry chunk gains only the chrome's own few KB, and a note with no drawing loads neither. ⚡ Every prop handed to the engine is STABLE (memoized `initialData`, ref-backed `onChange`, module-level `UIOptions`): `<Excalidraw>` is memoized and calls `onChange` whenever it renders, so a prop rebuilt per render is an infinite loop (React #185, caught in e2e). The modal keeps the latest snapshot in a REF and only its VERSION in state, so a drag costs one render rather than one per pointer move. **LOAD / SAVE**: `openDrawing` (`drawingScene.ts`) reads through `api.readAsset` with the embed's RAW target and keeps the resolved ABSOLUTE path and the read `mtime`; the save goes to `api.writeAsset` at that explicit path (writes are never fuzzy, 🔒 YAZ-876) with that mtime as `expectedMtime`. 🔒 **THE CANVAS WINS**: `writeProperty`'s CONFLICT dance with the one difference a scene forces — the re-read fetches nothing but a fresh guard (two scenes cannot be merged and the user is looking at theirs), then writes once; a second CONFLICT throws. **SHAPE ON DISK**: the library's own `serializeAsJSON(…, 'local')` — so element cleanup and the `files` filter (an image paste survives) are its rules — with two of ours: `source` stays `yaseen-docs` (pinned as `window.EXCALIDRAW_EXPORT_SOURCE` before the import, beside the font path) and a trailing newline, so a file YAZ-877 created and this saved back differ only in what was drawn. After a successful write the modal pokes the `DrawingFeed` (rule 27) and closes. ⚡ **KEYS ARE THE OVERLAY'S, NEVER `window`'s** — the YAZ-888 lesson `ConfirmRename` learned and paid for in e2e: React flushes mount effects INSIDE the dispatch of the event that opened the component, so a `window` listener hears the very click or keystroke that opened it, and this modal is opened by a CLICK. The overlay takes focus on mount so Esc lands before the canvas exists. **LEAVING** is one gesture with three triggers (Esc, click-away, ✕): clean → close; dirty → an INLINE confirm strip under the title bar ("Discard drawing changes?" / Cancel / Discard), never a second sheet stacked over this one, and Esc while it is up answers the strip. **SAVE IS DISABLED WHEN CLEAN** — the first snapshot the surface emits (at mount, from the loaded scene, before the engine volunteers one) is the baseline, and `getSceneVersion` is the comparison. **FAILURES ARE INLINE, NEVER A DIALOG**: a sidecar that is missing, unreadable or not a scene opens as the error state (message + close, nothing to write — reachable only by a race, since rule 27's broken chip is inert), and a failed write leaves the modal open with its error line and the canvas untouched. **THEME** is App's already-resolved appearance read back off `<html data-theme>` (`lib/theme.ts` `appliedTheme`), once, at open: the setting lives in App and threading a prop down to one transient modal is plumbing the feature has not earned. Known gap for the polish pass: a theme change while the modal is open does not follow. End-to-end in `desktop/e2e/drawing.spec.ts` step 5 (a preview click opens the modal on a real canvas with Save disabled; the rectangle tool is picked on the engine's own toolbar and DRAWN with real mouse drags; Save writes an element into the sidecar — still `yaseen-docs`, still pretty-printed with a trailing newline — the in-page preview redraws from the feed's poke, and reopening the saved drawing is clean and Esc-closes, with the note byte-identical throughout).

29. **Heading folding** (YAZ-1140, `src/editor/outline/headingFolding.ts` + `.css`, keymap `headingHotkeys.ts`): the heading-shaped twin of rule 5, registered in `createCrepe()` right after the bullet plugin. Foldable = an H1–H3 outside every list whose SECTION — its following sibling blocks up to the next heading of the same or a shallower level, or the end of the parent (blockquotes are walked; lists are skipped whole: the bullet chevron owns that gutter) — is non-empty. Each gets a widget `<button class="heading-toggle" aria-expanded>` at the start of the h element's inline content — INSIDE the h element, so it inherits the heading font-size and one `height: 1.3em` rule centres the chevron on the first line at every level — and collapsing adds `data-heading-folded="true"` to every section block (CSS `display:none`; an inner collapsed heading's widget sits inside a hidden block, so nesting compounds for free). Toggles are metadata-only transactions stamped `'heading-fold'` (`viewActions.ts`): never `markdownUpdated`, disk and mtime untouched. Fold keys = `h:` + the rule-5 scheme (`getOutlineFoldKey`) — a third key space beside fold and zoom keys — persisted in the SAME `folders[root].folds` bucket: `Editor.tsx` splits by prefix on load and writes the union of both plugins' latest reports, so neither kind can wipe the other's keys and rename/delete remapping rides the existing store path unchanged. ⌘↑/⌘↓ fold/unfold the innermost section at the caret (declining inside lists — rule 7's bullet handler owns those; ⌘⇧U/⌘⇧I stay bullets-only in v1); ⌘Z joins the view-action protocol, which YAZ-1140 made SYMMETRIC: every view-action plugin (fold, zoom, heading fold) drops its pending panic-undo when any OTHER kind fires, so ⌘Z reverts the single latest view action and a second ⌘Z can never resurrect an older one. CMD+F reveals matches hidden by collapsed sections silently and re-collapses them on its own `revealedHeadings` books (`findInPage.ts`); the block handle mutes over the chevron (rule 15); the bullets-only outline editor computes zero entries and stays inert. End-to-end: `desktop/e2e/headingFold.spec.ts` — chevron → section hidden → `h:` key in `yaseendocs.json` → quit → relaunch → restored, with a bullet fold coexisting in the same bucket.

30. **Inline `<br>` ↔ hardbreak** (YAZ-1452, `src/editor/inlineBreaks.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` right after underline): a `<br>` (any spelling, any case) is the hardbreak node, not an `html` atom — inside table cells it is the only line break GFM has, and Milkdown's `remarkPreserveEmptyLinePlugin` used to delete every one of them on parse (data loss on the next autosave). 🔒 **ORDER IS THE MECHANISM**: our `$remark` plugin runs BEFORE Milkdown's, which is removed and re-registered AFTER ours — never dropped, because `shouldPreserveEmptyLine` resolves its id to keep writing `<br />` for empty paragraphs. **LOAD**: a `<br>` becomes `break`; in a prose paragraph a `<br>` with nothing after it is dropped (CommonMark has no trailing hard break; remark would write a stray `\`), which is also what keeps the lone `<br />` blank-line marker round-tripping. **SAVE**: a hardbreak inside `tableCell` is written as `<br>` (remark's default writes a space there); elsewhere remark's default `\` + newline — so `a<br>b` in prose normalises to the backslash form (rule 6). **CELLS SERIALISE THEMSELVES**: `table_cell` / `table_header` `toMarkdown` feed the cell's inline content directly instead of going through the paragraph runner, because that runner always drops a paragraph's LAST hardbreak (a trailing `<br>` in a cell decayed one per save) and wrote `<br />` for an empty cell; several paragraphs in one cell are joined by a break, an empty cell is written empty. **ENTER AND SHIFT-ENTER IN A CELL** are our shortcut (priority 90 — above Milkdown's 50, below the wikilink picker's and outliner's 100 so an open `[[` picker still takes Enter): both always insert a hardbreak, a new line in the cell as in Google Docs (🔒 YAZ-1462). Milkdown's own Shift-Enter refuses to run inside tables, and its "second Shift-Enter after a break makes a new paragraph" rule split the table. Mod-Enter is still Milkdown's `exitTable`. Contract test: `inlineBreaks.test.ts` (19 cases, one row copied verbatim from the reporting doc).

31. **Highlight** (YAZ-1480, `src/editor/marks/highlight.ts` + the shared `marks/htmlPairs.ts`): ProseMirror mark `highlight` with ONE optional attribute `color`. **On disk**: `color: null` (yellow) is Obsidian's `==text==` — a vendored micromark tokenizer (gfm-strikethrough's attention run with `=`, exactly two: a lone `=` is text, `===` cancels, `\==` escapes) plus stringify `unsafe` rules: any `=` touching another `=` is written `\=` (`a == b` → `a \=\= b`, reads back as the same text; a lone `=` never; URLs never; an odd run escapes one short, `a === b` → `a \=\== b`, still stable). `postProcessMarkdown()` restores `=` inside `[[…]]` so a wikilink target keeps its bytes. `==` on its own line under a paragraph is still a setext heading. Two tight runs on one line pair up (`x==5 and y==6` on disk highlights `5 and y`, as in Obsidian); the typing rule is stricter than the parser (no `=` inside, no word character before), so the same string TYPED stays text and saves escaped. `green`/`blue`/`pink` have no Markdown syntax and store inline HTML `<mark class="highlight-<name>">text</mark>` — rule 11's `<u>` mechanism through the shared pair walk. A bare `<mark>` is read as yellow and written back as `==…==` (rule 6 normalisation); any other `<mark class/style>` is not this mark and passes through as inline HTML, bytes untouched — on PASTE every `<mark>` becomes the mark (unknown class → yellow): a pasted `<mark>` is a highlight. 🔒 A highlight nested inside a highlight ENDS the outer one (Milkdown's `ParserState` keeps one mark set) — lossy on the first save, stable after; a highlight inside a DIFFERENT mark is unaffected. **Toolbar** (`buildToolbar` in createCrepe.ts): four swatches (yellow, green, blue, pink), dots filled with the wash token, added into Crepe's own `formatting` group after Strikethrough — `getGroup('formatting')` THROWS if Crepe ever renames that group; `headingToolbar.test.ts` pins it. 🔒 D5, Bold's toggle rule: a dot is LIT when ANY selected text carries its colour (at a caret: the marks it would type with); pressing a lit dot removes exactly that colour, an unlit one applies it over any other colour; edge whitespace stays outside the mark; where marks cannot apply (code) the command declines and the key falls through. A lit dot is painted as a pressed button (`--crepe-color-selected`). `setHighlightCommand(color)` is the one command behind the swatches, `Mod-Shift-h` (yellow; a `$useKeymap`, so Crepe's `keymapRef` labels the yellow dot and its `aria-keyshortcuts` from it) and the `==x==` typing rule (yellow). Copy-out emits `<mark>`. **Styling**: translucent washes `--mark-bg` + `--mark-bg-<name>` in `:root` (one value per colour, both themes), text colour inherited, selector `.milkdown .ProseMirror mark` so every Crepe surface paints it. Not a Crepe feature (allowlist untouched).

**YAZ-1289 editor-link amendment to rules 23–25:** the editor still mounts these plugins only for Markdown source notes. Rule 23 consults the separate view-only source only when the raw target has a recognized explicit text/PDF/image extension; missing/loading/removed targets and heading/block suffixes remain unresolved and never run create-on-click, while `|display` is presentation only. Rule 24 composes explicit-extension catalog candidates with the Markdown candidates before the same matcher/ranker; recognized view-only target spellings suppress unroutable semantic rows, but semantic aliases whose display text matches remain. `WikilinkResolveSource.resolve` and `.records` stay the original Markdown feed by identity, so rule 25 and every other semantic consumer remain Markdown-only.

30. **Images, first-class** (YAZ-1656 — decisions D1–D8 and the demo learnings are comments on that issue; `src/editor/image/`): `![alt](src)` RENDERS from the vault, a pasted or dropped image is SAVED to the vault, and the picture can be resized, opened full-size and copied. 🔒 **THE ONE NODE VIEW** (`imageView.ts`, registered in `createCrepe()` only when `opts.image` supplies the vault root + the note's absolute path): rules 23/27 forbid node views for wikilinks and drawing embeds because those are plain TEXT with no node to view; the commonmark `image` node ALREADY EXISTS, so a node view here changes no schema and no serializer and `![alt|300](src "t")` stays byte-identical (`roundtrip.test.ts`). The rule it establishes: a node view is allowed only on a node the schema already has and never touches the serializer. **SRC** (`imageSrc.ts`): anything with a scheme passes through untouched (`https:`, `data:`; a foreign `app://fs/…` simply 404s); a leading `/` is root-relative; everything else becomes `app://vault/<encoded root>/<ref, each segment decoded ONCE then encoded>?from=<note's root-relative dir>` — decode-once because a markdown src is already percent-encoded (Obsidian writes `%20`), so `case%20a.png` reaches main as `case a.png`, never `case%2520a.png`. Main (`vaultProtocol.ts`) resolves note-relative → root-relative → shortest-path basename, images only. **STATES**: loading is quiet; broken (missing, foreign, escaped, non-image) is a small INERT chip "Broken image: <src as written>" with the markdown still editable — nothing is repaired or rewritten. **WIDTH** is Obsidian's `![alt|400](src)` — alt is already preserved byte-for-byte, so no serializer work — parsed for display (`parseAlt`; `a|b` is not a width) and written back by the resize. **RESIZE**: eight handles (four corners, four edge midpoints), every one WIDTH-ONLY so the aspect ratio can never break (only a width is stored): west handles grow leftward, north/south map through the aspect ratio, a corner follows the axis that moved more; clamp `MIN_WIDTH` 40 px … the editor column; the width is live on the `<img>` during the drag and ONE `setNodeMarkup` on mouseup writes `alt|<w>` (⌘Z is one step); Escape mid-drag cancels (capture-phase, so the editor's Escape→sidebar hand-off never hears it), `window` blur ends the gesture where it stands, a plain click commits nothing, read-only editors have no gesture. `update()` patches width/alt/title in place and rebuilds the `<img>` only on a src change; `ignoreMutation` answers false to PM's `selection` pseudo-mutation. **LIGHTBOX** (`ImageModal.tsx`, rule 28's shape): double-click → a full-window overlay of the FULL-resolution file (`95vw/95vh`, `object-fit: contain`, alt as caption); Esc / backdrop / ✕ close; keys are the overlay's (YAZ-888); focus returns to what had it. **PASTE / DROP** (`insertImage.ts`, wired in `clipboardPaste.ts` through the `imageOptionsCtx` slice, D6): image BYTES on the clipboard or in a drop beat whatever HTML rode along — a "Copy Image" from another app carries the PNG and an `<img src>` only that app can read, and the old behaviour kept the private src and lost the bytes (the reported bug). The file is written FULL-RESOLUTION, never re-encoded, to `assets/images/<note, whitespace → '-'>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.<ext from mime>` with `create: true` — never overwritten, a same-second collision retries `-2`, `-3`… — the sibling of `assets/drawings/` and root-relative so moving a note never breaks the link; the inserted node is `![<name>|400](assets/images/…)`, the `|400` (`PASTED_IMAGE_WIDTH`) only when the bitmap is wider than 400 px, so a screenshot lands at a manageable size and a 16 px icon is not blown up. Never in code (a fenced block is a CodeMirror node view that owns its own paste anyway); a drop over code or outside the document is REFUSED rather than passed on, because the next handler is Milkdown's upload plugin, which would inline the file as base64; a selected image becomes a caret AFTER itself before the insert (paste adds, never replaces); the first image file wins when several are dropped; any failure is the host's passive notice and nothing is inserted. HTML with a foreign `<img src>` and NO bytes is left alone (renders the broken chip; foreign URLs are never fetched, nothing is silently deleted). **WHERE IT IS NOT**: the folder-page outline editor (its bullets-only lock has no `image` node — wiring options there would write the file and then filter the insert away) and the hover preview card (no vault root in reach) get no `image` options and keep Crepe's stock `<img>`, both commented where they mount. **DEFERRED** (YAZ-1673): `![[img.png]]` embeds (zero in the vault, paste never produces them, would need a second decoration renderer) and a per-vault attachment-folder setting (free later: links are root-relative). End-to-end: `desktop/e2e/imagePaste.spec.ts` and `imageRender.spec.ts` (fixture PNGs encoded at test time by `imageFixtures.ts`).

## View modules

TOMBSTONE: this heading was "Bases modules" until YAZ-858.

Everything under this heading is a FOLDER-PAGE internal since YAZ-844 retired `.base` (Editor rule 19's tombstone). The name no longer stays: YAZ-858 renamed the machinery to match what it serves — `client/src/bases/` → `client/src/views/`, `baseFile.ts` → `viewSchema.ts`, `BaseView` → `ViewsPane`, `.base-*` → `.view-*` — a mechanical rename with zero behaviour change. The property index, the query engine, the view chrome and the cell editors were still BUILT for Obsidian Bases and were not rewritten — they were re-pointed, then re-named. Where an entry below still says "base", it is a tombstone naming something the format took with it (or a stored key kept for compatibility, see `baseGroups` under App state); the surface that mounts them is "The contents block", and the config they read is "Folder page settings". Nothing here reads or writes a `.base` file any more, and no entry may describe one as current.

### Properties (YAZ-835, under the LOCKED rulings of YAZ-816 — `<root>/.yaseendocs/properties.json`, succeeding the Bible A registry)

**What it is**: vault-wide property DECLARATIONS — the ONE registry concept that survived the type system's deletion (YAZ-836). `{ version: 1, properties: { <name>: { kind, target?, required? } } }`, keyed by BARE frontmatter key — never a canonicalised `note.x`. `kind` ∈ `PROPERTY_KINDS` = text / number / date / checkbox / list / link / multi-link (`shared/types.ts`), `target` is a link/multi-link column's picker constraint (an opaque string here; its meaning is `belongsToBasenames`' — see "Folder page settings": The belongs-to picker), `required` is report-only metadata that gates nothing, exactly as it did in the registry. Read and written in main through the vaultConfig plumbing (`desktop/src/main/properties/`, Electron-free like the vault index); the IPC half is `desktop/src/main/ipc/properties.ts`.

**The version rule, unchanged from the registry** (LOCKED, R2.5): `version: 1` is readable AND mutable; a numeric `version > 1` reads best-effort (known fields consumed, no `error`) but refuses every mutation — writing a v1 shape over a newer file would destroy fields this version does not know. An ABSENT file is empty declarations with NO error, and reading NEVER creates anything: the file is LAZY, the first successful mutation writes the `{ version: 1, properties: {} }` skeleton along with the dotfolder. A MALFORMED file (unparsable JSON, non-object root, missing/non-numeric `version`) reads as empty + an `error` string, and every mutation rejects `INVALID_CONFIG` — the file is never overwritten and never moved aside, deliberately unlike `store.ts`'s `.corrupt-<epoch>` policy: app state is disposable, the user's schema is not.

**Surface**: `getProperties(root)` / `setProperty(root, name, decl)` / `removeProperty(root, name)` / `subscribeProperties(root, listener)`, over channels `properties:get` / `properties:set-property` / `properties:remove-property` / `properties:changed` (`desktop/src/channels.ts`), reaching the renderer as `window.yaseenDocs.properties.get/setProperty/removeProperty/onChange` (bridge table above). Targeted mutators, never a whole-file PUT (the `StateApi` anti-clobber shape); every mutation is a serialised per-root read-modify-write (the store's write-chain idiom, so two can never interleave reads) that preserves unknown fields at every level for forward compat, and removing an unknown name is a no-op that never creates the file. `subscribeProperties` re-reads through the SAME chain, so notifications deliver in order; main holds ONE subscription per open-vault root (`AppState.windows`, dropped with the last window on it) and broadcasts the fresh declarations to EVERY window, which filter by their own root.

**Validation** (strict at the IPC boundary — a write is config, not content): names must match `PROPERTY_NAME` `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$` (snake_case, ONE definition in `shared/types.ts` so no mirror can drift), a declaration must be an object whose `kind` is in `PROPERTY_KINDS`, `target` a string and `required` a boolean — everything else `BAD_REQUEST`. Reading is deliberately laxer than writing: an unknown on-disk `kind` string stays on disk and reads back as `text`. `page_type` is now an ORDINARY name that declares like any other (`properties/index.test.ts`) — the identity rule that reserved it died with the type system.

**The clean break** (LOCKED, YAZ-835): `.yaseendocs/types.json` is NEVER read. No migration, no converter, no read-old-write-new — a vault carrying one simply starts with no declarations, and the file is left untouched on disk. Pinned by a test: *"an old types.json is simply not read — properties.json is the only file this module knows"* (`desktop/src/main/properties/index.test.ts`).

**Not ours** (TOMBSTONE, ⚡ YAZ-815 — ruled by Yasin): `.obsidian/types.json` is another app's file — a different folder, a different owner, a different shape — and we no longer touch it AT ALL. It used to be read fresh per `index(root)` call (never cached), ride `IndexResponse.types` and hold a rung in the typing ladder one BELOW our own declarations; nothing ever consumed that rung, because the one surface that mounts cell editors reads the window's link snapshot instead of fetching an index. A stale foreign artifact feeding a rung with no feed is worse than no rung, so the whole chain — the read, the field, the parameter and the rung — is deleted. `.yaseendocs/properties.json` is the vault's schema; we never wrote `.obsidian/types.json` and now we never read it either.

Files: `desktop/src/main/properties/index.ts`, `desktop/src/main/properties/index.test.ts`, `desktop/src/main/ipc/properties.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/useProperties.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/propertiesStub.ts` (+ test — the in-memory `PropertiesApi` the client tests install as `window.yaseenDocs.properties`), `shared/types.ts` (`PROPERTY_KINDS`, `PROPERTY_NAME`, `FOLDER_NAME`, `PropertyDecl`, `PropertiesResponse`, `PropertiesApi`).

### Property index (GRO-2127 contract, GRO-2128 index — now in the Electron main process)

The query engine behind a folder page's views reads notes through `IndexRecord`, never the filesystem. Transport: bridge method `index(root)` (Desktop D10), no HTTP anywhere — the index (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/`) is transport-agnostic (tests call `getIndex` directly); it moved, not rewrote, from the retired server process (GRO-2157).

Files: `shared/types.ts` (`IndexRecord`, `IndexResponse`), `shared/frontmatter.ts` (`splitFrontmatter` — moved from the client, which re-exports it — and `parseFrontmatter`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/scan.ts` (`scanFile` + pure `extractAliases` / `extractTags` / `extractLinks` / `extractEmbeds`), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/live.ts` (`getIndex`, cache + watcher), `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/index.ts` (barrel), `client/src/views/testRecords.ts` (`TEST_RECORDS`).

`IndexResponse { root, records: IndexRecord[], generatedAt }` — `records` is every `.md`/`.markdown` file under `root` (dot-entries and `node_modules` skipped, exactly like `tree(root)`), sorted by path. Images and everything else are never records — nor were `.base` files while the format existed (YAZ-844), which is why retiring it cost the index nothing: no field, no `CACHE_VERSION` bump, no rescan.

| `IndexRecord` field | Value |
|---|---|
| `path` | absolute |
| `name` / `basename` / `ext` | `VSL-v1.md` / `VSL-v1` / `md` (`md` or `markdown`, lower-case, no dot) |
| `folder` | root-relative, `/` separators, `''` at the root (`Content Pillars/1. Agentic Agency`) |
| `size` / `mtime` / `ctime` | bytes / mtime ms / birthtime ms (ctime ms where the platform has no birthtime) |
| `properties` | frontmatter parsed with yaml's default **core** schema: `null`/booleans/numbers typed, dates stay strings (`date: 2026-08-01` → `'2026-08-01'`), `pillar: null` is present with value `null`. `{}` when there is no frontmatter, the block is not a map, or YAML fails — the latter two also set `frontmatterError` (one-line yaml message or `frontmatter is not a map`). Files over `MAX_FILE_BYTES` are stat-only (`{}`, empty lists, no error). The reserved `comments` key is DELETED right after the parse (YAZ-1472, 🔒 D5 — a note's comment stream is content, not a property), so it never reaches a record, the cache, a view column or a link source; see "Comments". |
| `aliases` | frontmatter `aliases` — extra NAMES the note answers to (Links E2, GRO-2214): a list → each string item; a scalar string → ONE alias (never comma-split, the deliberate contrast with `tags`); trimmed, empties and non-strings dropped, de-duplicated (first appearance). Absent / non-string → `[]`. Only the `aliases` key (Obsidian's), never `alias`; nothing is stripped from a value, so `aliases: ["[[X]]"]` is an alias literally named `[[X]]`. |
| `tags` | frontmatter `tags`/`tag` (list → each string item; string → split on commas/whitespace; non-strings ignored; leading `#` stripped) followed by inline `#tags` in order of appearance. Inline rule: `#` preceded by start of text, whitespace or one of `( [ , ;`, then `[A-Za-z0-9_/-]+` with at least one non-digit (`#123` is not a tag), case preserved, nested `a/b` kept whole. Never inside fenced code (``` or ~~~), inline code spans or `http(s)://…` runs. De-duplicated, first appearance wins. |
| `links` | `[[target]]` targets with `\|alias`, `#heading` and `#^block` stripped and trimmed — frontmatter string values (top-level and inside lists) that are exactly `[[…]]` first, then the body. Embeds are excluded, and so is everything under the `aliases` key whatever it looks like (GRO-2214: an alias is one of this note's own NAMES, never an outgoing link). De-duplicated. |
| `embeds` | `![[target]]` targets from the body, same stripping, de-duplicated. |

Tags, links and embeds share one code-stripping pre-pass (`stripCode` in `scan.ts`): nothing inside a fenced code block (``` or ~~~) or an inline code span counts, matching Obsidian's metadata cache; `http(s)://…` runs are additionally ignored for tags only. Frontmatter values are never code-stripped.

`TEST_RECORDS` (`client/src/views/testRecords.ts`) is the 8-note `bases-fixture` as records rooted at `/vault` with `size`/`ctime`/`mtime` zeroed; `desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/live.test.ts` deep-equals the live scan to it, so client query tests and the main-process index are pinned to one truth.

Cache lifecycle (`getIndex(root)`):
1. First call: walk the root (`isSkipped` pruning, markdown only, 32 reads in flight), `scanFile` each file, keep a `Map<path, IndexRecord>`; concurrent first calls for one root share the single scan. A missing / non-directory root rejects with the same `BridgeFailure` codes as the fs bridge (`NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`, `FORBIDDEN`).
2. The root's shared chokidar watcher (`watchers.subscribe`) keeps the map live: `add`/`change` on a markdown path → re-`scanFile` and replace (a file that vanishes before the scan is dropped), `unlink` → delete, `unlinkDir` → delete every record under it; `addDir`/`ready`/`error` are no-ops. No rescan is ever needed.
3. Every call FIRST drains the watcher scans still in flight (YAZ-986: the answer is the truth as of the ask — a just-created file can never miss the snapshot its own `add` event provoked, which was YAZ-958's invisible-newborn bug; `liveDrain.test.ts` pins it by holding a scan open across a call), then returns the map sorted by path with a fresh `generatedAt` and resets a 10-minute idle timer; on expiry the watcher subscription is released and the map dropped (the next call rescans). Test hooks: `_evictAll()`, `_setIdleMs(ms?)`.

Persistent cache (GRO-2223, decisions D1–D4 — write GRO-2228, load GRO-2229): the record map additionally persists as one JSON per vault in `app.getPath('userData')/index-cache/<first 16 hex of sha256(root)>.json` (`desktop/src/main/vaultIndex/cache.ts` — Electron-free like `store.ts`: `main/index.ts` injects the dir via `initIndexCache`; the vault itself is never written, and cross-machine sync of the cache is a non-goal — the reconcile makes a foreign cache harmless anyway). Payload `{ version: CACHE_VERSION, root, records: IndexRecord[] }` (the constant is **3** today — see the load rules below); records whose frontmatter cannot round-trip through JSON (non-finite numbers — YAML `.inf`/`.nan` — or self-referential alias cycles) are left out of the write and simply rescan on the next start; the rest of the vault still caches. Persists are debounced ~5 s per root (`schedulePersist` — called from the watcher-incremental mutations, build success, and idle eviction as a natural last flush point) and land through the same `atomicWrite` as everything else, chained per root so writes never reorder; `flushIndexCache()` writes every pending root at once and joins `store.flush()` in the `before-quit` chain. The payload has ALWAYS been exactly those three keys and nothing else — pinned by `liveCache.test.ts` ("the persisted payload is version/root/records and nothing else"). `.obsidian/types.json` never entered it even while it was read per call, which is why ⚡ YAZ-815 deleting that read needed no invalidation and no CACHE_VERSION bump. GC (GRO-2230): `initIndexCache` sweeps the dir once per launch — ANY file (cache JSONs and crashed `.tmp-` leftovers alike) whose mtime is older than 90 days (`GC_MAX_AGE_MS`) is deleted, so vaults never reopened stop accumulating dead files. Every vault open refreshes its file's mtime (build success schedules a persist), so only truly abandoned entries age out, and a wrongly deleted cache is self-healing — exactly one full rescan. Deliberately minimal: no size caps, no LRU, no eviction policy of its own.

THE INVARIANT: the cache is only an accelerator — a wrong, missing, stale or corrupt cache can never produce wrong data. `loadIndexCache(root)` never throws; it answers `{ records, status }` with status `hit`, or degrades: `miss` (no file, or the payload names another root — a hash-prefix collision or a copied cache dir is simply not this vault's cache), `corrupt` (unparsable JSON, wrong shape, or any structurally malformed record — one bad element poisons the whole file), `version-mismatch` (numeric `version` ≠ `CACHE_VERSION`, now **3** — E2 added `aliases` to the record shape, GRO-2214; YAZ-1472 dropped `comments` from `properties`, see "Comments"). On build, `reconcile(root, files, load)` (`reconcile.ts`) turns the load into the live map: a non-hit is exactly today's full `scanAll`; on a hit it stat-sweeps every walked-and-cached file and reuses a cached record only when path + mtime + size all match (D2 — the record is its own validation key), `scanFile`-ing anything new or differing and dropping per-file stat/scan errors silently (same semantics as `scanAll`). The sweep is a sync `statSync` loop (~27 ms at 10k notes) and the cache load happens before `subscribe`, because the fresh watcher's initial scan floods the 4-thread libuv pool right at build time — async stats queue FIFO behind it and drain at the watcher's pace (~10× slower measured); warm 10k start lands ~99 ms vs ~1040 ms cold (GRO-2229 bench). A corrupt cache file is ignored IN PLACE (one `console.warn` at build) and overwritten by the next persist — deliberately unlike `store.ts`, which moves corrupt user state aside as `.corrupt-<epoch>` for forensics: the cache is derived data whose source of truth is the vault itself, so a corrupt copy holds nothing worth keeping, and move-aside would only accumulate junk for the GC to learn about (GRO-2230). RULE: any change to `IndexRecord`'s shape or the scanner's extraction semantics MUST bump `CACHE_VERSION` (`cache.ts`) — an old snapshot must never be read as if it had the new semantics; discard IS the migration (a mismatch degrades to one full rescan, there is no converter), and the fingerprint pin in `cache.test.ts` fails on such a change until the bump lands.

The reconcile also emits the cold-start diff for E1c rename detection (GRO-2242): `getColdStartDiff(root)` → `ColdStartDiff { root, scannedAt, cacheStatus, added, removed, changed }` (= the shared `ColdStartDiffResponse`, shipped to the renderer verbatim as the bridge's `coldDiff(root)`), held on the live entry and dropped with it on idle eviction. `added` carries ON-DISK stats and `removed` the CACHED stats — their (size, mtime) join is the rename signal; `changed` lists paths whose mtime or size moved. Miss semantics are honest: on any `cacheStatus` other than `hit` there is no before-snapshot, so all three lists are EMPTY (never "everything added") and `cacheStatus` says why — consumers must check `cacheStatus === 'hit'` before trusting them. Test hooks: `_resetIndexCache()`, `_setPersistDebounceMs(ms?)`, `_gcDone()`.

### Engine (GRO-2132 function library, GRO-2133 filters / sort / group / limit, GRO-2134 summaries)

`client/src/views/engine.ts` — `runView(def, view, records, { thisFile?, root?, resolve? }) → ViewResult { rows, groups, summaries, errors, total }`, pure and DOM-free, never throws. `RunOptions.resolve` (🔒 D2, YAZ-819) is the resolver every link in the run resolves through: absent, the engine builds one from `records` — which is the vault whenever the rows ARE the vault — while a folder page's contents pass only the MEMBERS as rows and inject the FULL-VAULT resolver, so a link cell pointing outside the members still resolves ("The contents block"). Pipeline: filters → values → sort → limit → group → summaries. A record passes when the base-level `def.filters` AND the view's `filters` both pass; `and` / `or` / `not` nest (`not` = none of the children true); a leaf is truthy per `isTruthy` (an ErrorValue fails). Every filter string is compiled once (`compile()` cache); a compile error or a malformed node makes that whole filter reject every row and adds one `EngineError { where, message }` — `where` is `filters` / `views[i].filters`, with `[j]` per child (`views[0].filters[1][0]`). `Row { record, file: FileValue, values }` — `values` is keyed exactly as the view writes the key (`view.order`, else `propertyKeys`): `file.x` is the file field, `formula.x` evaluates the formula (a compile error → ErrorValue cell + one `EngineError` at `formula.x`; an unknown formula is the evaluator's `unknown formula x` ErrorValue with no report), anything else is the note property (`note.` prefix optional) through `fromYaml`. Sort: `view.sort` in priority order, type-aware (numbers, dates/durations by ms, strings `localeCompare` base-insensitive + numeric, booleans false < true, lists by first element, links by target, mixed types by rank), null / missing / ErrorValue always last whatever the direction, path as the final tiebreak. `limit` applies after sort and before grouping; `total` is the pre-limit count. `groupBy` → one `Group { key, label: render(key), rows, summaries, fannedOut }` per distinct key (`equals`), ordered by `groupBy.direction` with the same comparator; null / `''` / empty list / ErrorValue rows go to a trailing `{ key: null, label: 'No value' }` group that is always last; no `groupBy` → `groups: null`. **Two levels** (YAZ-745, YAZ-1097/1098): `groupBy` is an ordered LIST of levels, OUTER FIRST — `groupByLevels` reads the single-object form as a one-element list, so the old spelling stays valid and byte-identical in behaviour, levels past the second are ignored in v1, and a level with no `property` string is not one. With two, the outer level buckets exactly as above and each outer group is then bucketed AGAIN by the inner one: `Group.children` (its inner groups, ordered by the inner level's own direction; a child never has children of its own) and `Group.direct` (the rows sitting straight under the outer) appear on EVERY group of a two-level run — possibly empty, and absent entirely in single-level mode, which is how a consumer tells the two apart — while the outer's own `rows` stay the WHOLE branch, `direct` plus every child's rows, so counts and summaries above it are unchanged. **The merge rule**: an inner key that `equals` its outer's is no child at all, its rows are `direct` — grouping problems by top-function then function, a function that IS its own top shows its rows straight under the section instead of under a header repeating the name — and a "No value" OUTER puts its value-less rows in `direct` too, so it never carries a "No value" child. Everything else applies PER LEVEL and last within its own branch: each outer gets its own trailing "No value" child, and fan-out stays one flag per level (YAZ-671's semantics unchanged — one list anywhere inside marks every inner group). **Fan-out** (YAZ-671 D1): a LIST value joins the entry to one group per DISTINCT non-empty element (`equals`, no resolver — so link identity is the exact target, and two spellings of one note stay two groups), never a combination group; empty elements drop and a list left with none falls to "No value" like `[]` already does. `Group.fannedOut` is true on EVERY group of such a grouping (the "No value" group included) and false otherwise — it is what the group-derived writes read. A fanned-out row is counted in each group it joins, so per-group summaries can sum ABOVE `total` while the footer stays de-duplicated over `kept` (D2, deliberate). `groupKeyOf` keys shift from the rendered list to the rendered element, so collapse state persisted against an old combination group simply never matches again — orphaned, harmless, no migration. `this` is the `FileValue` whose path equals `opts.thisFile`, else null. `propertyKeys(def, view, records)` is `view.order` if set, else `file.name` + every note key seen (sorted, as `note.<key>`); `propertyLabel(def, key)` is `def.properties[key].displayName` (looked up as written, bare and `note.`-prefixed) else the key without `note.`. Budget: 1000 records × 3-clause filter + 2 formulas + sort + groupBy under 50 ms (`engine.perf.test.ts`, the `perf` project) — a SECOND grouping level fits inside it (two levels measured at 4.1 ms), which is why the scalar path stayed a single lookup rather than wrapping every value in a throwaway list.

Link resolution (`makeResolver(files, root?, opts?)`, also `Scope.resolve`): a target matches by absolute path, then root-relative path (with or without `.md` / a leading slash), then bare basename — duplicate basenames resolve to the SHALLOWEST folder (Obsidian's shortest-path rule, GRO-2190), equal depth to the first in path order — and finally, LAST, a frontmatter ALIAS (Links E2, GRO-2214): a real name always beats an alias, whatever their depths, and two notes claiming one alias follow the same shallowest-then-first-in-path-order rule. An alias is a bare NAME, so it never joins a folder path (`[[Costs/CAC]]` does not resolve through the alias `CAC`). Case-insensitive throughout, `[[…]]` / `|alias` / `#heading` stripped. `opts.aliases: false` builds a NAME-ONLY resolver — the rename engine's referencing-set probe (see "Links": Rename), the one consumer that must NOT see aliases. `resolverFor(records, root?, opts?)` is the memoized accessor (WeakMap keyed on the records array identity, then per root and alias mode): everything reading the SAME snapshot gets ONE resolver — a refetched index is a new array and gets a fresh one; the per-resolver target cache is unchanged. Precisely (GRO-2197 audit — the earlier wording overclaimed; amended by YAZ-819/844): the resolver is one IMPLEMENTATION everywhere and the wikilink surfaces genuinely do share one INSTANCE, because `WikilinkIndexBridge` is the window's single index feed for decorations, the picker and backlinks alike (rules 23/24/25). The three surfaces that used to duplicate that feed — `BaseHost`, `BaseEmbed` and `BaseCodeBlock`, each with its own `useIndex` and therefore its own snapshot array and memo entry — died with the format (YAZ-844), and the ONE surface that runs views today reads the window's `WikilinkResolveSource` instead, so `useIndex` has exactly ONE caller left (`WikilinkIndexBridge`) and one window makes one fetch: the duplicated FETCHES are closed. The duplicated INSTANCE is not, and the reason is worth knowing — the wikilink feed built `resolverFor(records, root)` while the contents block and its outline built `resolverFor(records)` with no root, and the memo is keyed per records identity THEN per root and alias mode — so the same snapshot handed out two resolvers, and a link target written as an absolute `<root>/…` path resolved for the wikilink surfaces but not inside a folder page's views. CLOSED in YAZ-846: the root is threaded into every folder-page resolver (`ViewsPane`'s injected `RunOptions.resolve`, `OutlineView`'s click-rule resolver, and the belongs-to picker's in `TableView` / `CardsView` / `ListView`), so one window on one snapshot now holds ONE resolver instance and one set of semantics. A null root — a mount that does not know one — still degrades to name-and-relative-path resolution, which is the same resolver the engine would have built for itself. It backs the global `file(path)`, `link.asFile()`, and tightens `file.hasLink()`, `link.linksTo()` and link `==` to compare resolved paths — an unresolved side (e.g. `levels.png`, not a note) falls back to the textual `linkTargetsMatch`. Without a resolver `file()` / `asFile()` stay ErrorValues and matching is textual. List helpers `sum() mean() median() min() max()` ignore non-numbers and give null on none.

`client/src/views/summaries.ts` — `summarize(kind, values, custom?)`: built-ins (`BUILTIN_SUMMARIES`, case-insensitive) `Count` / `Empty` / `Filled` / `Unique` on any type (`isEmpty`, `equals`), `Average` / `Max` / `Median` / `Min` / `Range` / `Stddev` (population) / `Sum` on numbers (non-numbers ignored, none → null), `Earliest` / `Latest` / `Range` (→ duration) on dates, `Checked` / `Unchecked` on booleans; a non-built-in kind is looked up in `custom` (= `def.summaries`) and evaluated with `values` bound to the column (`Scope.extra`), else `ErrorValue('unknown summary kind: …')`. `runView` fills `result.summaries[prop]` over the post-limit rows and `group.summaries[prop]` per group from `view.summaries { prop: kind }`.

### View chrome (GRO-2135)

`client/src/views/ViewsPane.tsx` props: `{ parsed, onChange, root, thisFile, records, properties?, onOpenFile, folderPage }` — `root` keys view state persisted outside the page AND roots the resolver (see "Engine"), `thisFile` is the FOLDER PAGE's own path (it roots `this` in expressions AND the outline's ancestor guard), `records` is the MEMBERS, `properties` is the vault-wide declarations for 5B editor inference (typing rung 2, wired in YAZ-846; a `properties.error` renders its own passive "Could not load the vault's property declarations" line, never a fetch failure), and `folderPage` is the `FolderPageMode` bundle the contents block passes (settings + whole-vault snapshot + create + background-open, 🔒 D3 of YAZ-818 — see "The contents block"), REQUIRED since YAZ-846. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846, the amputation): `readOnly` (the retired embed chrome, which also gated cell editing, column resize, summary picking and group drag in `TableView` / `BoardView` / `CardsView` / `ListView` — gone from all four), `initialView` (which picked the starting tab for `![[X.base#View]]`), `types` (typing rung 3, `.obsidian/types.json` — nothing on this surface ever fed it, and ⚡ YAZ-815 then deleted the rung, the parameter and the read), `indexStatus` / `indexError` (the host's snapshot is always in hand) and the plain 5D `createFromSeed` path all had NO production caller once YAZ-844 retired `.base`, and were deleted rather than carried. `newNote.ts`'s `targetFolder` went with that last one. **Write-through**: every config change is `onChange(updateViews(parsed, mutate))` (re-parsed via `parseViews(serializeViews(…))` so sequential edits inside collections a previous edit created keep working — `doc.setIn` stores plain JS values), exactly one `onChange` per interaction, no Save button; the folder-page host turns it into exactly ONE `folder_page_settings` write through the one door. `onOpenFile` is threaded App (`openFile`) → `Editor` → `FolderPageContents` → `ViewsPane`, and the host feeds `records` (the MEMBERS) plus the whole-vault snapshot off the window's `WikilinkResolveSource` rather than a `useIndex` of its own. Component state is transient view state only — the active tab, the search text, the collapsed group keys (mirrored into the main-owned `baseGroups` store, see 4C), the pending optimistic moves (5C) and the last move/create error — everything else lives in the file. The body switches on `view.type`: `table` → `TableView`, `board` (OUR schema extension, D1 — not Obsidian's, and its keys round-trip untouched) → `BoardView`, `cards` → `CardsView`, `list` → `ListView`, `outline` → `OutlineView` (YAZ-820), which still needs a non-null `thisFile` to root its ancestor guard and falls through to the placeholder rows without one, anything else (unknown types) → the 4A placeholder row list. One narrowing rides here: while an outline is showing the Properties menu is not offered (`noProperties`) — see "The contents block".

Toolbar (`view/Toolbar.tsx`, `.view-toolbar`): left `view/ViewTabs.tsx` — one `role="tab"` per view (type glyph + name, active tab accent underline). The tabs are EDITABLE (🔒 D0, YAZ-1471, re-ruling 🔒 rule 4 of YAZ-819 and the "view CRUD is not this block's gesture" clause of 🔒 D3 of YAZ-818 — the whole editable half was behind a `readOnly` prop `ViewsPane` set UNCONDITIONALLY from YAZ-846 until YAZ-1471, kept whole and pinned but unreachable; the prop is gone from `ViewTabsProps` and the "…" button never returned, right-click being the one menu gesture). EVERY tab gesture is ONE `ViewsPane.update` — the same `onChange(parseViews(serializeViews(updateViews(…))))` door sort and columns use — and therefore ONE `folder_page_settings` write; which view is ACTIVE stays session state and switching still writes nothing. **Reorder** (D1): HTML5 drag, the insertion-slot idiom spelled ONCE for both horizontal strips in `client/src/lib/dragSlot.ts` — `insertionSlot(e, i)` (the target's own horizontal midpoint: before it, or after it) and `dropIndex(from, slot)` (past the grab point the slot shifts one left) — shared with the window's tab bar so the two strips cannot drift apart; the strip's empty tail is the END slot (the `e.target !== e.currentTarget` guard), a drop back on the item's own slot writes nothing, the ACTIVE view FOLLOWS its tab (the same move replayed over the indices, never a remembered name), and the in-flight indicators are `view-tab--dragging` on the grabbed tab plus `view-tab--insert-before` / `view-tab--insert-after` on the slot's neighbour. The drag payload is the PRIVATE mime `application/x-yaseen-view-tab` (TabBar's `WORKSPACE_PAGE_MIME` idiom) and never `text/plain`, which a tab mis-dropped on the note body above would have ProseMirror insert as text; `dataTransfer` is guarded throughout for jsdom. **The view menu** (D2): right-click on any tab opens the shared `ContextMenuSurface` — Rename (the tab becomes an inline `TextField`, `normalize` committing only a non-empty name NO OTHER view answers to, so a duplicate or an empty draft snaps back and writes nothing, and a tab in rename is not draggable) · Duplicate (`structuredClone` of the whole config inserted right after, named by `freeName` off `<name> copy`, and selected — greyed for an `outline` view, because a page owns ONE document) · Delete (greyed on the last view — `views` can never be empty — and never immediate: `view/ConfirmDeleteView.tsx` is the in-app sheet, MIRRORING `ConfirmRemoveMember` byte for byte in shape, with the confirm `--danger` because a view's configuration has no way back, focus starting on Cancel, Esc and click-away cancelling; its copy is the pure `deleteViewMessage(view)`, tested apart). The open menu and sheet hold INDICES, so they are guarded against a list shrunk under them by another window or a hand edit (YAZ-1488): `menuView` / `confirmView` are derived in render and a stale index renders nothing rather than throwing through a block with no error boundary above it. **"+"** (D5): the button at the end of the strip opens an anchored `Popover` type picker over `VIEW_TYPES` (`table` · `board` · `cards` · `list` · `outline`, each labelled by `viewTypeLabel` — the type capitalised; `outline` is offered only while the page has NONE, for the same one-document reason) — picking a type APPENDS a view named `freeName(<label>)` and mounts it in rename, so the two writes are the append and (only if you type) the name. **Overflow** (D6): `.view-tabs` IS the scroller and wears the shared `.scroll-strip` utility (`app.css`) — no scrollbar, a scroll-driven edge fade — with the active tab `scrollIntoView`n on every activation and labels ellipsized at 180px; the "+" sits OUTSIDE the scroller in `.view-tabs-wrap`, always visible and never masked. Right (YAZ-1397): **Sort** (badge = sort count + grouping-level count), **Properties**, **Filter** (badge = `countRules(view.filters)`, button and badge red for filter errors), the **groups toggle** when grouped, **Preview on hover** for Table/Board, **New**, **Search** (inline input; filters rendered row values case-insensitively without writing), then `N items` or `shown / total items`. Existing document-only Sync and absent document controls retain their conditions. The groups toggle (YAZ-744) is ONE button with no badge: any group expanded → "Collapse all groups", all collapsed → "Expand all groups"; a click replaces the whole collapsed set for the view — every group the view HAS, the ones an active search hides included — through the same `baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]` entry the per-group chevrons write, never the folder page's own card. Popovers (`view/Popover.tsx`, `.view-popover`) anchor under their trigger, close on Esc / click-away, move focus in on open. `view/TextField.tsx` commits on Enter/blur only (one change per edit), Esc reverts.

The Filter menu (deleted in YAZ-846, RESTORED in YAZ-1218 / YAZ-1226-1229). `view/FilterMenu.tsx` is the builder behind the **Filter** toolbar button: conjunction All / Any / None → `and` / `or` / `not`, property · operator · value rule rows, an Advanced raw-expression input, and the engine's `*.filters` `EngineError`s listed at the top of the popover (`.view-menu__errors`) with the button turned red — the same errors still ride the muted footer note. Its rule ↔ expression model is the pure `view/filterRows.ts` (`ruleToExpr` / `exprToRule` / `inferType` / `operatorsFor` / `toGroup` / `fromGroup` / `countRules`), back beside `view/keys.ts`'s `canonicalKey` (`status` → `note.status`; `file.x` / `formula.x` / `note.x` untouched; non-identifier names as `note["…"]`). What did NOT come back, deliberately (D1, 🔒 Q3 below): the This view / All views scope segment — the menu edits `view.filters` ONLY, because a folder page's set IS the lookup and `def.filters` has no persistence door on this surface (a write would round-trip in memory and vanish on the next index rebuild). `inferType` now defers to the typing ladder (D4): the column's assigned kind, else its dominant kind, else the value walk — so the operator list always agrees with the cell editor beside it, `link` / `multi-link` columns included. Five operators take a value CHECKLIST instead of a typed box (🔒 D5, YAZ-1467): single-value columns (text, link, select, `file.folder`) offer **is any of** / **is none of**, written `["a", "b"].contains(<prop>)` and its `!` form — the list literal, never the string `containsAny`, because only the list method compares by equality; list columns (list, tags, multi-select, multi-link, `file.tags`) offer **has any of** / **has none of** / **has all of**, written `<prop>.containsAny("a", "b")`, its `!` form, and `<prop>.containsAll("a", "b")`. `file.links` stays out (link-vs-string equality unverified) and the engine needed no change. A `Rule`'s `value` is therefore `string | string[]`, and moving to or from one of the five resets it to `[]` or `''`. A stored node the builder cannot represent renders read-only as `.view-rule__code` and is never destroyed.

Sort menu (`view/SortMenu.tsx`): `view.sort` rows (searchable property chooser, ASC/DESC toggle, drag grip, remove — an empty list deletes the key) + "Add sort" (`file.name ASC`); beneath, **Group by** edits BOTH levels (YAZ-745): the first chooser is the outer level, a **Then group by** row beneath it (offered only once an outer is set, its options omitting the outer's own key) sets the inner — one level keeps today's single-object form, two write the ordered list, clearing the inner collapses back to the object, "None" on the outer clears both, and an outer moved onto the inner's property drops the inner (the same property twice is not a grouping); rendering of groups is 4C. Properties menu (`view/PropertiesMenu.tsx`): checklist over the shown keys (as written, so `view.order` round-trips) plus every other known key (`view/properties.ts` `allPropertyKeys`: shown + `file.name` + note keys + the folder page's DECLARED columns — YAZ-895, so a declared column is offerable before any member carries a value — + formulas, deduped canonically); toggling/reordering writes `view.order` (shown keys only, in order; Table and Board views may hide `file.name`, while Cards and List keep their existing filename/title behavior); reordering is the 6-dot grip at the head of every SHOWN row — `file.name` included, hidden rows gripless — dragged with TabBar's midpoint insertion rule or nudged one step via ArrowUp/ArrowDown on the focused grip, always exactly one `writeOrder` per move so `frozenColumns` keeps following positionally (YAZ-1207; the ↑↓ arrow buttons are gone); for `type: board` views every shown note row additionally carries four card-style toggles (YAZ-1206/YAZ-1217) — bold, underline, hide label, and ⤴ join-to-the-row-above — while the shown `file.name` row carries ONLY ⤴ (the title takes part in layout, never text styling); each click is ONE write to `view.cardStyle[<canonical key>]`, with false flags, emptied entries and an emptied map all deleting themselves; the pencil sets `def.properties[key].displayName` (looked up as written / bare / `note.`-prefixed, written bare for note keys; empty deletes the displayName and an emptied entry/map). A Table-only **Frozen columns** select writes optional `view.frozenColumns`: None deletes it; every other option names one visible column and freezes the contiguous leading prefix through it. The number is positional, never a remembered key set — reorder changes which columns occupy the prefix, restore/new columns append outside it, and hiding clamps and persists it. `frozenColumnCount` is the one tolerant reader: absent, malformed, negative or non-finite → zero; fractions truncate; the effective count never exceeds visible columns. A Board-only **Board → Column width** row is the ONE Board-wide numeric editor, with `px` visible beside it: 280 by default, `min=180`, step 1 and no arbitrary maximum. Only a CHANGED finite draft commits, on Enter or blur, rounded to the nearest integer and then lower-clamped to 180; Enter followed by blur still commits once. Normalized 280 deletes `cardSize`, while every other valid width persists as a NUMBER through ONE `folder_page_settings` write. Escape, empty or non-finite input, and an unchanged or normalized-unchanged value write nothing. The field displays existing numeric and legacy small/medium/large values through the unchanged `cardWidth` resolver, and open/focus/blur of an untouched value never rewrites it — including a legacy numeric width below 180. Columns are DECLARED here too (YAZ-896/897): every `note.*` row carries a kind select — `auto` (a disabled placeholder, never a choice) while undeclared, meaning the ladder's lower rungs decide — writing `folder_page_settings.columns[<bare>].kind` through `FolderPageMode.setColumns`, the declarations' one door beside `Mutate` (ONE settings write, `views` untouched; C1, LOCKED: member VALUES are never migrated or rewritten by a retype — the declaration alone moves, so retyping is always reversible); a declared link/multi-link column additionally shows its per-page `target` inline (empty deletes the key), and a trailing **+ Add column** (name per `PROPERTY_NAME`, kind, target for link kinds only, duplicates and bad names refused inline with nothing written) declares the column AND appends `note.<name>` to the view's `order` in that SAME single settings write — `setColumns`' optional `views` argument exists precisely so this is never two. The declaration's index echo then drives YAZ-999's separate missing-only member reconciliation (see "Declared columns are presence invariants"). For `type: list` views only, a trailing **List** section edits how those properties display — `markerStyle` (Bullet / Number / None select), `indentProperties` (checkbox) and `propertySeparator` (TextField, placeholder `, `) — one write per change, the default value DELETES the key (bullet / unchecked / empty-or-`, `), like SortMenu clearing `sort` / `groupBy`.

Searchable controls (YAZ-1395/1396): `ColumnSearch` matches trimmed, case-insensitive substrings across display labels, written keys and canonical keys. The Properties header stays visible while scrolling, reports match count and hides reorder grips during filtering (clear search to reorder). `ColumnPicker` reuses the search field for Sort, Group by and Then group by; its bounded list expands inside the existing menu, shows labels/keys and the selected option, and supports arrows/Enter, Escape, click-away and normal focus traversal. Typing, canceling or choosing the current canonical value does not write. Existing candidate sources remain: Properties includes declared folder columns; Sort keeps its existing shown/record/formula discovery and missing-current-key fallback. The Filter menu's Property is the same `ColumnPicker` (YAZ-1466), on top-level and nested-group rows alike, built from the shared `view/properties.ts` `propertyOptions` helper Sort also uses. The same component has a `multiple` mode (🔒 D6, YAZ-1467) — the Filter menu's value checklist (trigger labelled `Value`, like the typed box it replaces; `noun="options"` rewords the search box, its label and the no-match line — "Search options…", "No options found."): a click or Enter TOGGLES one value and writes once, the panel stays open until Escape or click-away, and a stored value no record carries still lists itself, ticked. `multiple` defaults off, so Sort's picker is untouched. Toolbar popovers constrain themselves to the viewport and clipping ancestors, resize with their content and allow internal option scrolling without dismissal.

Sort priority (YAZ-1396): drag the same six-dot grip used for columns, or press ArrowUp/ArrowDown while it is focused. The complete rule moves with its direction in one write; cancellation, same-position and boundary moves write nothing. Duplicate-property rules have distinct local UI identities that survive reparse after our edits and are never serialized. A changed external sort invalidates an in-flight drag. Single-rule grips are disabled. Grouping levels are not draggable.
