The compact sort grid uses a flexible property field, fixed direction width, equal control heights and a separate grouping section; only the new picker removes the native select width cap.

Bulk property visibility (YAZ-1384): Table and Board offer **Select all** and **Unselect all** above the Properties checklist when search is empty. During search, the labels become **Select results / Unselect results** and affect only matching canonical keys, preserving unmatched visibility and shown order. No matches disables both actions. Select all writes the menu’s canonical-deduplicated keys, retaining shown order and appending hidden properties, including declared columns and formulas. Unselect all writes explicit `order: []`, including hiding `file.name`; the existing `writeOrder` path removes frozen columns. Each effective click makes one active-view settings write; satisfied actions are disabled and dimmed. The menu remains available with no visible columns, and Board retains its blank card shells. Selecting all after clearing uses the default menu order and does not restore the previous custom order or frozen columns. Cards and List do not offer these actions.

Body (4A placeholder, view types with no real body yet): one row per result — `file.name` as a button calling `onOpenFile(record.path)`, then the other `order` values `render()`ed and ` · `-joined. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846, completed in YAZ-861): the two index states that used to replace the body whatever the view type — `.view-view__pending` "Loading the vault index…" and `.view-view__error` "Could not load the vault index: …" — went with `indexStatus` / `indexError`; the one host there is always has its snapshot in hand. The `__pending` class outlived them by one wave, holding a "This folder page has no views. [Add view]" branch that could not be reached — `readViews` and `folderPageViewSet` BOTH fall back to `DEFAULT_VIEWS`, so `def.views` is never empty — and YAZ-861 deleted branch, rule and class together, on the FilterMenu's precedent (below): unreachable UI with a live code path behind it is worse than no UI. `.views-pane__error` (renamed from `.view-view__error` with the pane) survives and still carries the create / properties / settings-write failures. A folder page with no members renders an empty body, which is the truth. Tests: `view/keys.test.ts`, `view/Toolbar.test.tsx` (mounts ViewsPane as a folder page's contents over `TEST_RECORDS` + a views fixture, `testFolderPage()` supplying the required bundle; asserts YAML via `serializeViews` next to the DOM). The tabs are pinned there in four describes (YAZ-1471): **drag to reorder** — a drop on a tab's LEFT half inserts before it in ONE write, the strip's empty tail lands it LAST with the last tab marked `--insert-after`, a drop back on its own slot writes nothing, the ACTIVE view follows its tab both when passed and when dragged, a tab in rename is not draggable, and the payload is the PRIVATE view-tab mime (a `text/plain` name would paste into the note body); **the right-click menu** — opening writes nothing, Rename refuses a duplicate or empty name (the field snaps back, nothing written) and commits a free one in ONE update on Enter OR blur (once, never twice), Duplicate clones after and opens the copy and is disabled for an outline, Delete is disabled on the last view and ASKS first (Escape and click-away both cancel and write nothing), confirming is ONE update, and the selection hands over to the right neighbour (the left one for the last tab); **"+"** — the picker lists every type in menu order and opening writes nothing, Outline is offered only while the page has none, picking appends in ONE update and mounts the tab in rename (Escape keeps the given name, typing over it is the SECOND update), and the name is the first FREE one off the type label; **overflow** — the ACTIVE tab is scrolled into view on mount and on every activation, and the tablist IS the scroller with the "+" outside it. `view/ConfirmDeleteView.test.ts` pins the sheet's pure copy apart from the sheet (`deleteViewMessage`: an outline names the document that goes with it, every other view its configuration). `FolderPageContents.test.tsx` drives the same arc through the real host: a toolbar change → ONE `folder_page_settings` write through the one door (the assertion `BaseHost.test.tsx`'s debounced-bridge-write test became when the format retired), switching a view writes NOTHING, a "+" on a page with no settings key writes the three defaults plus the new view once, a drag past the first tab writes the new order with the active view following, an open menu or sheet survives the views list shrinking under it (YAZ-1488), and the default view is pinned end to end (a saved START opens there and opening writes nothing, a stale name falls back to the first view silently, `Page → Default view` is ONE whole-key write carrying the name, `First view` clears the key, a rename CARRIES the saved START and a delete CLEARS it in the SAME write, and an external edit that changes ONLY `defaultView` rebuilds the def — D4's proof that it rides in the stamp). `folderPageSettings.test.ts` pins 🔒 D3's retirement from the read side: a persisted list WITHOUT a board reads back without one (what the card says is what you get, YAZ-1471), a persisted list WITH a board — whatever its name — passes through untouched, and `DEFAULT_VIEWS` is still three, outline first, each read handing back its own copy.

Table (4B, GRO-2136, YAZ-742, YAZ-1151, `view/TableView.tsx`, `.view-table`): `type: table` renders a `<table>` over the post-search rows — sticky header (labels via `propertyLabel`), columns = `propertyKeys` in `order`, widths from `view.columnSize` (px per key, default 150; drag the header's right-edge handle, min 60, live preview, ONE write on mouseup), `view.rowHeight` presets short/medium/tall → 28/44/68px via `--view-table-row-h`. The note's `.editor-host` remains the ONLY vertical scroller and `.view-table-wrap` remains the table's horizontal scroller. Because native sticky otherwise chooses that horizontal wrapper for both axes, `TableView` listens to the nearest vertical ancestor and translates the existing `<thead>` by `--view-table-header-y`: zero before the table reaches the viewport, the outer scroll delta while rows remain, and clamped at `table height − header height` so it exits with the table bottom. Scroll events coalesce into one animation frame; resize observation refreshes layout changes; unmount removes every listener, observer, pending frame and style. The first effective `view.frozenColumns` real cells in every header/body/summary row are CSS-sticky horizontally, with cumulative `left` offsets derived from those same live widths; resizing a leading column therefore moves every later frozen offset during preview without another state model. Header intersections sit above body cells, frozen cells have an opaque background, and the two axes compose because vertical motion belongs to `<thead>` while horizontal `left` offsets remain on its `<th>` cells; the header remains vertically sticky even when the frozen count is zero. Group headers and window spacers stay ordinary full-width `colSpan` rows — only real property cells freeze — so grouping, keyboard coordinates and vertical windowing keep one semantic table and their existing structure. Inside each group row, the complete compact controls cluster is horizontally sticky at the Table's left padding edge independently of `frozenColumns`; the spanning cell itself remains ordinary, never becomes a data/frozen cell, and is not vertically sticky. A TWO-LEVEL grouping (YAZ-745, YAZ-1100) is still ONE flat display list — an outer header, then its merge-rule `direct` rows, then one inner section per child, whose header cell carries `.view-table__group-cell--nested` — so windowing, `data-cell` coordinates and keyboard navigation are untouched, and collapse-all covers both levels in document order against the store's key-count cap. The inner indent is one 28px step applied to that cluster's sticky `left` (36px against the outer's 8px), NOT to cell padding, precisely because the cluster pins at `left` while the table scrolls sideways: padding would slide the inner label back onto the outer's offset at the right edge, which is the YAZ-1043-46 mechanism honoured once per level. Collapsing an outer folds its whole BRANCH, inner headers included, while the inner section's own collapse survives underneath it — its key being outer-scoped, see "The contents block". The `file.name` cell is a `.view-table__link` button → `onOpenFile`; other cells by type — numbers right-aligned (`.view-table__cell--num`), booleans as checkboxes, lists and links/files as chips (`.view-table__chip`, `--link`), ErrorValues as a red `#ERROR` chip with the message in `title`, everything else `render()` (editing: see "Cell editing" below). A secondary click on any non-editing data cell first focuses that exact cell, closes an open hover preview, then opens `PageContextMenu` for the row's exact absolute `record.path`; typed editors keep the native menu. With `view.preview: true` (YAZ-1244), resting on a data row opens that page's read-only body after the shared hover-intent delay; leaving uses the shared grace window, while drag start and secondary click close it immediately. Summary row pinned in `<tfoot>`: each cell is a click-to-pick kind (None + `BUILTIN_SUMMARIES` + `def.summaries` names) writing `view.summaries[columnKey]` (existing keys matched canonically; removing the last kind deletes the map), value shown via `summarize` over the shown rows. Arrow keys move focus between body cells (`data-cell="row:col"`), Enter on a name cell opens the note — ⌘⏎ a background tab, ⌥⏎ the right panel, the name link's click rule (YAZ-1557, `lib/openGesture.ts`); a BOARD card is the same kind of cell (🔒 D2): every card is `tabIndex=0`, a plain click selects it (the table cell's focus ring), arrows walk ↑↓ through the column's cards in DOM order (nested sections included) and ←→ to the same row of the neighbour column, clamped — DOM-driven off `:scope > .view-board__col`, not a parallel index, because a column is a tree and the DOM already is its reading order — and the title, Enter, or a modifier opens; a title-less card keeps `aria-label={record.name}` and has no role; one click selects an editable property cell and a double-click opens its editor. Above 500 rows only a scroll-positioned slice of `<tr>`s is mounted between spacer rows (no library); rows render from one flat slice so 4C can interleave group header rows. Tests: `view/frozenColumns.test.ts`, `view/TableView.test.tsx`, `view/TableNestedGroups.test.tsx`, and the dedicated `desktop/e2e/freezeColumns.spec.ts` (kept out of YAZ-999's shared folder-column spec) beside `desktop/e2e/nestedGroups.spec.ts` for the two-level render and its per-level sticky label.

**The Table since YAZ-1513** (the column overhaul, YAZ-1537–1551; header drag YAZ-1548; polish YAZ-1549): the `#` GUTTER is the first, always-sticky column (`.view-table__gutter`, fixed width, the frozen offsets starting after it) — 1-based and RESTARTING at every group header, nested sections included, a DISPLAY POSITION and never an id: no `data-cell` and no tabIndex (outside the arrow-key grid), not a drag source or target, nothing to summarise. `ViewDef.rowNumbers?: boolean` decides it, ABSENT = shown, so a shown gutter leaves no key behind — Properties → Table → **Row numbers**, or the `#` header's own right-click, whose only item is "Hide row numbers". The NAME cell renders the page TITLE — `record.basename`, no extension — in every skin through ONE shared renderer (`GroupHeader.tsx` `pageTitle`: the table's name cell, the board's card title, the card's title, the list's primary), while `file.name`'s VALUE keeps its extension for the engine, sort and filter; its label is `defaultLabel` = **Name**. A HEADER RIGHT-CLICK (`view/TableHeaderMenu.tsx`, on the row menu's own surface) offers **Rename column…** (an inline field holding the stored display name with the default label as its placeholder — Enter commits through `setDisplayName`, an emptied field goes back to the default, Esc cancels), **Hide column** (a table may hide `file.name`, YAZ-1007), **Add column to the right…** (the SAME "+ Add column" form, the new key landing after this one) and **Delete column…** (disabled with a tooltip for the undeletable tiers, otherwise the confirm sheet — "Declared columns are presence invariants"). HEADER DRAG (YAZ-1548) reorders a property header by dropping it on another header's left or right half — the Properties list's slot rule — and writes ONE order; the gutter is neither source nor target. `view/columnOrder.ts` `withOrder(view, order)` is THE one order writer, carrying the frozen clamp (YAZ-1007: the prefix is a count over the shown columns, so a shorter order clamps it and an empty order deletes the key): the Properties checklist, reorder and bulk buttons, "+ Add column", the header menu's Hide and Add-right, the header drag and a delete's prune all spell an order through it, so no view can hold an order its `frozenColumns` disagrees with. The PROPERTIES panel is TWO-LEVEL: the list row is grip · checkbox · type icon · label, the key shown only when two labels collide; opening a row is the DETAIL — an editable title (the label, the same `setDisplayName`), the key, the type, options (select / multi-select), the relation, card styling (Board only), "Back to columns", Hide in this view and Delete column…. Every declaration edit reaches disk through the host's doors ("The write path" under "Folder page settings"), and `FolderPageContents` keeps an optimistic AHEAD copy of `columns` — exposed as `FolderPageMode.settings.columns`, exactly as `parsed` runs ahead of the index for the views — so a declaration written and not yet echoed back is what the next gesture (a delete's prune, a second add) reads.

Board (4D, GRO-2138, YAZ-1177, `view/BoardView.tsx`, `.view-board`): `type: board` is OUR schema extension (D1) — the view keys are `type: board`, `groupBy` (required for columns), `order` (the card fields) and `cardSize`. The tolerant reader remains the shared `view/cardWidth.ts`: a finite positive number is px, legacy small/medium/large presets are 220/280/340, and anything else is 280. The Properties editor above is intentionally Board-only and writes numeric `cardSize`; it does not alter Cards' `cardSize`, Table's per-column widths, or the global content-width setting. Every outer column gets that one width through `--view-board-col-w`, so columns stay equal and nested child sections inherit their outer column's geometry; there is deliberately no per-column resize drag or preset picker. Every key round-trips byte-for-byte through the parser (`viewSchema.test.ts` board fixture) — a property that mattered for Obsidian interop while `.base` files existed and now simply means a hand-written board survives an edit to any other view. With ONE group level, columns remain the engine's post-search groups on one horizontally scrolling row (a column with no shown cards disappears, "No value" last). With TWO levels, there is still exactly ONE column per outer group; the outer's merge-rule `direct` cards render first, then its child groups stack vertically inside that column. Every outer and child header uses the shared `GroupHeader` content (chevron, typed value, count, summaries over that level's SHOWN cards). Outer collapse folds the whole branch; child collapse hides only that child's cards and persists under the outer-scoped `nestedGroupKeyOf` described above. Cards: when `file.name` is present in `order`, it renders as a `.view-board__title` button → `onOpenFile`; when omitted, the title/open button is omitted and an otherwise-empty order leaves blank draggable card shells; the other `order` properties render as small label/value rows (`propertyLabel` + the table's typed `cellContent` — shared from `GroupHeader.tsx`; a missing value keeps the label with an empty value). A secondary click anywhere on a rendered `.view-board__card` closes an open hover preview, then opens the shared `PageContextMenu` actions in the fixed order Open in new tab → Copy path → Reveal in Finder, always targeting that rendered row's exact absolute `record.path` — including hidden titles, direct/inner nested cards, and repeated fanned-out cards — while headers, add controls, placeholders, and the no-group hint retain the native menu; background open leaves the folder page active, and failed clipboard/reveal actions report through the passive notice. With `view.preview: true` (YAZ-1244), resting on any rendered card opens that page's read-only body after the shared hover-intent delay; leaving uses the shared grace window, while drag start and secondary click close it immediately. Per-property `view.cardStyle` (YAZ-1206, reshaped by YAZ-1217's JOIN model) lays those rows out: a card is `.view-board__line` rows following `view.order`, where each key starts a new line unless its `join: true` continues the line being built — appended right after a muted `.view-board__dash` element (never a schema flag); consecutive joins chain onto one line, join on the first rendered key is a no-op, and the title is UN-PINNED — `file.name` renders at its order position, can be joined onto and can itself join, while ignoring `bold`/`underline`/`hideLabel` (those class the note rows; `hideLabel` drops the label span). Because the flags ride the shared `cardList`, cards inside subgroup sections inherit them unchanged. A writable group header's `+` creates in that exact scope; a nested child seeds BOTH raw note properties, while `file.*` and `formula.*` levels remain read-only. Without `groupBy` the body is a centered `.view-board__hint` whose "Group by…" button writes the first non-file property as `view.groupBy { property, direction: ASC }` through the file (one write; the Sort menu edits it after). Cards drag at their visible level — direct cards between outer columns, child cards between child sections, including across outer columns. An inner target is never a descendant of the outer target, so one drop cannot bubble into a second move. A cross-outer child move updates both raw properties through ONE `writeProperties` whole-file transaction; the ordinary one-key path still uses `writeProperty`. Out of scope: a third grouping level, swimlanes and board card images. Tests: `view/BoardView.test.tsx`, `view/NestedGroupActions.test.tsx`, `writeProperty.test.ts`, and `desktop/e2e/nestedGroups.spec.ts`; card styles and the grip additionally in `view/Toolbar.test.tsx`, `desktop/e2e/boardCardStyles.spec.ts` and `desktop/e2e/propertiesReorder.spec.ts`.

Cards (4E, GRO-2139, `view/CardsView.tsx`, `.view-cards`): `type: cards` (Obsidian's schema; view keys `image`, `cardSize`, `imageFit`, `imageAspectRatio`) renders a responsive grid — `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(var(--view-card-w), 1fr))`, width from the shared `cardWidth` (Obsidian's numeric px, or the legacy small/medium/large presets). Each card: an optional cover, then `file.name` as a `.view-card__title` button → `onOpenFile`, then the view's other `order` properties as label/value rows (`propertyLabel` + the shared typed `cellContent`). The view's `image` names a per-note property; the note's VALUE decides the cover — `#rgb`/`#rrggbb` → a colour block, `http(s)://` → the URL straight into `src`, a wikilink `[[pic.png]]` or plain path → a local vault asset through `api.readAsset` (bridge table above) turned into a `data:` URL, cached per root+ref so a grid never fetches one cover twice. While loading, on any failure, or with no/an unresolvable value the cover is the neutral `.view-card__cover--empty` placeholder (never a broken image); only note properties resolve (`file.`/`formula.` image keys → placeholder); a view without `image` renders no cover element at all. `imageFit` cover|contain and `imageAspectRatio` (number, default 1:1) land as `--view-card-fit` / `--view-card-ratio` on the grid. Grouped results render 4C sections (the shared `GroupHeader` over each group's own grid) with the SAME persisted collapse state as the table/board (`baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]`, never the folder page's own card); search narrows cards and drops empty groups. A lightbox is out of scope; property editing: see "Cell editing". Tests: `view/CardsView.test.tsx`, `desktop/src/main/fs/assets.test.ts`.

List (4F, GRO-2140, `view/ListView.tsx`, `.view-list`): `type: list` (Obsidian's schema; view keys `markerStyle`, `indentProperties`, `propertySeparator`) renders one item per record. The FIRST property in `order` is the primary line (Obsidian: the primary list item is whatever sits on top of the Properties menu): `file.name` — the default when `order` is empty or absent — renders as a `.view-list__title` button → `onOpenFile`; any other first property renders its typed value (`cellContent`) as `.view-list__primary` and file.name is NOT implicitly added. The remaining `order` properties render inline after the primary — `render()`ed, empty values skipped, joined by `propertySeparator` (default `, `) — or, with `indentProperties: true`, as indented label/value rows beneath it (`propertyLabel` + the shared typed `cellContent`). `markerStyle` bullet | number | none draws the item marker (default bullet; number = the ordinal within its list, restarting per group; an unknown value falls back to bullet). Grouped results render 4C sections (the shared `GroupHeader` over each group's own list) with the SAME persisted collapse state as the table/board/cards (`baseGroups[<pagePath>::<viewName>]`, never the folder page's own card); search narrows items and drops empty groups. The three keys are edited in the Properties menu's List section (above). Property editing: see "Cell editing". Tests: `view/ListView.test.tsx`.

### Cell editing (5B — GRO-2141 write path, GRO-2142 editors)

`transformFile(path, transform)` (`client/src/views/writeProperty.ts`) is the shared application-level whole-file write boundary: read the current bytes, apply a pure transform, write with `expectedMtime`; on `CONFLICT` re-read and recompute once, then surface a second conflict. A no-op never touches disk. `writeProperties(path, writes)` is its ordered multi-key specialization, applying every `setFrontmatterProperty` transform before the single guarded disk write; `writeProperty(path, key, value)` delegates to it for the ordinary one-key case. This matters when one gesture changes coupled keys — YAZ-1177's cross-outer child drag — because parallel same-file writes can each succeed while the last one silently erases the other. Broken frontmatter (`FrontmatterWriteError`) propagates and nothing is written. The properties panel's TYPED ROWS (⚡ YAZ-884) call `writeProperty` AS IS, one key per row edit / add / delete, then apply the same one-key transform to the panel's own copy so its raw fallback cannot fall behind. Its RAW mode (⚡ YAZ-883, "The properties panel") keeps its purpose-built `replaceFrontmatter` flow; the folder-page reverse (YAZ-1022) uses `transformFile` because its flag, active outline value, and Markdown body are one logical change.

Editor inference (`client/src/views/editorType.ts`, locked precedence — `columnTyping` computes the per-column halves once per render, `cellEditor` adds the per-note value on top). The LADDER, top rung first: (1) the FOLDER PAGE's own `folder_page_settings.columns` declaration, view-scoped — WIRED in YAZ-819 (`columnTyping(key, records, properties, folderPage)` → `columnKindIn`, threaded `ViewsPane` → `TableView` / `CardsView` / `ListView`), see "Folder page settings" Q8; (2) a vault-wide declaration in `.yaseendocs/properties.json` (`PropertiesResponse`, via `useProperties` — see "Properties"), which also carries the link/multi-link `target` that narrows the picker — WIRED in YAZ-846, App-owned and threaded App → `Editor` → `FolderPageContents` → `ViewsPane` (see "The contents block": Typing); (3) the note's own YAML value; (4) the dominant value type across the view's records for a note without the key; (5) text, the final fallback. `file.*` and `formula.*` never get an editor (read-only). TOMBSTONE (⚡ YAZ-815, ruled by Yasin): between (2) and (3) sat an explicit `.obsidian/types.json` assignment, delivered as `IndexResponse.types` — an imported artifact ranking BELOW the vault's own schema. It was UNWIRED from the day rung 2 landed, because its data rode `IndexResponse`, which the one surface that mounts these editors deliberately never fetches (🔒 D2 — it reads the window's link snapshot instead), so `columnTyping`'s `types` parameter was passed `undefined` by every caller. A rung with no feed is not a rung: the rung, the parameter, the `IndexResponse` field and the per-call read of another app's file are all deleted. Kinds: text / number / checkbox / date / list / link / multi-link. TOMBSTONE: the type-scoped rung that used to sit above all of these — a `page_type`-keyed per-type schema — died with the type system in YAZ-836.

`view/EditableCell.tsx` is one editable property cell, shared by table cells and card/list property rows: its display (typed like a read-only cell) opens the editor directly in cards/lists, while `TableView` selects the editable `<td>` on a single click and delegates a double-click — including on an empty cell — or Enter on its focused cell to that same `[data-edit]` control. In table edit mode the real `<td>` is the cell-sized surface and sole geometry owner: the shared editor fills its normal containing block with no measured/copied geometry, while cards and lists keep their compact shared-editor layout unchanged. A table checkbox follows the same deliberate gesture and toggles exactly once. Clicks inside an open editor stay with that editor; nested activation is never replayed. Enter/blur commit through `writeProperty` — or through the host's own `onCommit` when it passes one, the seam the properties panel writes through (⚡ YAZ-884, "The properties panel") — and Esc cancels; the link editor completes `[[…]]` over the index's basenames through the shared matcher (`links/completion.ts` — one matcher with the editor's `[[` picker, rule 24; GRO-2191). The `list` editor remains free-form chips: Enter adds the pending text as one item, a comma is literal text, and the committed value is a YAML array. Commits are optimistic: the committed value renders immediately and stays until the index refetch delivers it; a failed write reverts the cell and shows an inline error. Checkboxes are live and commit on every toggle, no edit mode. An untouched chips editor (no chip added/removed, no pending text) never commits — its seed stringifies the list, so a no-op commit would rewrite `[1, 2]` as `['1', '2']` (7B, GRO-2148). The note's own open editor window absorbs the resulting frontmatter-only `change` silently (GRO-2186, see the autosave note under "Bridge API").

### Group drag (5C — GRO-2143)

`view/groupDrag.ts`, shared by the board's columns and the grouped table's sections. `dragKey(view)` is the bare frontmatter key a drop writes — null (drag disabled) unless `groupBy` is a note property. (It was also off under the views' `readOnly`, the read-only embed chrome's prop; that lost its last caller with the `.base` format in YAZ-844 and was deleted in YAZ-846 — see the View chrome tombstone.) This is the GROUP drag, unrelated to the outline's row drag (YAZ-820), which reorders presentation on the folder page's own card and writes no member's frontmatter at all. `useGroupDrag(key, onMove)` owns the transient HTML5 drag state: `source(path, gk)` spreads on each card/row, `target(group)` on each column/section (rows included — events bubble), the hovered target highlights, a drop on the source group is a no-op, and Esc cancels (real drags fire `dragend`; a document keydown fallback covers jsdom). A drop on ANOTHER group fires `onMove(path, value)` with the target's raw YAML value read off that group's FIRST row — so the written type matches what the members already carry — or `undefined` for "No value", which DELETES the key. **Fanned-out groups SWAP instead** (YAZ-671 D3): because the row sits in several groups, overwriting with a neighbour's whole list would destroy its other values, so `onMove` carries a third `GroupSwap { remove, add }` argument — `remove` is the source group's value (`drag.key`, which is why the drag state carries the group VALUE and not just its `groupKeyOf` string) and `add` is the target's; either is null at "No value". ViewsPane edits the page's list rather than replacing it: elements are matched with the engine's own `equals` over `fromYaml` and NO resolver — the exact comparison that decided the grouping — so a swap can only remove the element that put the row in that group, and every other value survives. Dropping a fanned-out row on "No value" removes just that element and never deletes the key. `dragKey` is now `groupByKey` (one name; the group "+" needs the same key whether or not a drag is in flight). ViewsPane owns the optimistic side: `PendingMove { key, value, prevRaw }` per path, patched into the records BEFORE the engine runs (`applyMoves`) so the card lands in its target group with sort/values/summaries consistent, committed through 5B's `writeProperty`. Clearing follows 5B's discipline — an entry clears only when the index refetch moves that key's raw off `prevRaw` (our write landing, or a concurrent writer winning), never before. A failed write drops the move (the card snaps back) and flags the card/row inline (`moveError`). Tests: `view/GroupDrag.test.tsx`.

### New note (5D — GRO-2144)

`newNote.ts`, behind the toolbar's **New** button and each group header's "+". `deriveSeed(def, view)` is pure over the filter ASTs (def + view): equality leaves `note.x == <literal>` seed `x` with the literal's YAML type, `file.hasTag("t")` appends to a `tags` list, and a single `file.inFolder("…")` names the root-relative target folder — only **and-reachable** leaves count (`or`/`not` subtrees are skipped entirely; seeding those would not, or would anti-, satisfy the view), and non-equality rules are ignored by design (locked kickoff decision). A group "+" additionally seeds the group's property with the raw value of that group's first row ("No value" seeds nothing). Under FAN-OUT (YAZ-671 D4) it seeds `[render(group.key)]` instead — that group's own element as a one-item list, because the first row's raw value is a neighbour's WHOLE list there; `render()` returns a link to its `[[…]]` form, the same one the picker inserts. "No value" still seeds nothing. PLACEMENT is the folder page's, always (🔒 Q5/Q6, YAZ-819): `FolderPageMode.create` births from the DECLARATION and parks per the settings' `folder`, and the seed rides along so a group "+" still seeds its group. TOMBSTONE (YAZ-846): the plain path — `targetFolder(seedFolder, root, thisFile)` (the `inFolder` seed under the root, else the page's own folder, else the root) plus `ViewsPane`'s `createFromSeed` — is deleted, function and all. `deriveSeed` still RETURNS `seed.folder` because that is what the filter says; nothing obeys it any more. The name is `untitledName` over the basenames already in the parking folder (`Untitled`, `Untitled 2`, `Untitled 3`…), which `FolderPageContents.createMember` applies — always, since YAZ-903 retired the outline create row that used to name one. `createNewNote` is ONE atomic content-at-create call — `createFile({ path, content: seedContent(properties) + body })` — no create-then-write second step and no race; an empty seed still creates the file with empty content. Success opens the note; failure shows `.view-view__error` "Could not create note: …" and opens nothing. Tests: `newNote.test.ts`, `view/NewNote.test.tsx`.

### Folder page settings (YAZ-830/831/832 — decisions Q1-Q8 of YAZ-815, LOCKED)

**The one door** (Q1): a folder page's config lives under ONE reserved frontmatter key, `folder_page_settings`, NEXT TO — never inside — the sacred `folder_page: true` flag (see "Links": Folder pages; the flag answers *is it one?*, the settings answer *how does it look?*, and upgrading the flag itself to an object was rejected: one key, one shape). `client/src/views/folderPageSettings.ts` is the only reader AND the only writer — every surface asks `folderPageSettings(record)`, every edit goes back through `writeFolderPageSettings`, no surface re-parses the key, and the key STRING itself is module-private — the rename engine, which since YAZ-864 needs to walk INTO the key rather than read it, is no exception: it comes through `mapFolderPageSettingsLinks` (the module's own answer to *where do links live in here* — `views[].order` entries, every `views[].outline` LINE that is exactly a wikilink (YAZ-900, the line rule staying `outlineDoc.ts`'s and never re-spelled here) and `columns.<name>.target`, over the RAW value so nothing is normalised away) and the key name rides back in the result (see "Links": Rename). Settings ride in `record.properties` like any other frontmatter, so the whole model costs nothing elsewhere: no indexer field, no `CACHE_VERSION` bump, no IPC. Shape: `columns` (column name → `{ kind, target?, required? }` — the shared `PROPERTY_KINDS` nine — `text` / `number` / `date` / `checkbox` / `list` / `link` / `multi-link` / `select` / `multi-select` (the last two landed in 0b2382a; it read "seven" until YAZ-1513 caught up), the same set the vault-wide declarations use, see "Properties"; an unknown kind means the column is ABSENT, so typing falls through to the lower rungs; `required` survives as report-only metadata that gates nothing, there as here) · optional `folder` (the parking bin for new pages — the shared `FOLDER_NAME` grammar read under the report-don't-block rule, so a folder unusable at rest reads as absent) · optional `formulas` (name → expression, `ViewSet.formulas` verbatim — WIRED in YAZ-745, because `formula.<name>` is a column, a sort AND a grouping LEVEL and the engine reads every one of them off `def.formulas`, so a page that could not declare a formula could not group on one either; a non-map is ignored and a non-string entry dropped, report-don't-block like the rest, and the expression itself is never parsed here — a bad one is the engine's own reported cell error) · optional `defaultView` (YAZ-1104 — the NAME of the view a fresh open starts on; any string reads verbatim, staleness being the pane's concern and not a problem, and since 🔒 D4 of YAZ-1471 it rides into the in-memory def as `ViewSet.defaultView` so one write moves it with the views, see "The contents block") · optional `properties` (YAZ-1513 — the column LABELS, `ViewSet.properties` verbatim, `key → { displayName }`; see **Labels** below) · `views` (the view objects VERBATIM — the schema `viewSchema.ts` parses and `ViewsPane` renders, key for key, with unknown view types and extra keys riding along untouched; `outline` is simply a view type, and since YAZ-844 retired `.base` this key is the ONLY place those objects live; `ViewDef.rowNumbers?: boolean` is one of those keys since YAZ-1513 — ABSENT means the Table's `#` gutter is shown, `false` hides it, see "The contents block").

**Labels** (🔒 D4, YAZ-1513): a column's KEY never changes — `properties` is `ViewSet.properties` verbatim, `key → { displayName }`, and it says what the header SAYS, never what the card stores or what a member's frontmatter is spelled with. READING is `engine.ts` `propertyLabel(def, key)`: the entry, looked up as written, bare and `note.`-prefixed (so a rename can never fork a second entry for one column), else `defaultLabel(key)` — the `file.*` fields have their own label table (`file.name` → **Name**, `file.mtime` → **Modified**, …), every other key is its last dotted segment in sentence case with underscores read as spaces (`note.kpi_category` → "Kpi category", `formula.score` → "Score"). WRITING is ONE function, `view/columnLabel.ts` `setDisplayName(def, key, name)`, spelled by exactly two gestures — the Properties detail's title field (the pencil) and the header menu's "Rename column…" — as a `Mutate` body, so a rename is one `onChange` → one `folder_page_settings` write, optimistic like every other config edit. A BLANK reads as absent and is written as absent: an emptied entry, then an emptied `properties` map, delete themselves (the YAML default-deletes rule), so a column back on its default label leaves no key behind. `readProperties` is tolerant like the rest — a non-map is ignored with a problem, a non-map entry or a non-string `displayName` drops just that entry — and only `displayName` is read; the module's whole vocabulary for a label is that one word.

**Tolerant parsing** (Q1): the read never throws and never blocks. Every bad shape becomes one human one-line `problems` entry plus a safe default, so a hand-edited page always renders — `readViews` mirrors `parseViews`'s view assertion but DROPS a bad entry where `parseViews` itself would throw, a non-map `columns` declares none, a non-map column or an unknown kind costs only that column, and a non-string `target` / non-boolean `required` drops just that key while the column stays. An absent key — or a bare `folder_page_settings:` — is a page with no settings YET: defaults, zero problems. `problems` is a read-time report for a surface to show: never thrown, never written back.

**The defaults** (Q7, amended YAZ-935): a flagged page with no settings renders `DEFAULT_VIEWS` — `outline` FIRST, then `table`, then `board` (two skins until YAZ-935 added the third) — and so does one whose `views` is unusable or empty. That is the WHOLE reach of the rule: it describes a card that lists no usable views, and never a card that lists some. YAZ-935's other half — the READ-TIME injection that spliced a Board into any `views` list that did not already carry one, so a hand-written list of two came back as three — is RETIRED by 🔒 D3 (YAZ-1471): `readViews` returns what the card says, a persisted list without a board reads back without one, and a persisted board (whatever its name) passes through untouched. It had to go the moment the tabs became editable — a read that adds a view back is a Delete the user cannot make stick. Each read hands back its own copy, because the defaults exist to be edited and written back. Consequence, now literal (YAZ-819/820): the flag alone is enough for a page to render its contents block with a working outline of everything belonging to it — see "The contents block".

**Born with a column** (YAZ-1513, ⚡ REVERSING 🔒 D1 of YAZ-841 on purpose): every folder page is nevertheless BORN with `DEFAULT_COLUMNS` — one `status` Select whose options are `1-Backlog` / `2-Todo` / `3-In-Progress` / `4-Done`, in the order the board shows them (`shared/folderPageDefaults.ts`, re-exported by `folderPageSettings.ts` for the renderer and loaded by the seed script below, so the app and the script can never disagree about the declaration) — through ONE builder, `bornFolderPage(properties)`: the flag, plus the declaration ONLY when the page has no settings key yet. All three births spell it and so does Home: "New folder page" and the Topics tree's sub-topic create go through `newFolderPageProperties()` = `bornFolderPage({})`; "Turn into folder page" goes through `turnIntoFolderPage(content)`, which is write-if-missing — a page turned back keeps its settings (reverse deletes only the flag and the active outline), so turning it forward again seeds nothing; and `ensureHome.ts` creates Home through `newFolderPageProperties()`. TOMBSTONE (🔒 D1 of YAZ-841, in three places until YAZ-1513): "the gesture only has to write the flag — `folder_page: true` and NOTHING else". Reversed because a folder page with no column is a table with nothing to do, and the flag-only rule had every vault declaring the same Select by hand. 🔒 D3 (YAZ-1471, NO read-time injection) is UNCHANGED, and it is WHY this is a BIRTH write rather than a default the reader supplies: a read that put `status` back would be a Delete column the user could not make stick. Pages born BEFORE the rule get the column from the ONE-SHOT `tools/seedDefaultColumns.mjs --vault <path>` — a dry run by default, `--apply` to write, refusing a vault that is not a clean git repo (the undo button is `git checkout .`, exactly `migrateFolderPages.mjs`'s rule, the two sharing `tools/lib/vault.mjs`), idempotent (a page that has the column is "nothing to do") — which adds `columns.status` and appends `note.status` to every non-outline view's `order`, per key through the app's own `setFrontmatterProperty` so every other byte survives. Member notes are NOT written by the script: YAZ-999's presence invariant stamps `status:` onto them when the page is next opened.

**No stored filters** (Q3): a folder page's set IS the lookup — everyone who belongs to it, computed per index snapshot ("Links": Folder pages) — so its settings hold NO filter for its contents; a folder page's rows can never disagree with what clicking its links does. Per-view extra filters WITHIN the set — deferred when this was locked — shipped in YAZ-1218 (YAZ-1226-1229): the Filter menu writes `views[i].filters` through the one settings door, and the MEMBERSHIP is still never filtered; settings-level (`def.filters`) filters remain excluded.

**The [D5] ordering rule, in ONE place** (Q3): `orderedMembers(members, settings, resolve)`. The FIRST `outline` view's `order` is a list of wikilinks, each resolved exactly as a CLICK resolves it — case-insensitively, alias-aware, `|alias` / `#heading` stripped, through the resolver handed in, the same rule `links/folderPages.ts` counts belonging by. Listed members come first, in entry sequence; every unlisted member follows alphabetically by basename through the SAME `Intl.Collator` (`sensitivity: 'base'`, `numeric: true`) the engine sorts strings with, so outline order and table sort can never drift apart. No order at all — no outline view, no `order`, a non-list `order` — is all-alphabetical, and non-string entries are ignored. A STALE entry is ignored harmlessly: unresolved, resolving to a non-member, or naming a member already placed. An order list therefore never needs cleaning up after a rename or a delete, and every member appears exactly ONCE whatever the list says. No surface re-implements this: the Topics tree orders every level of its descent through it, each level through its OWN folder page's settings ("The Topics sidebar").

**`order` RETIRES for an edited outline** (YAZ-903, amending the rule above): the outline view no longer reads `order` to arrange rows — it IS a document now (`views[i].outline`, "The contents block") — so `order` survives on a folder page's card only until that page's outline is first edited. A page with no `outline` yet is SEEDED from it (`fromOrder`, `views/outlineDoc.ts`: the entries verbatim, stale ones included, then every unlisted member alphabetically — this rule's arrangement, frozen into text), and the first edit writes the document and DELETES `order` in the same single settings write. The rule itself is unchanged and still has exactly one implementation — and `outlineOrderOf` is where BOTH sources meet (YAZ-905, closing the seam YAZ-904 found): an edited outline answers with its DOCUMENT's link lines in document order (prose lines name nobody), a never-edited card still answers from `order`, so the Topics tree and the outline can never drift apart whichever a page holds. `mapFolderPageSettingsLinks` still walks `order` entries as link leaves for the rename engine — a card nobody has edited must not lose them. What died with YAZ-820's rows is the outline's own depth-0 drag, the one gesture that ever WROTE this list.

**Column typing is VIEW-SCOPED** (Q8): `columnKindIn(settings, key)` answers one folder page's own declaration, or null. Two folder pages may declare the same key differently (`owner: text` here, `owner: link` there) and NEITHER wins globally — the key edits per the folder page whose view is rendering, which is the typing ladder's top rung, above vault-wide declarations and value inference (see "Cell editing"). There is therefore no vault-global answer for a folder-page column, no conflict resolver and no error state, and none may be built. The rung is WIRED into `editorType.ts` (YAZ-819, "Cell editing"), and so is the vault-wide rung below it (YAZ-846); the `.obsidian/types.json` rung that used to sit below THAT is gone entirely (⚡ YAZ-815 — see "The contents block": Typing). YAZ-1513 leaves the ladder as it is; what it added is the keys the app OWNS, in TWO tiers in `links/reservedKeys.ts` (lifted out of the properties panel so there is one list), spelled from the real constants and never a local literal: `RESERVED_KEYS` — `folder_page`, `folder_page_settings`, `comments` — the panel's **Reserved** rows, whose VALUE is the app's; and `APP_OWNED_KEYS` = `RESERVED_KEYS` ∪ `folder_pages` — the belonging list, whose value IS the user's to edit but which the Topics tree reads — so a column on any of these can be hidden in a view and never deleted (`deleteColumn.ts` refuses the wider tier).

**Declared columns are presence invariants** (YAZ-999): every CURRENT direct member of a folder page gets every declared column key it is missing: `[]` for `list` / `multi-link`, `null` for every scalar kind. `views/folderPageColumns.ts` is the one reconciliation service, and `scaffold.ts` shares its `emptyColumnValue` rule for newborn members. An open `FolderPageContents` reconciles whenever its settings or direct-members snapshot changes, covering in-app declarations, external declaration edits and membership echoes. A confirmed Topics move invokes the same service after its `folder_pages` write, because the destination may be closed; source-of-truth membership/settings always land first. Descendants are never included.

Presence is tested by the exact YAML key only—no aliases, IDs or kind comparison. The index record is only a fast skip for keys it already shows; a missing-looking record must be checked against the latest file bytes through `writePropertyIfMissing`, including its expected-mtime retry, so a concurrent/external value wins. Existing `null`, false, zero, empty string, list, wrong-kind or legacy values are page-owned and byte-preserved. Retyping never migrates them; removing a declaration through the type editor, moving a page away, or removing it from every folder page never deletes them; rejoining a page or sharing the same key across several folder pages reuses the retained value.

THE ONE EXCEPTION (YAZ-1513): the explicit **Delete column** gesture — "Delete column…" on the header's right-click menu or in the Properties detail, CONFIRM-FIRST through `ConfirmDeleteColumn` (our own sheet, never a native dialog; Cancel focused, Esc cancels, Enter confirms, the confirm button `--danger` because notes ARE rewritten; the copy names the label, the bare key and the count of direct members carrying it, taken ONCE when the sheet opens) — strips the bare key from every DIRECT member that carries it. `views/deleteColumn.ts` is ONE function behind both doorways, and its ORDER is the contract: (1) the declaration, every view reference — `order` through `withOrder` so `frozenColumns` follows, `sort`, `groupBy`, `summaries`, `columnSize`, `cardStyle`, a cards `image`, and every filter leaf naming the key (an emptied `and` / `or` / `not` going with its last leaf; a key spelled inside a string literal is not a reference) — and the `properties` label go in ONE settings write through `FolderPageMode.setColumns(columns, views, { properties })`, which is AWAITED: if it is refused nothing else moves, because settings are the source of truth and this very invariant must not re-add the key underneath the strips; (2) only then each member, against fresh bytes through `transformFile`, byte-preserving every other key, a note whose disk no longer carries the key simply not written; (3) strip failures aggregated into the folder-page banner the way this invariant aggregates its own — successes committed, no rollback, the next open the retry. Undeletable, only hideable: `file.*`, `formula.*` and `APP_OWNED_KEYS` (`links/reservedKeys.ts`, below), `undeletableReason` being the one rule both menus disable their item by. ACCEPTED by ruling: a member that also belongs to another folder page declaring the same key gets it re-added by THAT page's invariant on its next open — the declaration is per page, and the value follows whichever declaration is live. All eligible writes are attempted, failures are aggregated in the folder-page error banner, successful files stay committed, and the next matching snapshot/open is the retry—there is no rollback or compensating overwrite.

**The belongs-to picker** (Q2): a link/multi-link column's `target` is a wikilink to a folder page — `target: "[[KPIs]]"` = "pages that belong to KPIs". It is an opaque string in the settings module; its meaning lives in `links/folderPages.ts` `belongsToBasenames(records, resolve, target)`: the pages in that folder page, resolved like a click and answered by the lookup's `pagesIn`, with ALL basenames as the fallback when the target is unresolved, holds nobody, or is not a folder page at all. That fallback is the report-don't-block rule — the picker narrows when it can and never goes empty, never an error. TOMBSTONE (Q4, done in YAZ-836): the `page_type`-keyed `views/relation.ts` `relationBasenames` it succeeded is deleted, file and all — `belongsToBasenames` is the only picker narrowing left, and a stored `target` still naming an old TYPE simply resolves to nothing and falls back to all pages until it is re-pointed at a folder page by hand — the narrowing itself has been live in the contents block since YAZ-819 (its e2e spec covers the narrowed picker), so a `target` naming a real folder page works today. The still-valid vault-wide property declarations moved to `.yaseendocs/properties.json` (YAZ-835) — see "Properties".

**Birth from a folder page** (Q5/Q6): `scaffoldFromFolderPage(name, settings)` (`views/scaffold.ts`) is `scaffoldProperties`' analog with the DECLARATION as the schema, never a hardcoded shape — every declared column uses the same `emptyColumnValue` as YAZ-999's existing-member reconciliation (`[]` for `list` / `multi-link`, `null` otherwise) plus `folder_pages: ["[[F]]"]` LAST, spelled the way the click rule reads it back. LAST means precedence-last AND position-last: `newPageFromFolderPage` merges scaffold ← template frontmatter ← seed exactly as `newEntityParts` does, then DELETES the key and re-appends it — an assignment would leave it wherever the template or the seed first put it, so neither can redirect the birth or displace it. The new page is an ORDINARY page: the `folder_page` flag is never born here (only 4-'s explicit "make this a folder page" writes it), and the page belongs to F from its first byte — never Uncategorized, never lost. Templates follow the folder page with today's rule byte for byte, re-keyed: `<root>/.yaseendocs/templates/<folder page basename>.md`, existence = has-template (no manifest, no declared field), read over plain `readFile` — the sanctioned path for markdown at any absolute path, the dotfolder being invisible to tree/index/watcher but not to a direct read (the rule the deleted per-type templates used, rule 22's tombstone), template keys outside the declaration kept — frontmatter is source of truth and the declaration gates no content — and the body taken verbatim (no template → `''`). Parking is `memberFolder(root, folderPagePath, settings)` — the settings' `folder`, created level by level, and without one the folder page's OWN directory. It is a named helper rather than an inline expression because TWO gestures ask it (⚡ YAZ-869): this block's New / outline create row, and the Topics tree's right-click on the folder page itself.

**The write path** (Q1): `writeFolderPageSettings(path, next)` writes the whole block back as the ONE key through the shared one-key writer (`writeProperty`, see "Cell editing" — its read → rewrite → `expectedMtime` → retry-once dance is REUSED, never duplicated), so a settings edit preserves every other key on the page, and the note's own open editor absorbs the resulting frontmatter-only `change` silently (GRO-2186, the autosave note under "Bridge API"). EXACTLY what the caller passes is written: an all-default value is still a value, and `undefined` DELETES the key — always the caller's explicit choice, never this module's inference. `columns` is omitted while empty and `folder` while absent; `problems` never reaches disk. The UI reaches this writer through exactly two doors, both on the contents block's host: `onChange` (every `Mutate`-driven views edit — the tab gestures among them since 🔒 D0 of YAZ-1471, and `defaultView` among the keys it carries since 🔒 D4) and `FolderPageMode.setColumns(columns, views?, { properties }?)` (YAZ-895, WIDENED by YAZ-1513 — the declarations, with `views` riding along when one gesture moves both and the LIVE def's `views` / `defaultView` when it does not, and the labels the caller's when it speaks (`undefined` inside meaning NONE) else the live def's; it RESOLVES when the write landed and REJECTS when it did not, which is what `deleteColumn` awaits), each ONE write, failures in the same banner. `FolderPageMode.deleteColumn(key)` (YAZ-1513) is not a third door: its settings half IS `setColumns`, and only the member strips are its own ("Declared columns are presence invariants"). It was THREE doors until YAZ-1471 deleted `FolderPageMode.setDefaultView`.

Files: `client/src/views/folderPageSettings.ts`, `client/src/views/folderPageSettings.test.ts`, `shared/folderPageDefaults.ts` (`DEFAULT_COLUMNS`), `client/src/views/deleteColumn.ts` (+ test), `client/src/views/view/columnOrder.ts` (`withOrder`), `client/src/views/view/columnLabel.ts` (`setDisplayName`), `client/src/views/view/TableHeaderMenu.tsx`, `client/src/views/view/ConfirmDeleteColumn.tsx` (+ their tests), `client/src/links/reservedKeys.ts`, `tools/seedDefaultColumns.mjs` (+ test, sharing `tools/lib/vault.mjs` with the migration), `desktop/e2e/columns.spec.ts` (the end-to-end arc over `fixtures/columns-vault`), and `client/src/views/scaffold.ts` — `scaffoldFromFolderPage` / `folderPageTemplatePath` / `newPageFromFolderPage` / `EntityParts` / `memberFolder` / `ensureFolder`, which is the WHOLE file since YAZ-836 took the type half — with `client/src/views/scaffold.test.ts`.


## Document magnification (YAZ-1410)

Each Markdown `CrepeHost` owns a temporary percentage, default **100%**. The entire percentage
and arrow button immediately left of Sync opens the dropdown (YAZ-1430). A labeled Custom
input above the presets accepts whole numbers **50–200**, with an optional `%` suffix, and
presets remain **50, 75, 90, 100, 125, 150, 200**. Enter or leaving the control applies a valid draft;
Escape cancels it. Invalid input preserves the applied value and keeps the panel open with a
short range message, including after an outside dismissal attempt; focus is not pulled back.
Choosing a preset replaces the draft and closes the list. Typing a partial number never resizes
the document. Explicit completion or Escape returns focus to the trigger; outside dismissal
does not steal focus from the destination.

Note and outline editors use the native text caret through Crepe's `Cursor.virtual: false`
setting. The virtual overlay and its caret-hiding class are absent: its viewport measurements
would scale twice inside CSS zoom. Click/selection coordinates are not remapped. The Cursor
feature stays enabled for gap/drop support. The virtual directional affinity tail and its
extra formatting-boundary arrow step are intentionally removed; native/editor mark behavior
applies. Tests cover the shared factory, preserved selection/typing/undo and boundary keys.

CSS `zoom` applies to `.editor-host`: title, properties, body, folder contents, comments and backlinks.
The control/status row, tabs, sidebar and dialogs outside the scroller keep their usual size.
Retained editor mounts preserve zoom across tab switches; closing/reopening or another remount
(including path changes) resets it. Separate mounted editors, including right panes, are
independent. Zoom does not change Markdown, autosave, IPC, settings, or persisted app state.
Non-Markdown viewers, Electron's existing application zoom and outline bullet-focus zoom keep
their separate behavior. No Fit mode, added zoom shortcuts or persistent preference.

DOM geometry used for pointer hit areas, drag distances and pinned folder-table headers must
convert viewport measurements to local layout units when inside this scaled scroller.

Milkdown table drag previews and insertion indicators use the version-pinned
`@milkdown/components@7.22.1` archive in `client/vendor`, enforced for all transitive consumers
by the root npm override. The narrow patch converts viewport sizes/offsets to local CSS pixels;
it preserves row/column drop targeting and the existing 100% behavior. Rebuild/provenance
instructions live in `client/vendor/milkdown-components-7.22.1-yaz1410.md`. The shipped runtime
has geometry regression coverage at 50%, 100%, 125% and 200%; upgrades must preserve those tests.

### Configured empty Board columns (YAZ-1417)

`view.showEmptyColumns` defaults off and is saved per Board view. `boardOptionGroups` inserts configured Select/Multi-select options after engine evaluation, retaining observed unlisted/typed values. `ASC` means property option order and `DESC` reverses it; the Sort menu labels this override explicitly. Within-column card sorting stays independent. Search retains configured destinations when the toggle is on.

Empty destinations carry an exact `optionValue`; drag and New card can therefore write/seed a value without borrowing the first card. Multi-select targets remain fanned out and cross-outer nested moves retain the existing atomic two-property write. Nested groups obey the engine merge rule: an inner value equal to its outer belongs in `outer.direct`, not a duplicate empty child.

Verification lives in `boardOptionGroups.test.ts`, `PropertyDefinitionEditor.test.tsx`, `SelectValueEditor.test.tsx`, `writeFolderColumn.test.ts`, `FrontmatterPanel.test.tsx`, `Toolbar.test.tsx`, and the existing Board/drag/typing/filter/scaffold suites. `tools/prototypes/yaz-1417.mjs` creates a synthetic local review vault and isolated app profile; it never seeds a real vault. No new colors, bulk record migrations, duplication/layout customization or AI property features are included.
